Śrīmad Bhāgavatam — Second Canto

with the Bhāvārthadīpikā of Śrīdhara Svāmī

Each entry presents the original Bhāgavata verse in Devanāgarī and IAST, followed by compact controls for a literal word-by-word gloss, an English translation, and Śrīdhara Svāmī’s Sanskrit commentary. The English beneath Śrīdhara is a close translation of his Sanskrit commentary—not a summary or paraphrase; glosses, objections, alternative readings and quotations are retained rather than silently compressed. Genuine textual variants are not silently flattened.

Contents

  2. 2.2 — Liberation through Yoga

  10. 2.10 — Origin of the Senses and Other Principles

Textual basis

Śrīdhara’s text is checked primarily against the Canto 2 electronic recension derived from the Pandey/Caukhamba edition, the multi-commentary recension, the Vishvasa verse-by-verse presentation, and older Bombay/printed witnesses where readings require confirmation. Obvious OCR corruption is corrected; meaningful pāṭhāntaras are preserved or noted.

2.1 The First Step in God Realization

ŚB 2.1.1

श्रीशुक उवाच
वरीयानेष ते प्रश्नः कृतो लोकहितं नृप ।
आत्मवित्सम्मतः पुंसां श्रोतव्यादिषु यः परः ॥ १ ॥
śrī-śuka uvāca
varīyān eṣa te praśnaḥ
kṛto loka-hitaṁ nṛpa
ātmavit-sammataḥ puṁsāṁ
śrotavyādiṣu yaḥ paraḥ
Śrī Śuka said: O King, your question is most excellent and beneficial to the world. It is approved by knowers of the Self and is supreme among all subjects worthy of hearing.
Word-for-word

śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śuka said; varīyān — most excellent; eṣaḥ — this; te — your; praśnaḥ — question; kṛtaḥ — asked; loka-hitam — beneficial to the world; nṛpa — O King; ātma-vit-sammataḥ — approved by knowers of the Self; puṁsām — for people; śrotavya-ādiṣu — among matters to be heard and related subjects; yaḥ — which; paraḥ — supreme.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

राज्ञः प्रश्नमभिनन्दति—वरीयानिति । ते त्वया पुंसां श्रोतव्यादिषु मध्ये यः परः श्रेष्ठः प्रश्नः कृतः, एष वरीयान् । यतो लोकहितमेतत् मोक्षहेतुत्वात् । आत्मविदां ब्रह्मविदां मुक्तानां सम्मतो यतः ॥ १ ॥

rājñaḥ praśnam abhinandati—varīyān iti | te tvayā puṁsāṁ śrotavyādiṣu madhye yaḥ paraḥ śreṣṭhaḥ praśnaḥ kṛtaḥ, eṣa varīyān | yato loka-hitam etat mokṣa-hetutvāt | ātmavidāṁ brahmavidāṁ muktānāṁ sammato yataḥ ||1||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. rājñaḥ — of the king; praśnam — question; abhinandati — he praises; varīyān iti — by the word “most excellent”; te tvayā — by you; puṁsāṁ śrotavyādiṣu madhye — among matters to be heard by people and the like; yaḥ paraḥ śreṣṭhaḥ praśnaḥ — the question which is highest and best; kṛtaḥ — asked; eṣa varīyān — this is most excellent; loka-hitam — beneficial to the world; mokṣa-hetutvāt — because it is a cause of liberation; ātma-vidām brahma-vidām muktānām — of knowers of the Self, knowers of Brahman, and liberated persons; sammataḥ — approved.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śuka praises the king’s question. It is “most excellent” because, among all matters people may hear about, it asks about what is highest. It benefits the world because it leads toward liberation, and it is approved by knowers of the Self, knowers of Brahman, and the liberated.

ŚB 2.1.2

श्रोतव्यादीनि राजेन्द्र नृणां सन्ति सहस्रशः ।
अपश्यतामात्मतत्त्वं गृहेषु गृहमेधिनाम् ॥ २ ॥
śrotavyādīni rājendra
nṛṇāṁ santi sahasraśaḥ
apaśyatām ātma-tattvaṁ
gṛheṣu gṛha-medhinām
O best of kings, householders who do not perceive the truth of the Self have thousands upon thousands of matters to hear about.
Word-for-word

śrotavya-ādīni — matters to hear and so forth; rājendra — O best of kings; nṛṇām — of people; santi — there are; sahasraśaḥ — by the thousands; apaśyatām — of those who do not see; ātma-tattvam — the truth of the Self; gṛheṣu — in household affairs; gṛha-medhinām — of householders absorbed in home life.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र तावत् स्वाभाविकक्रियाणामनर्थहेतुत्वं वदन् “ब्रूहि यद्वा विपर्ययम्” इत्यस्योत्तरमाह—श्रोतव्यादीनीति त्रिभिः । गृहेषु सक्तानामत एव गृहमेधिनां तद्गतपञ्चसूनापराणाम् । मेधतिर्हिंसार्थः ॥ २ ॥

tatra tāvat svābhāvika-kriyāṇām anartha-hetutvaṁ vadan “brūhi yad vā viparyayam” ity asyottaram āha—śrotavyādīnīti tribhiḥ | gṛheṣu saktānām ata eva gṛha-medhināṁ tad-gata-pañca-sūnā-parāṇām | medhatir hiṁsārthaḥ ||2||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. tatra tāvat — first, in this connection; svābhāvika-kriyāṇām — of ordinary natural activities; anartha-hetutvam — their being a cause of harm or futility; vadan — stating; brūhi yad vā viparyayam — “tell what is contrary”; ity asya uttaram āha — he gives the answer to that; śrotavyādīnīti tribhiḥ — with “things to be heard” and the following three verses; gṛheṣu saktānām — of those attached to household life; gṛha-medhinām — householders absorbed in the home; tad-gata-pañca-sūnā-parāṇām — occupied with the five unavoidable household violences; medhatiḥ hiṁsārthaḥ — the verbal root medh has the sense of violence.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śuka first answers the question about the contrary or mistaken course of life by showing the futility of ordinary activities. Those whose awareness is fixed on household concerns have innumerable things to hear about and pursue, while failing to see the truth of the Self.

ŚB 2.1.3

निद्रया ह्रियते नक्तं व्यवायेन च वा वयः ।
दिवा चार्थेहया राजन् कुटुम्बभरणेन वा ॥ ३ ॥
nidrayā hriyate naktaṁ
vyavāyena ca vā vayaḥ
divā cārthehayā rājan
kuṭumba-bharaṇena vā
At night their lifespan is taken away by sleep and sexual enjoyment; by day it passes in striving for wealth and in maintaining the family.
Word-for-word

nidrayā — by sleep; hriyate — is taken away; naktam — at night; vyavāyena — by sexual activity; ca — and; vā — or; vayaḥ — lifespan; divā — by day; ca — and; artha-īhayā — by striving for wealth; rājan — O King; kuṭumba-bharaṇena — by maintaining the family; vā — or.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तैश्च वृथैवायुर्व्ययो भवतीत्याह । नक्तं रात्रौ यद्वय आयुस्तन्निद्रया व्यवायेन रत्या वा ह्रियते । दिवा अह्नि यद्वयस्तदर्थार्थमुद्यमेन, सिद्धेऽर्थे कुटुम्बपोषणेन वेति । चकारावनुक्तसमुच्चयार्थौ ॥ ३ ॥

taiś ca vṛthaivāyur-vyayo bhavatīty āha | naktaṁ rātrau yad vaya āyus tan nidrayā vyavāyena ratyā vā hriyate | divā ahni yad vayas tad arthārtham udyamena, siddhe ’rthe kuṭumba-poṣaṇena veti | ca-kārāv anukta-samuccayārthau ||3||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. taiś ca — and by those activities; vṛthā eva — entirely in vain; āyuḥ-vyayaḥ — expenditure of life; bhavati — occurs; naktam rātrau — at night; yad vayaḥ āyuḥ — whatever span of life; tat nidrayā — that by sleep; vyavāyena ratyā vā — or by intercourse, sexual pleasure; hriyate — is taken away; divā ahni — by day; arthārtham udyamena — by exertion for wealth; siddhe arthe — once wealth is obtained; kuṭumba-poṣaṇena — by supporting the family; ca-kārau — the two occurrences of “and”; anukta-samuccaya-arthau — include further unmentioned activities.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. These pursuits merely spend away a person’s life. At night life is consumed by sleep or sexual pleasure; in the daytime by exertion to obtain wealth and, once wealth is acquired, by maintaining the family. The two occurrences of “and” also gather in other similar occupations left unstated.

ŚB 2.1.4

देहापत्यकलत्रादिष्वात्मसैन्येष्वसत्स्वपि ।
तेषां प्रमत्तो निधनं पश्यन्नपि न पश्यति ॥ ४ ॥
dehāpatya-kalatrādiṣv
ātma-sainyeṣv asatsv api
teṣāṁ pramatto nidhanaṁ
paśyann api na paśyati
Though body, children, spouse and the rest are impermanent, a heedless person treats them as his own defending host. Even while seeing their destruction, he does not truly see.
Word-for-word

deha-apatya-kalatra-ādiṣu — in body, children, spouse and so on; ātma-sainyeṣu — regarded as one’s own defending forces; asatsu — impermanent; api — although; teṣām — of them; pramattaḥ — heedless and attached; nidhanam — destruction or death; paśyan — seeing; api — even; na paśyati — does not truly see.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ननु नश्वरकुटुम्बाद्यर्थं कथमायुर्व्ययं कुर्यात् ? तत्राह । देहादिष्वात्मनः सैन्येषु परिकरेष्वसत्सु मिथ्याभूतेष्वपि प्रमत्तः प्रसक्तस्तेषां पित्रादिदृष्टान्तेन नाशं पश्यन्नपि नानुसन्धत्ते ॥ ४ ॥

nanu naśvara-kuṭumbādy-arthaṁ katham āyur-vyayaṁ kuryāt? tatrāha | dehādiṣv ātmanaḥ sainyeṣu parikareṣv asatsu mithyā-bhūteṣv api pramattaḥ prasaktas teṣāṁ pitrādi-dṛṣṭāntena nāśaṁ paśyann api nānusandhatte ||4||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. nanu — an objection: surely; naśvara-kuṭumbādi-artham — for the sake of a perishable family and the like; katham āyuḥ-vyayam kuryāt — why would one spend one’s life?; tatra āha — he answers; dehādiṣu — in the body and so forth; ātmanaḥ sainyeṣu — as one’s own army; parikareṣu — retinue or supports; asatsu — impermanent; mithyā-bhūteṣu api — even though falsely conceived as lasting supports; pramattaḥ — heedless; prasaktaḥ — attached; teṣām — their; pitrādi-dṛṣṭāntena — through the example of one’s father and others; nāśam paśyan api — even while seeing destruction; na anusandhatte — does not reflect upon or recognize it.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Why would anyone spend life for a family known to be perishable? Because body, relatives and the rest are imagined as one’s protective retinue. Though they are impermanent, attachment makes a person heedless: even after seeing the death of parents and others, he fails to apply that lesson to himself and his own circle.

ŚB 2.1.5

तस्माद् भारत सर्वात्मा भगवानीश्वरो हरिः ।
श्रोतव्यः कीर्तितव्यश्च स्मर्तव्यश्चेच्छताभयम् ॥ ५ ॥
tasmād bhārata sarvātmā
bhagavān īśvaro hariḥ
śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca
smartavyaś cecchatābhayam
Therefore, descendant of Bharata, one who desires fearlessness should hear about, glorify and remember Bhagavān, the Lord Hari, the Self of all.
Word-for-word

tasmāt — therefore; bhārata — O descendant of Bharata; sarva-ātmā — the Self of all; bhagavān — Bhagavān; īśvaraḥ — the Lord; hariḥ — Hari; śrotavyaḥ — should be heard about; kīrtitavyaḥ — should be glorified; ca — and; smartavyaḥ — should be remembered; ca — and; icchatā — by one desiring; abhayam — fearlessness.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

एवं विपर्ययप्रश्नोत्तरमुक्त्वा, श्रोतव्यादिप्रश्नस्योत्तरमाह—तस्मादिति । हे भारत भरतवंश्य । सर्वात्मेति प्रेष्ठत्वमाह । भगवानिति सौन्दर्यम् । ईश्वर इत्यावश्यकत्वम् । हरिरिति बन्धहारित्वम् । अभयं मोक्षमिच्छता ॥ ५ ॥

evaṁ viparyaya-praśnottaram uktvā, śrotavyādi-praśnasyottaram āha—tasmād iti | he bhārata bharata-vaṁśya | sarvātmeti preṣṭhatvam āha | bhagavān iti saundaryam | īśvara ity āvaśyakatvam | harir iti bandha-hāritvam | abhayaṁ mokṣam icchatā ||5||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. evaṁ viparyaya-praśna-uttaram uktvā — having thus answered the question about the contrary course; śrotavyādi-praśnasya uttaram āha — he now answers the question of what should be heard and so forth; tasmāt iti — with “therefore”; he bhārata — O Bhārata; bharata-vaṁśya — descendant of Bharata; sarvātmā iti preṣṭhatvam āha — “Self of all” expresses His dearness; bhagavān iti saundaryam — “Bhagavān” expresses beauty; īśvara iti āvaśyakatvam — “Lord” expresses His indispensability or sovereignty; hariḥ iti bandha-hāritvam — “Hari” expresses His removal of bondage; abhayam mokṣam icchatā — by one desiring fearlessness, liberation.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having described the mistaken course, Śuka now answers what should truly be heard, praised and remembered. Śrīdhara draws significance from each name: “Self of all” indicates supreme dearness, “Bhagavān” beauty, “Īśvara” lordship and indispensability, and “Hari” the removal of bondage. “Fearlessness” is liberation.

ŚB 2.1.6

एतावान् सांख्ययोगाभ्यां स्वधर्मपरिनिष्ठया ।
जन्मलाभः परः पुंसामन्ते नारायणस्मृतिः ॥ ६ ॥
etāvān sāṅkhya-yogābhyāṁ
sva-dharma-pariniṣṭhayā
janma-lābhaḥ paraḥ puṁsām
ante nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ
Whether through Sāṅkhya, yoga, or steadfast fulfillment of one’s own dharma, the supreme gain of human birth is remembrance of Nārāyaṇa at the end.
Word-for-word

etāvān — this is the extent; sāṅkhya-yogābhyām — by Sāṅkhya and yoga; sva-dharma-pariniṣṭhayā — by steadfastness in one’s own dharma; janma-lābhaḥ — the gain of birth; paraḥ — supreme; puṁsām — for people; ante — at the end; nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ — remembrance of Nārāyaṇa.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अतः परमन्यच्छ्रेयो नास्तीत्याह—एतावानेव जन्मनो लाभः फलम् । तमाह—नारायणस्मृतिरिति । साङ्ख्यादिभिः साध्य इति तेषां स्वातन्त्र्येण लाभत्वं वारयति । साङ्ख्यमात्मानात्मविवेकः । योगोऽष्टाङ्गः । अन्ते तु स्मृतिः परो लाभः, न तन्महिमा वक्तुं शक्य इत्यर्थः ॥ ६ ॥

ataḥ param anyac chreyo nāstīty āha—etāvān eva janmano lābhaḥ phalam | tam āha—nārāyaṇa-smṛtir iti | sāṅkhyādibhiḥ sādhya iti teṣāṁ svātantryeṇa lābhatvaṁ vārayati | sāṅkhyam ātmānātma-vivekaḥ | yogo ’ṣṭāṅgaḥ | ante tu smṛtiḥ paro lābhaḥ, na tan-mahimā vaktuṁ śakya ity arthaḥ ||6||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. ataḥ param anyat śreyaḥ na asti — beyond this there is no other higher good; etāvān eva janmanaḥ lābhaḥ phalam — this alone is the gain and fruit of birth; nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ — remembrance of Nārāyaṇa; sāṅkhyādibhiḥ sādhyaḥ — attained through Sāṅkhya and the other disciplines; teṣāṁ svātantryeṇa lābhatvam vārayati — he denies that those disciplines independently constitute the ultimate gain; sāṅkhyam — Sāṅkhya; ātma-anātma-vivekaḥ — discrimination between Self and non-Self; yogaḥ aṣṭāṅgaḥ — yoga is eightfold; ante tu smṛtiḥ paraḥ lābhaḥ — remembrance at the end is the supreme gain; na tan-mahimā vaktum śakyaḥ — its greatness cannot be fully expressed.

Witness note. The TTD printed recension adds a short explanatory phrase identifying svadharma as performance of prescribed duties; the Gauḍīya Grantha Mandira transcription omits that expansion. The shared reading above is retained.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. There is no higher gain of birth than remembrance of Nārāyaṇa at life’s end. Sāṅkhya and yoga are means toward it, not independent ultimate ends: Sāṅkhya discriminates Self from non-Self, and yoga is the eightfold discipline. The greatness of final remembrance cannot be adequately expressed.

ŚB 2.1.7

प्रायेण मुनयो राजन् निवृत्ता विधिषेधतः ।
नैर्गुण्यस्था रमन्ते स्म गुणानुकथने हरेः ॥ ७ ॥
prāyeṇa munayo rājan
nivṛttā vidhi-ṣedhataḥ
nairguṇya-sthā ramante sma
guṇānukathane hareḥ
O King, sages who have generally withdrawn from prescribed injunctions and prohibitions, and who abide beyond the guṇas, nevertheless delight in recounting Hari’s qualities.
Word-for-word

prāyeṇa — generally; munayaḥ — sages; rājan — O King; nivṛttāḥ — withdrawn; vidhi-niṣedhataḥ — from injunction and prohibition; nairguṇya-sthāḥ — established beyond the guṇas; ramante sma — delight; guṇa-anukathane — in recounting the qualities; hareḥ — of Hari.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र सदाचारं प्रमाणयति—प्रायेणेति । विधिषेधतो विधिनिषेधाभ्यां निवृत्ता नैर्गुण्ये ब्रह्मणि स्थिता अपि । स्म प्रसिद्धम् ॥ ७ ॥

tatra sad-ācāraṁ pramāṇayati—prāyeṇeti | vidhi-ṣedhato vidhi-niṣedhābhyāṁ nivṛttā nairguṇye brahmaṇi sthitā api | sma prasiddham ||7||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. tatra — here; sad-ācāram — the conduct of the wise; pramāṇayati — he adduces as evidence; prāyeṇa iti — by the word “generally”; vidhi-niṣedhābhyām nivṛttāḥ — withdrawn from injunctions and prohibitions; nairguṇye brahmaṇi — in Brahman beyond the guṇas; sthitāḥ api — although established; sma — marks this as a well-known fact.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śuka appeals to the established conduct of the wise. Even sages who have gone beyond Vedic injunction and prohibition and stand established in the nirguṇa Brahman are well known to delight in recounting Hari’s qualities.

ŚB 2.1.8

इदं भागवतं नाम पुराणं ब्रह्मसम्मितम् ।
अधीतवान् द्वापरादौ पितुर्द्वैपायनादहम् ॥ ८ ॥
idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma
purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam
adhītavān dvāparādau
pitur dvaipāyanād aham
At the beginning of Dvāpara I studied from my father Dvaipāyana this Purāṇa called the Bhāgavatam, which is consonant with the Veda.
Word-for-word

idam — this; bhāgavatam — Bhāgavatam; nāma — named; purāṇam — Purāṇa; brahma-sammitam — equal or consonant with the Veda; adhītavān — I studied; dvāpara-ādau — at the beginning of Dvāpara; pituḥ — from my father; dvaipāyanāt — from Dvaipāyana; aham — I.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

किमिदमपूर्वं कथयसि ? सत्यमपूर्वमेवेदमित्याह । इदं भगवत्प्रोक्तं तन्नामैकप्रधानं पुराणं ब्रह्मसम्मितं सर्ववेदतुल्यम् । यद्वा, ब्रह्म सम्यक् मितं येन । कुतस्त्वया प्राप्तमत आह—अधीतवानिति । द्वैपायनात्पितुः । कदा ? द्वापरादौ । द्वापर आदिर्यस्य कालस्य तस्मिन् द्वापरान्त इत्यर्थः । शन्तनुसमकाले व्यासावतारप्रसिद्धेः ॥ ८ ॥

kim idam apūrvaṁ kathayasi? satyam apūrvam evedam ity āha | idaṁ bhagavat-proktaṁ tan-nāmaika-pradhānaṁ purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitaṁ sarva-veda-tulyam | yad vā, brahma samyak mitaṁ yena | kutas tvayā prāptam ata āha—adhītavān iti | dvaipāyanāt pituḥ | kadā? dvāparādau | dvāpara ādir yasya kālasya tasmin dvāparānta ity arthaḥ | śantanu-sama-kāle vyāsāvatāra-prasiddheḥ ||8||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. kim idam apūrvam kathayasi — what unprecedented thing are you telling?; satyam apūrvam eva idam — indeed, this is truly unprecedented; idam bhagavat-proktam — this was spoken by Bhagavān; tan-nāma-eka-pradhānam — it has His name as its chief subject; purāṇam — Purāṇa; brahma-sammitam — equal to Brahman/the Veda; sarva-veda-tulyam — equal to all the Vedas; yad vā — alternatively; brahma samyak mitam yena — that by which Brahman is rightly measured or understood; kutas tvayā prāptam — how did you obtain it?; adhītavān iti — he answers with “I studied”; dvaipāyanāt pituḥ — from his father Dvaipāyana; kadā — when?; dvāparādau — at the beginning of Dvāpara; tasmin dvāparānte — explained as at Dvāpara’s end; śantanu-sama-kāle — in the time of Śantanu; vyāsa-avatāra-prasiddheḥ — because Vyāsa’s appearance then is well known.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The Bhāgavatam is something extraordinary: a Purāṇa spoken by Bhagavān and centered upon Him, “equal to Brahman” in the sense of being equal to the Veda, or alternatively a work by which Brahman is rightly understood. Śuka received it from his father Dvaipāyana. Śrīdhara explains “at the beginning of Dvāpara” as referring to the end of Dvāpara, in light of the traditional chronology of Vyāsa and Śantanu.

ŚB 2.1.9

परिनिष्ठितोऽपि नैर्गुण्य उत्तमश्लोकलीलया ।
गृहीतचेता राजर्षे आख्यानं यदधीतवान् ॥ ९ ॥
pariniṣṭhito ’pi nairguṇya
uttama-śloka-līlayā
gṛhīta-cetā rājarṣe
ākhyānaṁ yad adhītavān
Though firmly established beyond the guṇas, O royal sage, my mind was captivated by Uttamaśloka’s pastimes, and thus I studied this narration.
Word-for-word

pariniṣṭhitaḥ — firmly established; api — although; nairguṇye — in transcendence beyond the guṇas; uttama-śloka-līlayā — by Uttamaśloka’s pastimes; gṛhīta-cetāḥ — with mind captivated; rājarṣe — O royal sage; ākhyānam — narration; yat — which; adhītavān — I studied.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

सिद्धस्य तव कुतोऽध्ययने प्रवृत्तिः ? तत्राह—परिनिष्ठितोऽपीति । गृहीतचेता आकृष्टचित्तः ॥ ९ ॥

siddhasya tava kuto ’dhyayane pravṛttiḥ? tatrāha—pariniṣṭhito ’pīti | gṛhīta-cetā ākṛṣṭa-cittaḥ ||9||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. siddhasya tava — for you who are already perfected; kutaḥ adhyayane pravṛttiḥ — why was there engagement in study?; tatra āha — he answers; pariniṣṭhitaḥ api iti — with “although firmly established”; gṛhīta-cetāḥ — one whose mind has been seized; ākṛṣṭa-cittaḥ — whose consciousness has been attracted.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The implied question is: if Śuka was already perfected, why did he engage in study? The verse answers that although he was firmly established beyond the guṇas, his mind was seized—drawn irresistibly—by the pastimes of Uttamaśloka.

ŚB 2.1.10

तदहं तेऽभिधास्यामि महापौरुषिको भवान् ।
यस्य श्रद्दधतामाशु स्यान्मुकुन्दे मतिः सती ॥ १० ॥
tad ahaṁ te ’bhidhāsyāmi
mahā-pauruṣiko bhavān
yasya śraddadhatām āśu
syān mukunde matiḥ satī
I shall tell it to you, for you are deeply devoted to the Supreme Person. In those who hear it with faith, pure understanding directed toward Mukunda quickly arises.
Word-for-word

tat — that; aham — I; te — to you; abhidhāsyāmi — shall tell; mahā-pauruṣikaḥ — deeply devoted to the Supreme Person; bhavān — you; yasya — of which; śraddadhatām — of those hearing with faith; āśu — quickly; syāt — may arise; mukunde — toward Mukunda; matiḥ — understanding or devotional attention; satī — pure and genuine.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तदिति । महापुरुषो विष्णुः तदीयः महापौरुषिकः । यस्य यस्मिन् श्रद्दधतां श्रद्धां कुर्वतां सती अहैतुकी ॥ १० ॥

tad iti | mahāpuruṣo viṣṇuḥ tadīyaḥ mahā-pauruṣikaḥ | yasya yasmin śraddadhatāṁ śraddhāṁ kurvatāṁ satī ahaitukī ||10||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. tad iti — on the word “that”; mahāpuruṣaḥ viṣṇuḥ — the Great Person is Viṣṇu; tadīyaḥ — belonging or devoted to Him; mahā-pauruṣikaḥ — one devoted to the Great Person; yasya yasmin — “of which,” that is, in which Bhāgavatam; śraddadhatām — of those possessing faith; śraddhām kurvatām — of those placing faith in it; satī — genuine; ahaitukī — unmotivated, causeless.

Witness note. This short gloss is explicit in the TTD printed Bhāvārtha-dīpikā. The Gauḍīya Grantha Mandira presentation groups verses 9–10 but prints only the gloss attached to verse 9; the TTD reading supplies the separate verse-10 gloss.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Mahā-pauruṣika” means one who belongs to or is devoted to the Great Person, Viṣṇu. In the Bhāgavatam, those who place faith develop a genuine, unmotivated orientation toward Mukunda.

ŚB 2.1.11

एतन्निर्विद्यमानानामिच्छतामकुतोभयम् ।
योगिनां नृप निर्णीतं हरेर्नामानुकीर्तनम् ॥ ११ ॥
etan nirvidyamānānām
icchatām akuto-bhayam
yogināṁ nṛpa nirṇītaṁ
harer nāmānukīrtanam
O King, repeated glorification of Hari’s name has been decisively established as the fearless course for the detached, the desirous, and the yogīs alike.
Word-for-word

etat — this; nirvidyamānānām — of those detached or disenchanted with material desire; icchatām — of those who desire; akutaḥ-bhayam — fearless, from which there is no fear; yoginām — of yogīs; nṛpa — O King; nirṇītam — decisively established; hareḥ — of Hari; nāma — the name; anu-kīrtanam — repeated or continuous glorification.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

साधकानां सिद्धानां च नातः परमन्यच्छ्रेयोऽस्तीत्याह—एतदिति । इच्छतां कामिनां तत्तत्फलसाधनमेतदेव । निर्विद्यमानानां मुमुक्षूणां मोक्षसाधनमेतदेव । योगिनां ज्ञानिनां फलं चैतदेव निर्णीतम् । नात्र प्रमाणं वक्तव्यमित्यर्थः ॥ ११ ॥

sādhakānāṁ siddhānāṁ ca nātaḥ param anyac chreyo ’stīty āha—etad iti | icchatāṁ kāmināṁ tat-tat-phala-sādhanam etad eva | nirvidyamānānāṁ mumukṣūṇāṁ mokṣa-sādhanam etad eva | yogināṁ jñānināṁ phalaṁ caitad eva nirṇītam | nātra pramāṇaṁ vaktavyam ity arthaḥ ||11||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. sādhakānām — of practitioners; siddhānām — of perfected persons; na ataḥ param anyat śreyaḥ asti — there is no higher good than this; icchatām kāminām — for desirous persons; tat-tat-phala-sādhanam — the means to each respective desired fruit; nirvidyamānānām mumukṣūṇām — for the disenchanted, seekers of liberation; mokṣa-sādhanam — a means to liberation; yoginām jñāninām — for yogīs and jñānins; phalam ca — and the fruit; nirṇītam — decisively established; na atra pramāṇam vaktavyam — no further proof need be stated here.

Witness note. The TTD printed recension adds an explicit gloss on akuto-bhayam: kutaścid api bhaya-rahitam, “free from fear from any quarter.” The shared reading is given above.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. For practitioners and for the perfected, there is no higher good than this. For those who seek worldly ends, glorification of Hari is the means to their respective fruits; for seekers of liberation, it is the means to liberation; and for yogīs and jñānins, it is itself the fruit. Śrīdhara says this conclusion is already established and needs no further proof.

ŚB 2.1.12

किं प्रमत्तस्य बहुभि: परोक्षैर्हायनैरिह ।
वरं मुहूर्तं विदितं घटते श्रेयसे यत: ॥ १२ ॥
kiṁ pramattasya bahubhiḥ
parokṣair hāyanair iha
varaṁ muhūrtaṁ viditaṁ
ghaṭate śreyase yataḥ
What use are many years that pass unnoticed for a heedless person here? Better one consciously recognized moment, because in it one can strive for the highest good.
Word-for-word

kim — what use; pramattasya — for the heedless; bahubhiḥ — by many; parokṣaiḥ — unseen or unnoticed; hāyanaiḥ — years; iha — here; varam — better; muhūrtam — one moment; viditam — consciously known; ghaṭate — one strives or applies oneself; śreyase — for the highest good; yataḥ — because, by which.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अल्पमेवायुरवशिष्टं किमहं साधयेयम् ? इति मा शुच इत्याह—किमिति त्रिभिः । परोक्षैरलक्षितैर्हायनैर्वर्षैः । विदितं वृथा यातीति ज्ञातम् । यतो येन ज्ञानेन । घटेत यत्नं कुर्यात् ॥ १२ ॥

alpam evāyur avaśiṣṭaṁ kim ahaṁ sādhayeyam? iti mā śuca ity āha—kim iti tribhiḥ | parokṣair alakṣitair hāyanair varṣaiḥ | viditaṁ vṛthā yātīti jñātam | yato yena jñānena | ghaṭeta yatnaṁ kuryāt ||12||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. alpam eva āyuḥ avaśiṣṭam — only a little life remains; kim aham sādhayeyam — what can I accomplish?; mā śuca — do not grieve; kim iti tribhiḥ — he answers with three verses beginning with “kim”; parokṣaiḥ alakṣitaiḥ hāyanaiḥ varṣaiḥ — years that are unseen or unnoticed; viditam — known; vṛthā yāti iti jñātam — recognized as otherwise passing in vain; yena jñānena — by that awareness; ghaṭeta — one should set oneself to it; yatnam kuryāt — one should make an effort.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One should not grieve, “Only a little life remains—what can I accomplish?” Many years may pass unnoticed and therefore fruitlessly. A single moment becomes valuable when its value is consciously known, because that awareness moves one to make an effort for the highest good.

ŚB 2.1.13

खट्‍वाङ्गो नाम राजर्षिर्ज्ञात्वेयत्तामिहायुष: ।
मुहूर्तात्सर्वमुत्सृज्य गतवानभयं हरिम् ॥ १३ ॥
khaṭvāṅgo nāma rājarṣir
jñātveyattām ihāyuṣaḥ
muhūrtāt sarvam utsṛjya
gatavān abhayaṁ harim
The royal sage named Khaṭvāṅga, learning the remaining extent of his life, abandoned everything within a moment and went to Hari, the fearless refuge.
Word-for-word

khaṭvāṅgaḥ — Khaṭvāṅga; nāma — named; rāja-ṛṣiḥ — royal sage; jñātvā — having learned; iyattām — the limited extent; iha — here; āyuṣaḥ — of his lifespan; muhūrtāt — within a moment; sarvam — everything; utsṛjya — abandoning; gatavān — went or attained; abhayam — the fearless state or refuge; harim — Hari.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

खट्वाङ्गो हि देवपक्षे स्थित्वा दैत्यानजयत् । ततः प्रसन्नैर्देवैः “वरं वृणीष्व” इत्युक्ते तेनोक्तं प्रथमं तावन्ममायुः कथ्यतामिति । ततो देवैरुक्तं तत्तु मुहूर्तमात्रमस्तीति । ततोऽतिशीघ्रं विमानेन भुवमागत्य हरिं शरणं गत इति । यत इयं स्वर्गभूमी रजोधिका । कर्मभूमिः पृथ्वी ॥ १३ ॥

khaṭvāṅgo hi deva-pakṣe sthitvā daityān ajayat | tataḥ prasannair devair “varaṁ vṛṇīṣva” ity ukte tenoktaṁ prathamaṁ tāvan mamāyuḥ kathyatām iti | tato devair uktaṁ tat tu muhūrta-mātram astīti | tato ’tiśīghraṁ vimānena bhuvam āgatya hariṁ śaraṇaṁ gata iti | yata iyaṁ svarga-bhūmī rajo’dhikā | karma-bhūmiḥ pṛthvī ||13||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. deva-pakṣe sthitvā — having taken the side of the gods; daityān ajayat — he defeated the demons; prasannaiḥ devaiḥ — by the pleased gods; varaṁ vṛṇīṣva — “choose a boon”; mama āyuḥ kathyatām — let my remaining lifespan first be told; muhūrta-mātram asti — only a muhūrta remains; atiśīghram — immediately, with great speed; vimānena bhuvam āgatya — returning to earth by aerial car; harim śaraṇam gataḥ — he took refuge in Hari; svarga-bhūmī rajo’dhikā — heaven is a region in which rajas predominates; karma-bhūmiḥ pṛthvī — earth is the field of action.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Khaṭvāṅga fought for the gods and defeated the demons. When they offered him a boon, he first asked how much life remained. Hearing that only a muhūrta was left, he immediately returned to earth and took refuge in Hari. Śrīdhara adds that heaven is dominated by rajas, whereas earth is the field in which decisive spiritual action is performed.

ŚB 2.1.14

तवाप्येतर्हि कौरव्य सप्ताहं जीवितावधि: ।
उपकल्पय तत्सर्वं तावद्यत्साम्परायिकम् ॥ १४ ॥
tavāpy etarhi kauravya
saptāhaṁ jīvitāvadhiḥ
upakalpaya tat sarvaṁ
tāvad yat sāmparāyikam
O descendant of Kuru, your life now has a limit of seven days. Within that time, therefore, accomplish everything that pertains to the final passage.
Word-for-word

tava — your; api — indeed, also; etarhi — now; kauravya — O descendant of Kuru; saptāham — seven days; jīvita-avadhiḥ — the limit of life; upakalpaya — arrange or accomplish; tat sarvam — all that; tāvat — within that span; yat — which; sāmparāyikam — pertaining to the hereafter or final passage.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तव तु एतर्ह्यपीदानीमपि । तावदिति तावता कालेन साम्परायिकं पारलौकिकं साधनं सम्पादय ॥ १४ ॥

tava tu etarhy apīdānīm api | tāvad iti tāvatā kālena sāmparāyikaṁ pāralaukikaṁ sādhanaṁ sampādaya ||14||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. tava tu — as for you; etarhi — now; idānīm api — even at this very time; tāvat iti — by “within that span”; tāvatā kālena — in that much time; sāmparāyikam — pertaining to the final passage; pāralaukikam sādhanam — the means relating to the world beyond; sampādaya — accomplish.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. For Parīkṣit, “now” means precisely this present moment, and “within that span” means within the seven days that remain. Śrīdhara therefore reads the verse as an instruction to accomplish, in that allotted time, the means pertaining to the final passage and the world beyond.

ŚB 2.1.15

अन्तकाले तु पुरुष आगते गतसाध्वस: ।
छिन्द्यादसङ्गशस्त्रेण स्पृहां देहेऽनु ये च तम् ॥ १५ ॥
anta-kāle tu puruṣa
āgate gata-sādhvasaḥ
chindyād asaṅga-śastreṇa
spṛhāṁ dehe ’nu ye ca tam
When the final hour has come, a person should cast off fear and, with the weapon of nonattachment, cut away longing for the body and for all that follows upon it.
Word-for-word

anta-kāle — at the final time; tu — indeed; puruṣaḥ — a person; āgate — when it has arrived; gata-sādhvasaḥ — free from fear; chindyāt — should cut off; asaṅga-śastreṇa — with the weapon of nonattachment; spṛhām — longing; dehe — for the body; anu — following or connected with it; ye — those who or those which; ca — and; tam — it.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तत्र वैराग्यं तावदाह—अन्तकाल इति । गतसाध्वसो मृत्यु-भय-शून्यः । असङ्गो नाम अनासक्तिस्तेन शस्त्रेण स्पृहां सुखेच्छां, तं देहमनु ये पुत्रकलत्रादयस्तेष्वपि स्पृहां छिन्द्यात् ॥ १५ ॥

tatra vairāgyaṁ tāvad āha—anta-kāla iti | gata-sādhvaso mṛtyu-bhaya-śūnyaḥ | asaṅgo nāma anāsaktis tena śastreṇa spṛhāṁ sukhecchāṁ, taṁ deham anu ye putra-kalatrādayas teṣv api spṛhāṁ chindyāt ||15||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. vairāgyam tāvat āha — first he teaches renunciation; gata-sādhvasaḥ — free from fear; mṛtyu-bhaya-śūnyaḥ — devoid of fear of death; asaṅgaḥ nāma anāsaktiḥ — nonattachment means absence of clinging; tena śastreṇa — with that as a weapon; spṛhām — longing; sukha-icchām — the wish for pleasure; taṁ deham anu ye — those who follow upon or are connected with the body; putra-kalatra-ādayaḥ — sons, spouse and others; teṣu api spṛhām chindyāt — one should cut off longing for them as well.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śuka first teaches vairāgya. To be “free from fear” is to be free from fear of death. Nonattachment itself is the weapon: with it one cuts away the desire for bodily pleasure and also attachment to children, spouse, and everything else bound up with the body.

ŚB 2.1.16

गृहात् प्रव्रजितो धीर: पुण्यतीर्थजलाप्लुत: ।
शुचौ विविक्त आसीनो विधिवत्कल्पितासने ॥ १६ ॥
gṛhāt pravrajito dhīraḥ
puṇya-tīrtha-jalāplutaḥ
śucau vivikta āsīno
vidhivat kalpitāsane
Having left home, steady and self-controlled, and bathed in the water of a sacred tīrtha, one should sit in a pure, secluded place upon a seat properly prepared.
Word-for-word

gṛhāt — from home; pravrajitaḥ — having departed; dhīraḥ — steady or self-controlled; puṇya-tīrtha-jala-āplutaḥ — bathed in the waters of a sacred tīrtha; śucau — in a pure place; vivikte — secluded; āsīnaḥ — seated; vidhivat — according to rule; kalpita-āsane — upon a properly prepared seat.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

किं च गृहात् प्रव्रजितो निर्गतः । गृहे स्थितस्य पुनरप्यासक्तिसम्भवात् । तत्राष्टाङ्गयोगमाह । धीर इति ब्रह्मचर्यादियमोपलक्षणम् । पुण्यतीर्थेति स्नानादिनियमोपलक्षणम् । आसनमाह—शुचाविति । विविक्ते एकान्ते । विधिवत् कुशाजिनचैलैः क्रमेण निर्मिते ॥ १६ ॥

kiṁ ca gṛhāt pravrajito nirgataḥ | gṛhe sthitasya punar apy āsakti-sambhavāt | tatrāṣṭāṅga-yogam āha | dhīra iti brahmacaryādi-yamopalakṣaṇam | puṇya-tīrtheti snānādi-niyamopalakṣaṇam | āsanam āha—śucāv iti | vivikte ekānte | vidhivat kuśājina-cailaiḥ krameṇa nirmite ||16||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. gṛhāt pravrajitaḥ — gone forth from home; nirgataḥ — having departed; gṛhe sthitasya — for one remaining at home; punar api āsakti-sambhavāt — because attachment may arise again; aṣṭāṅga-yogam āha — he now teaches eight-limbed yoga; dhīra iti — “steady” indicates; brahmacarya-ādi-yama — restraints such as celibacy; puṇya-tīrtha — sacred tīrtha; snāna-ādi-niyama — observances such as bathing; śucau — in a pure place; vivikte ekānte — secluded, solitary; vidhivat — according to rule; kuśa-ajina-cailaiḥ krameṇa nirmite — prepared in order from kuśa grass, deerskin, and cloth.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One leaves home because remaining there may allow attachment to arise again. Śrīdhara sees the verse as beginning the eight-limbed yoga: “steady” points to restraints such as brahmacarya; bathing at a sacred tīrtha points to observances; and the seat is to be pure, solitary, and properly layered with kuśa grass, deerskin, and cloth.

ŚB 2.1.17

अभ्यसेन्मनसा शुद्धं त्रिवृद्ब्रह्माक्षरं परम् ।
मनो यच्छेज्जितश्वासो ब्रह्मबीजमविस्मरन् ॥ १७ ॥
abhyasen manasā śuddhaṁ
trivṛd-brahmākṣaraṁ param
mano yacchej jita-śvāso
brahma-bījam avismaran
One should mentally repeat the pure, supreme, threefold syllable of Brahman and, mastering the breath, restrain the mind without forgetting that brahma-seed.
Word-for-word

abhyaset — should practice or repeat; manasā — mentally; śuddham — pure; tri-vṛt — threefold; brahma-akṣaram — the sacred syllable of Brahman; param — supreme; manaḥ — the mind; yacchet — should restrain; jita-śvāsaḥ — with the breath mastered; brahma-bījam — the seed of Brahman; avismaran — not forgetting.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

जपगर्भं प्राणायामं वक्तुं जप्यमाह । त्रिवृद्ब्रह्माक्षरं त्रिवृत् त्रिभिरकारादिभिर्वर्तितं ग्रथितं ब्रह्माक्षरं प्रणवं मनसाऽभ्यसेदावर्तयेत् । मनसेन्द्रियप्रत्याहारं वक्तुं प्राणायामेन मनोनियमनमाह । मनो यच्छेद्वशीकुर्यात् । ब्रह्मबीजं प्रणवमविस्मरन्नेव जितश्वासः सन् ॥ १७ ॥

japa-garbhaṁ prāṇāyāmaṁ vaktuṁ japyam āha | trivṛd-brahmākṣaraṁ trivṛt tribhir akārādibhir vartitaṁ grathitaṁ brahmākṣaraṁ praṇavaṁ manasābhyased āvartayet | manasendriya-pratyāhāraṁ vaktuṁ prāṇāyāmena mano-niyamanam āha | mano yacched vaśīkuryāt | brahma-bījaṁ praṇavam avismarann eva jita-śvāsaḥ san ||17||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. japa-garbham prāṇāyāmam — prāṇāyāma containing japa; vaktum japyam āha — to teach it, he first states what is to be repeated; trivṛd-brahmākṣaram — the threefold syllable of Brahman; tribhiḥ akārādibhiḥ — by the three letters beginning with a; vartitam grathitam — formed and woven together; praṇavam — praṇava, Oṁ; manasā abhyaset āvartayet — one should mentally practice and repeat it; prāṇāyāmena mano-niyamanam — restraint of the mind through breath regulation; mano yacchet vaśīkuryāt — one should bring the mind under control; brahma-bījam praṇavam avismaran — never forgetting praṇava, the seed of Brahman; jita-śvāsaḥ san — while having mastered the breath.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. To teach prāṇāyāma joined with japa, Śrīdhara first identifies the object of repetition: praṇava, Oṁ, the threefold brahma-syllable formed from the three letters beginning with a. It is repeated mentally. Breath regulation then supports control of the mind, all while the practitioner keeps the brahma-seed present in awareness.

ŚB 2.1.18

नियच्छेद्विषयेभ्योऽक्षान्मनसा बुद्धिसारथि: ।
मन: कर्मभिराक्षिप्तं शुभार्थे धारयेद्धिया ॥ १८ ॥
niyacched viṣayebhyo ’kṣān
manasā buddhi-sārathiḥ
manaḥ karmabhir ākṣiptaṁ
śubhārthe dhārayed dhiyā
With intelligence as charioteer, one should use the mind to withdraw the senses from their objects; then, by discernment, fix the mind—though pulled about by past activity—upon the auspicious object.
Word-for-word

niyacchet — should withdraw or restrain; viṣayebhyaḥ — from sense objects; akṣān — the senses; manasā — by the mind; buddhi-sārathiḥ — with intelligence as charioteer; manaḥ — the mind; karmabhiḥ — by actions; ākṣiptam — pulled or agitated; śubha-arthe — upon the auspicious object; dhārayet — should hold or fix; dhiyā — by intelligence.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

प्रत्याहारमाह । नियच्छेन्निगृह्णीयात् । अक्षानिन्द्रियाणि । निश्चयात्मिका बुद्धिः सारथिर्यस्य सः । धारणामाह—मन इति । पुनश्च कर्मभिस्तद्वासनाभिराक्षिप्तमाकृष्टम् । शुभार्थे भगवद्रूपे ॥ १८ ॥

pratyāhāram āha | niyacchen nigṛhṇīyāt | akṣān indriyāṇi | niścayātmikā buddhiḥ sārathir yasya saḥ | dhāraṇām āha—mana iti | punaś ca karmabhis tad-vāsanābhir ākṣiptam ākṛṣṭam | śubhārthe bhagavad-rūpe ||18||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. pratyāhāram āha — he teaches withdrawal of the senses; niyacchet — should restrain; nigṛhṇīyāt — should hold back; akṣān — the senses; indriyāṇi — the sense faculties; niścayātmikā buddhiḥ — decisive intelligence; sārathiḥ yasya saḥ — one whose charioteer it is; dhāraṇām āha — he teaches concentration; karmabhiḥ — by actions; tad-vāsanābhiḥ — by the latent impressions arising from them; ākṣiptam ākṛṣṭam — pulled and dragged about; śubhārthe — upon the auspicious object; bhagavad-rūpe — upon the form of Bhagavān.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The first half teaches pratyāhāra: the senses are restrained from their objects under the direction of decisive intelligence. The second teaches dhāraṇā. Though the mind is pulled by actions and their residual impressions, intelligence should hold it upon the auspicious object—Śrīdhara identifies this explicitly as the form of Bhagavān.

ŚB 2.1.19

तत्रैकावयवं ध्यायेदव्युच्छिन्नेन चेतसा ।
मनो निर्विषयं युक्त्वा तत: किञ्चन न स्मरेत् ।
पदं तत्परमं विष्णोर्मनो यत्र प्रसीदति ॥ १९ ॥
tatraikāvayavaṁ dhyāyed
avyucchinnena cetasā
mano nirviṣayaṁ yuktvā
tataḥ kiñcana na smaret
padaṁ tat paramaṁ viṣṇor
mano yatra prasīdati
Then one should meditate on each limb in turn with unbroken awareness of the complete form. Having fixed the mind free from sense-objects, one should remember nothing else. That is Viṣṇu’s supreme station, where the mind becomes serene.
Word-for-word

tatra — thereupon; eka-avayavam — one limb at a time; dhyāyet — should meditate; avyucchinnena — unbroken, without disconnecting; cetasā — with consciousness; manaḥ — the mind; nirviṣayam — free from sense objects; yuktvā — having fixed; tataḥ — then; kiñcana — anything else; na smaret — should not remember; padam — state or station; tat — that; paramam — supreme; viṣṇoḥ — of Viṣṇu; manaḥ — the mind; yatra — where; prasīdati — becomes serene.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ध्यानमाह—तत्र इति । एकमेकं पादाद्यवयवम् । अव्युच्छिन्नेन समग्ररूपादवियुक्तेन । आश्रयविशेषेण सामान्यतश्चित्तस्थिरीकरणं धारणा । अवयवविभावनया तद्दार्ढ्यं ध्यानमिति भेदः । समाधिमाह । निर्विषयं मनो युंक्त्वा समाधाय । स्थिरीभूते मनसि स्फुरत्परमानन्दमात्राकारं कृत्वेत्यर्थः । प्रसीदति उपशाम्यति ॥ १९ ॥

dhyānam āha—tatra iti | ekam ekaṁ pādādy avayavam | avyucchinnena samagra-rūpād aviyuktena | āśraya-viśeṣeṇa sāmānyataś citta-sthirīkaraṇaṁ dhāraṇā | avayava-vibhāvanayā tad-dārḍhyaṁ dhyānam iti bhedaḥ | samādhim āha | nirviṣayaṁ mano yuṁktvā samādhāya | sthirībhūte manasi sphurat-paramānanda-mātrākāraṁ kṛtvety arthaḥ | prasīdati upaśāmyati ||19||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. dhyānam āha — he teaches meditation; ekam ekam pādādi avayavam — each individual limb beginning with the feet; avyucchinnena — without interruption; samagra-rūpāt aviyuktena — without separating it from the complete form; āśraya-viśeṣeṇa — by a particular support; citta-sthirīkaraṇam — stabilization of consciousness; dhāraṇā — concentration; avayava-vibhāvanayā — through contemplation of the limbs; tad-dārḍhyam — intensification of that stability; dhyānam — meditation; samādhim āha — he teaches samādhi; nirviṣayam manaḥ — the mind free from sense objects; samādhāya — having completely fixed it; sthirībhūte manasi — when the mind has become steady; sphurat-paramānanda-mātra-ākāram — taking the form of nothing but manifest supreme bliss; prasīdati — becomes serene; upaśāmyati — comes to rest.

Witness note. The TTD printed recension adds an explicit distinction after the discussion of dhyāna: tatra ca mano-vṛtteḥ vilayaḥ samādhir iti vivekaḥ — “there, the dissolution of mental modification is distinguished as samādhi.”

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Meditation proceeds limb by limb, beginning with the feet, while each limb remains inseparable from awareness of the complete divine form. Dhāraṇā is stabilization of the mind on a chosen support; dhyāna is the strengthening of that stability through sustained contemplation of the limbs. Samādhi is then indicated when the mind is free from sense-objects, wholly settled, and takes the form of manifest supreme bliss. “Becomes serene” means that the mind comes to rest.

ŚB 2.1.20

रजस्तमोभ्यामाक्षिप्तं विमूढं मन आत्मन: ।
यच्छेद्धारणया धीरो हन्ति या तत्कृतं मलम् ॥ २० ॥
rajas-tamobhyām ākṣiptaṁ
vimūḍhaṁ mana ātmanaḥ
yacched dhāraṇayā dhīro
hanti yā tat-kṛtaṁ malam
If one’s own mind is agitated by rajas and bewildered by tamas, the steady practitioner should restrain it through dhāraṇā, which destroys the impurity produced by those modes.
Word-for-word

rajas-tamobhyām — by rajas and tamas; ākṣiptam — agitated; vimūḍham — bewildered; manaḥ — the mind; ātmanaḥ — one’s own; yacchet — should restrain; dhāraṇayā — by concentration; dhīraḥ — the steady person; hanti — destroys; yā — which; tat-kṛtam — produced by them; malam — impurity.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

गुणवशात् पुनरपि क्षोभे सति धारणामेव स्थिरीकुर्यादित्याह । रजसा आक्षिप्तं तमसा विमूढं स्वीयं मनो निरुन्ध्यात् । तत्कृतं रजस्तमोभ्यां कृतम् ॥ २० ॥

guṇa-vaśāt punar api kṣobhe sati dhāraṇām eva sthirīkuryād ity āha | rajasā ākṣiptaṁ tamasā vimūḍhaṁ svīyaṁ mano nirundhyāt | tat-kṛtaṁ rajas-tamobhyāṁ kṛtam ||20||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. guṇa-vaśāt — under the influence of the guṇas; punar api kṣobhe sati — if agitation arises again; dhāraṇām eva sthirīkuryāt — one should stabilize concentration itself; rajasā ākṣiptam — agitated by rajas; tamasā vimūḍham — bewildered by tamas; svīyam manaḥ — one’s own mind; nirundhyāt — one should restrain; tat-kṛtam — produced by those; rajas-tamobhyām kṛtam — caused by rajas and tamas.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. If the guṇas disturb the mind again, the practitioner should re-establish dhāraṇā. Rajas agitates the mind and tamas bewilders it; concentration restrains that disturbed mind and removes the impurity produced by those two modes.

ŚB 2.1.21

यस्यां सन्धार्यमाणायां योगिनो भक्तिलक्षण: ।
आशु सम्पद्यते योग आश्रयं भद्रमीक्षत: ॥ २१ ॥
yasyāṁ sandhāryamāṇāyāṁ
yogino bhakti-lakṣaṇaḥ
āśu sampadyate yoga
āśrayaṁ bhadram īkṣataḥ
When such concentration is steadily maintained, the yogī who beholds the auspicious refuge quickly attains yoga marked by devotion.
Word-for-word

yasyām — in which concentration; sandhāryamāṇāyām — while being steadily maintained; yoginaḥ — of the yogī; bhakti-lakṣaṇaḥ — marked or characterized by devotion; āśu — quickly; sampadyate — arises or becomes accomplished; yogaḥ — yoga, spiritual integration; āśrayam — the support or refuge; bhadram — auspicious, blessed; īkṣataḥ — of one who beholds.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

यत इति । यतो यस्यां क्रियमाणायां भद्रं सुखात्मकम् आश्रयं विषयं पश्यतस्तत्रैव प्रीतिर्भवति ॥ २१ ॥

yata iti | yato yasyāṁ kriyamāṇāyāṁ bhadraṁ sukhātmakam āśrayaṁ viṣayaṁ paśyatas tatraiva prītir bhavati ||21||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. yata iti — on the word “from which/because”; yasyāṁ kriyamāṇāyām — when that concentration is being practiced; bhadram — the auspicious; sukhātmakam — whose nature is happiness; āśrayam — the support or refuge; viṣayam — the object; paśyataḥ — for one seeing it; tatra eva — toward that very object; prītiḥ bhavati — love or delight arises.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. When this concentration is actually practiced, the yogī beholds the auspicious support, the object whose very nature is happiness. Because that object is directly contemplated, love arises toward that very object; this explains why the resulting yoga bears the mark of bhakti.

ŚB 2.1.22

राजोवाच
यथा सन्धार्यते ब्रह्मन् धारणा यत्र सम्मता ।
यादृशी वा हरेदाशु पुरुषस्य मनोमलम् ॥ २२ ॥
rājovāca
yathā sandhāryate brahman
dhāraṇā yatra sammatā
yādṛśī vā hared āśu
puruṣasya mano-malam
The King said: O brāhmaṇa, how is concentration to be sustained, upon what is it properly fixed, and of what kind must it be to swiftly remove a person’s mental impurity?
Word-for-word

rājā uvāca — the King said; yathā — how; sandhāryate — is sustained or fixed; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; dhāraṇā — concentration; yatra — where, upon what; sammatā — approved or accepted; yādṛśī — of what kind; vā — or; haret — would remove; āśu — swiftly; puruṣasya — of a person; manaḥ-malam — impurity of the mind.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

यथेति । यथा यत्र यादृशी चेतिकर्तव्यताविषयतत्तद्विशेषाणां प्रश्नाः ॥ २२ ॥

yatheti | yathā yatra yādṛśī ceti-kartavyatā-viṣaya-tat-tad-viśeṣāṇāṁ praśnāḥ ||22||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. yathā — “how”; yatra — “where/upon what”; yādṛśī — “of what kind”; ca — and; iti-kartavyatā — the procedure, how it is to be done; viṣaya — the object or locus; tat-tad-viśeṣāṇām — of those respective particulars; praśnāḥ — questions.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara reads the three interrogatives separately. “How?” asks for the procedure of dhāraṇā; “where?” asks for its proper object; and “of what kind?” asks for the particular form of concentration capable of removing mental impurity.

ŚB 2.1.23

श्रीशुक उवाच
जितासनो जितश्वासो जितसङ्गो जितेन्द्रिय: ।
स्थूले भगवतो रूपे मन: सन्धारयेद्धिया ॥ २३ ॥
śrī-śuka uvāca
jitāsano jita-śvāso
jita-saṅgo jitendriyaḥ
sthūle bhagavato rūpe
manaḥ sandhārayed dhiyā
Śrī Śuka said: Having mastered posture, breath, attachment, and the senses, one should use discernment to hold the mind upon Bhagavān’s gross universal form.
Word-for-word

śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śuka said; jita-āsanaḥ — one who has mastered the sitting posture; jita-śvāsaḥ — one who has mastered the breath; jita-saṅgaḥ — one who has mastered attachment or association; jita-indriyaḥ — one whose senses are mastered; sthūle — in the gross form; bhagavataḥ — of Bhagavān; rūpe — in the form; manaḥ — the mind; sandhārayet — should hold firmly; dhiyā — by intelligence or discernment.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

यथेत्यस्य उत्तरम्—जितासन इति । विषयमाह—स्थूल इति सार्धेन ॥ २३ ॥

yathety asya uttaram—jitāsana iti | viṣayam āha—sthūla iti sārdhena ||23||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. yathā iti asya uttaram — the answer to the question “how?”; jitāsana iti — is given with “one who has mastered the posture”; viṣayam āha — he states the object; sthūla iti — with the word “gross”; sārdhena — together with the following half-verse.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The phrase beginning with “having mastered the posture” answers the King’s question about how the concentration is performed. The word “gross,” together with the following half-verse, begins the answer concerning the object on which the mind is to be fixed.

ŚB 2.1.24

विशेषस्तस्य देहोऽयं स्थविष्ठश्च स्थवीयसाम् ।
यत्रेदं व्यज्यते विश्वं भूतं भव्यं भवच्च सत् ॥ २४ ॥
viśeṣas tasya deho ’yaṁ
sthaviṣṭhaś ca sthavīyasām
yatredaṁ vyajyate viśvaṁ
bhūtaṁ bhavyaṁ bhavac ca sat
This particular manifestation is His body, the grossest among gross things, in which the universe—existing as past, future, and present—becomes manifest.
Word-for-word

viśeṣaḥ — the particular manifestation; tasya — His; dehaḥ — body; ayam — this; sthaviṣṭhaḥ — the grossest; ca — and; sthavīyasām — among gross things; yatra — in which; idam — this; vyajyate — is manifested; viśvam — the universe; bhūtam — past; bhavyam — future; bhavat — present; ca — and; sat — the existent, the total manifested effect.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

विशेष इति । विशेषो विराड्देहः अतिस्थूलानामपि स्थूलतरः । सत् इति कार्यमात्रम् ॥ २४ ॥

viśeṣa iti | viśeṣo virāḍ-dehaḥ atisthūlānām api sthūlataraḥ | sat iti kārya-mātram ||24||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. viśeṣa iti — on the word “particular manifestation”; viśeṣaḥ — the particular form; virāḍ-dehaḥ — is the body of Virāṭ; atisthūlānām api — even among extremely gross things; sthūlataraḥ — grosser still; sat iti — the word “existent”; kārya-mātram — means the entire realm of effects or manifested products.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “The particular manifestation” is the virāṭ body. It is grosser even than what is ordinarily regarded as extremely gross. The word sat here denotes the whole manifested order of effects.

ŚB 2.1.25

अण्डकोशे शरीरेऽस्मिन् सप्तावरणसंयुते ।
वैराज: पुरुषो योऽसौ भगवान् धारणाश्रय: ॥ २५ ॥
aṇḍa-kośe śarīre ’smin
saptāvaraṇa-saṁyute
vairājaḥ puruṣo yo ’sau
bhagavān dhāraṇāśrayaḥ
Within this body of the cosmic egg, enclosed by seven coverings, the Vairāja Puruṣa—Bhagavān Himself—is the support and object of concentration.
Word-for-word

aṇḍa-kośe — within the cosmic egg or shell; śarīre — in the body; asmin — this; sapta-āvaraṇa-saṁyute — enclosed by seven coverings; vairājaḥ — pertaining to Virāj, the universal form; puruṣaḥ — the Puruṣa; yaḥ asau — that very one who; bhagavān — Bhagavān; dhāraṇā-āśrayaḥ — the support or object of concentration.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

अस्य चोपलक्षणत्वेन विषयत्वम् । वस्तुतस्तु विराट् जीवनियन्ता भगवानेव विषय इत्याह—अण्डकोश इति । अण्डकोशे अण्डकोशान्तर्वर्तिनि कटाह एव पृथिव्यावरणम् । ततोऽप्तेजोवाय्वाकाशाहङ्कारमहत्तत्त्वानीति सप्त ॥ २५ ॥

asya copalakṣaṇatvena viṣayatvam | vastutas tu virāṭ-jīva-niyantā bhagavān eva viṣaya ity āha—aṇḍa-kośa iti | aṇḍa-kośe aṇḍa-kośāntar-vartini kaṭāha eva pṛthivy-āvaraṇam | tato ’p-tejo-vāyv-ākāśāhaṅkāra-mahat-tattvānīti sapta ||25||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. asya — of this virāṭ form; upalakṣaṇatvena — by serving as an indication or representative; viṣayatvam — its being the object of meditation; vastutaḥ tu — but in reality; virāṭ-jīva-niyantā — the ruler of the living principle of Virāṭ; bhagavān eva — Bhagavān alone; viṣayaḥ — is the true object; aṇḍa-kośe — in the cosmic egg; aṇḍa-kośāntar-vartini kaṭāhe — in the shell situated within the cosmic egg; pṛthivī-āvaraṇam — the earth covering; tataḥ — thereafter; ap-tejas-vāyu-ākāśa-ahaṅkāra-mahat-tattvāni — water, fire, air, ether, ahaṅkāra, and mahat; sapta — seven in all together with earth.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The virāṭ body is an object of concentration by way of representation; ultimately, the true object is Bhagavān, the inner ruler of the living principle associated with Virāṭ. Śrīdhara then enumerates the seven cosmic coverings: earth, water, fire, air, ether, ahaṅkāra, and mahat.

ŚB 2.1.26

पातालमेतस्य हि पादमूलं
पठन्ति पार्ष्णिप्रपदे रसातलम् ।
महातलं विश्वसृजोऽथ गुल्फौ
तलातलं वै पुरुषस्य जङ्घे ॥ २६ ॥
pātālam etasya hi pāda-mūlaṁ
paṭhanti pārṣṇi-prapade rasātalam
mahātalaṁ viśva-sṛjo ’tha gulphau
talātalaṁ vai puruṣasya jaṅghe
They declare Pātāla to be the soles of His feet and Rasātala His heels and foreparts of the feet; Mahātala is the two ankles of the universal creator, and Talātala the shanks of the Puruṣa.
Word-for-word

pātālam — Pātāla; etasya — of Him; hi — indeed; pāda-mūlam — the base or soles of the feet; paṭhanti — they declare in authoritative teaching; pārṣṇi-prapade — the heels and foreparts of the feet; rasātalam — Rasātala; mahātalam — Mahātala; viśva-sṛjaḥ — of the creator of the universe; atha — then; gulphau — the two ankles; talātalam — Talātala; vai — indeed; puruṣasya — of the Puruṣa; jaṅghe — the two shanks.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

विराड्देहतज्जीवतदन्तर्यामिणामभेदमारोप्योपासनं कर्तव्यमित्याशयेनाह—पातालमिति । पातालं पादमूलं पादस्याधोभागम् । पातालादीनां तदवयवता विधीयते । पातालादीन्यतलान्तान्यधस्तनादारभ्य सप्त भूविवराणि । पठन्ति गृणन्तीत्यादि प्रमाणप्रदर्शनम् । पार्ष्णिप्रपदे पादस्य पश्चात्पुरोभागौ ॥ २६ ॥

virāḍ-deha-taj-jīva-tad-antaryāmiṇām abhedam āropyopāsanaṁ kartavyam ity āśayenāha—pātālam iti | pātālaṁ pāda-mūlaṁ pādasyādho-bhāgam | pātālādīnāṁ tad-avayavatā vidhīyate | pātālādīny atalāntāny adhastanād ārabhya sapta bhū-vivarāṇi | paṭhanti gṛṇantīty-ādi pramāṇa-pradarśanam | pārṣṇi-prapade pādasya paścāt-puro-bhāgau ||26||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. virāḍ-deha — the body of Virāṭ; taj-jīva — its associated living principle; tad-antaryāmiṇām — and their indwelling ruler; abhedam āropya — superimposing non-difference; upāsanam kartavyam — meditation is to be performed; pātālam — Pātāla; pāda-mūlam — the base of the foot; pādasya adho-bhāgam — the underside of the foot; tad-avayavatā vidhīyate — their status as His limbs is prescribed; pātālādīni atalāntāni — the regions from Pātāla through Atala; adhastanāt ārabhya — beginning from the lowest; sapta bhū-vivarāṇi — the seven subterranean regions; paṭhanti gṛṇanti — “they declare,” “they recite”; pramāṇa-pradarśanam — shows appeal to scriptural authority; pārṣṇi-prapade — heel and forefoot; paścāt-puro-bhāgau — rear and front portions of the foot.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Meditation proceeds by contemplatively superimposing non-difference among the virāṭ body, its living principle, and the indwelling Lord. The seven subterranean regions, beginning from the lowest Pātāla and ending with Atala, are contemplated as limbs of that form. Words such as “they declare” signal that this mapping is presented on scriptural authority.

ŚB 2.1.27

द्वे जानुनी सुतलं विश्वमूर्ते-
रूरुद्वयं वितलं चातलं च ।
महीतलं तज्जघनं महीपते
नभस्तलं नाभिसरो गृणन्ति ॥ २७ ॥
dve jānunī sutalaṁ viśva-mūrter
ūru-dvayaṁ vitalaṁ cātalaṁ ca
mahītalaṁ taj-jaghanaṁ mahīpate
nabhastalaṁ nābhi-saro gṛṇanti
Sutala is the two knees of the universal form; Vitala and Atala are His two thighs. O lord of the earth, Mahītala is His hip-region, and the region of space is the lake-like hollow of His navel.
Word-for-word

dve jānunī — the two knees; sutalam — Sutala; viśva-mūrteḥ — of the universal form; ūru-dvayam — the pair of thighs; vitalam — Vitala; ca — and; atalam — Atala; ca — and; mahītalam — Mahītala, the earth-plane; tat-jaghanam — His hip or pelvic region; mahīpate — O lord of the earth; nabhastalam — the region of space; nābhi-saraḥ — the navel as a lake or hollow; gṛṇanti — they declare.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

द्वे इति । ऊरुद्वयं तस्याधोभागे वितलम् उत्तरभागे अतलमिति ज्ञेयम् । नभस्तलं भुवर्लोकम् । नाभिरेव सरः ॥ २७ ॥

dve iti | ūru-dvayaṁ tasyādho-bhāge vitalam uttara-bhāge atalam iti jñeyam | nabhastalaṁ bhuvar-lokam | nābhir eva saraḥ ||27||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. dve iti — on the verse beginning “two”; ūru-dvayam — the pair of thighs; tasya adho-bhāge — in their lower portion; vitalam — Vitala; uttara-bhāge — in the upper portion; atalam — Atala; iti jñeyam — thus it should be understood; nabhastalam — the region of space; bhuvar-lokam — Bhuvarloka; nābhiḥ eva — the navel itself; saraḥ — is the lake.

Witness note. The TTD printed recension explicitly preserves nabhastalaṁ bhuvar-lokam, “the region of space is Bhuvarloka.” The GGM/Chaukhamba transcription has the same core gloss but omits that short phrase.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Vitala is assigned to the lower portions of the two thighs and Atala to their upper portions. The “region of space” is Bhuvarloka, while the navel itself is described as the lake.

ŚB 2.1.28

उर:स्थलं ज्योतिरनीकमस्य
ग्रीवा महर्वदनं वै जनोऽस्य ।
तपो वराटीं विदुरादिपुंस:
सत्यं तु शीर्षाणि सहस्रशीर्ष्ण: ॥ २८ ॥
uraḥ-sthalaṁ jyotir-anīkam asya
grīvā mahar vadanaṁ vai jano ’sya
tapo varāṭīṁ vidur ādi-puṁsaḥ
satyaṁ tu śīrṣāṇi sahasra-śīrṣṇaḥ
His chest is the host of luminaries; Maharloka is His neck and Janaloka His mouth. They know Tapoloka as the forehead of the Primal Person, while Satyaloka is the heads of the Thousand-headed Lord.
Word-for-word

uraḥ-sthalam — the chest-region; jyotiḥ-anīkam — the host or array of luminaries; asya — of Him; grīvā — the neck; mahaḥ — Maharloka; vadanam — the mouth; vai — indeed; janaḥ — Janaloka; asya — His; tapaḥ — Tapoloka; varāṭīm — the forehead; viduḥ — they know; ādi-puṁsaḥ — of the Primal Person; satyam — Satyaloka; tu — while; śīrṣāṇi — the heads; sahasra-śīrṣṇaḥ — of the thousand-headed One.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

उरःस्थलमिति । ज्योतिरनीकं ज्योतिषां समूहं स्वर्गम् । महर्लोकं ग्रीवेति गृणन्ति । तपोलोकं रराटीं ललाटम् । सत्यं सत्यलोकं तच्छिरः ॥ २८ ॥

uraḥ-sthalam iti | jyotir-anīkaṁ jyotiṣāṁ samūhaṁ svargam | mahar-lokaṁ grīveti gṛṇanti | tapo-lokaṁ rarāṭīṁ lalāṭam | satyaṁ satya-lokaṁ tac-chiraḥ ||28||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. uraḥ-sthalam iti — on “the chest-region”; jyotir-anīkam — the array of lights; jyotiṣām samūham — the collection of luminaries; svargam — heaven, Svargaloka; mahar-lokam — Maharloka; grīvā iti — as the neck; gṛṇanti — they declare; tapo-lokam — Tapoloka; rarāṭīm — the forehead; lalāṭam — forehead; satyam satya-lokam — “Satya,” meaning Satyaloka; tat-śiraḥ — that is His head.

Witness note. The TTD recension preserves the concluding clarification tac-chiraḥ, “that is His head.” Jīva Gosvāmī independently notes the syntax of Śrīdhara’s gloss here, confirming how the verb of declaration is to be construed.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The “host of luminaries” is the celestial region of Svarga. Maharloka is contemplated as the neck, Tapoloka as the forehead, and Satyaloka as the head of the cosmic Person.

ŚB 2.1.29

इन्द्रादयो बाहव आहुरुस्रा:
कर्णौ दिश:श्रोत्रममुष्य शब्द: ।
नासत्यदस्रौ परमस्य नासे
घ्राणोऽस्य गन्धो मुखमग्निरिद्ध: ॥ २९ ॥
indrādayo bāhava āhur usrāḥ
karṇau diśaḥ śrotram amuṣya śabdaḥ
nāsatya-dasrau paramasya nāse
ghrāṇo ’sya gandho mukham agnir iddhaḥ
The radiant gods headed by Indra are said to be His arms; the directions are His ears, and sound His faculty of hearing. Nāsatya and Dasra, the two Aśvins, are the Supreme Person’s nostrils; scent is His sense of smell, and blazing Agni His mouth.
Word-for-word

indra-ādayaḥ — Indra and the other gods; bāhavaḥ — the arms; āhuḥ — they say; usrāḥ — the radiant deities; karṇau — the two ears; diśaḥ — the directions; śrotram — the hearing faculty; amuṣya — of Him; śabdaḥ — sound; nāsatya-dasrau — Nāsatya and Dasra, the Aśvin twins; paramasya — of the Supreme; nāse — the two nostrils; ghrāṇaḥ — the faculty of smell; asya — His; gandhaḥ — scent; mukham — the mouth; agniḥ — Agni; iddhaḥ — blazing.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

इन्द्रादय इति । उस्रा देवास्तेजोमयशरीरत्वात्, ते बाहव इत्याहुः । दिशोऽस्मदादिश्रोत्राधिष्ठात्र्यो देवताः । कर्णौ श्रोत्रस्याधिष्ठानम् । शब्दस्तु श्रोत्रविषयः, स तस्य श्रोत्रेन्द्रियम् । एवं नासिकादिष्वपि । नासत्यदस्रावश्विनौ नासे नासापुटे । इद्धो दीप्तः ॥ २९ ॥

indrādaya iti | usrā devās tejo-maya-śarīratvāt, te bāhava ity āhuḥ | diśo ’smad-ādi-śrotrādhiṣṭhātryo devatāḥ | karṇau śrotrasyādhiṣṭhānam | śabdas tu śrotra-viṣayaḥ, sa tasya śrotrendriyam | evaṁ nāsikādiṣv api | nāsatya-dasrāv aśvinau nāse nāsā-puṭe | iddho dīptaḥ ||29||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. usrāḥ devāḥ — the usrāḥ are the gods; tejo-maya-śarīratvāt — because their bodies are made of radiance; te bāhavaḥ — they are the arms; diśaḥ — the directions; asmad-ādi-śrotra-adhiṣṭhātryaḥ devatāḥ — the deities presiding over the hearing of beings such as ourselves; karṇau — the ears; śrotrasya adhiṣṭhānam — the seat of the hearing faculty; śabdaḥ — sound; śrotra-viṣayaḥ — the object of hearing; sa tasya śrotrendriyam — that functions as His hearing faculty in this mapping; evaṁ nāsikādiṣu api — similarly with the nose and the following faculties; nāsatya-dasrau aśvinau — Nāsatya and Dasra are the two Aśvins; nāse nāsā-puṭe — the two nostrils; iddhaḥ — blazing; dīptaḥ — luminous.

Witness note. Jīva Gosvāmī explicitly cites Śrīdhara’s phrase evaṁ nāsikādiṣv api and notes that the “similarly” is to be understood generally rather than as an exceptionless equation in every detail.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The gods are called usrāḥ because of their radiant bodies, and the deities headed by Indra are contemplated as the arms. The directional deities preside over hearing; the ears are the seat of that faculty, while sound is its object. The same scheme of presiding deity, organ, faculty, and object is then extended to the nose and the other senses.

ŚB 2.1.30

द्यौरक्षिणी चक्षुरभूत्पतङ्ग:
पक्ष्माणि विष्णोरहनी उभे च ।
तद्भ्रूविजृम्भ: परमेष्ठिधिष्ण्य-
मापोऽस्य तालु रस एव जिह्वा ॥ ३० ॥
dyaur akṣiṇī cakṣur abhūt pataṅgaḥ
pakṣmāṇi viṣṇor ahanī ubhe ca
tad-bhrū-vijṛmbhaḥ parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyam
āpo ’sya tālū rasa eva jihvā
The expanse of heaven is His pair of eyes, and the sun became His faculty of sight. Day and night are Viṣṇu’s eyelashes; the movement of His brows is the station of Brahmā. The waters are His palate, and taste itself is His tongue.
Word-for-word

dyauḥ — heaven or the celestial expanse; akṣiṇī — the two eyes; cakṣuḥ — the faculty of sight; abhūt — became; pataṅgaḥ — the sun; pakṣmāṇi — eyelashes; viṣṇoḥ — of Viṣṇu; ahanī — day and night; ubhe — both; ca — and; tat-bhrū-vijṛmbhaḥ — the movement or expansion of His eyebrows; parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyam — the station of Parameṣṭhin, Brahmā; āpaḥ — the waters; asya — His; tālū — palate; rasaḥ — taste; eva — indeed; jihvā — the tongue.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

द्यौरिति । द्यौरन्तरिक्षं चन्द्रसूर्याधिष्ठानभूतत्वात् । अक्षिणी नेत्रगोलके । चक्षुरिन्द्रियं पतङ्गः सूर्यः । अहनी रात्र्यहनी, अहरशब्दस्यैकशेषः । पक्ष्माणि नेत्ररोमाणि । परमेष्ठिधिष्ण्यं ब्रह्मपदम् । विजृम्भः भ्रूविक्षेपसमकालः । तालु अधिष्ठानं, जिह्वा इन्द्रियम् ॥ ३० ॥

dyaur iti | dyaur antarikṣaṁ candra-sūryādhiṣṭhāna-bhūtatvāt | akṣiṇī netra-golake | cakṣur-indriyaṁ pataṅgaḥ sūryaḥ | ahanī rātry-ahanī, ahar-śabdasyaīka-śeṣaḥ | pakṣmāṇi netra-romāṇi | parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyaṁ brahma-padam | vijṛmbhaḥ bhrū-vikṣepa-sama-kālaḥ | tālu adhiṣṭhānaṁ, jihvā indriyam ||30||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. dyauḥ — the celestial expanse; antarikṣam — the intermediate space; candra-sūrya-adhiṣṭhāna-bhūtatvāt — because it is the locus of moon and sun; akṣiṇī — the two eyes; netra-golake — the eyeballs; cakṣuḥ-indriyam — the faculty of sight; pataṅgaḥ sūryaḥ — pataṅga means the sun; ahanī — the two “days”; rātri-ahanī — night and day; ahar-śabdasya eka-śeṣaḥ — the single remaining form of the word ahar stands for the pair; pakṣmāṇi — eyelashes; netra-romāṇi — hairs of the eyes; parameṣṭhi-dhiṣṇyam — the station of Parameṣṭhin; brahma-padam — Brahmā’s station; vijṛmbhaḥ — the expansion or movement; bhrū-vikṣepa-sama-kālaḥ — simultaneous with the motion of the brows; tālu adhiṣṭhānam — the palate is the locus; jihvā indriyam — the tongue is the sense faculty.

Witness note. The TTD printed recension preserves this fuller explanatory form. The GGM/Chaukhamba transcription gives the same core identifications but abbreviates several clauses, including the explanations of the celestial locus, the paired day-and-night expression, the eyelashes, and the brow movement.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara identifies the celestial expanse with the eye-region because it is the locus of the moon and sun; the sun functions as the sight-faculty. “The two days” means the pair night and day, and the eyelashes are literally the hairs of the eyes. The station of Parameṣṭhin is Brahmā’s station, associated with the movement of the cosmic Person’s brows; the palate serves as the locus and the tongue as the corresponding sense faculty.

ŚB 2.1.31

छन्दांस्यनन्तस्य शिरो गृणन्ति
दंष्ट्रा यम: स्‍नेहकला द्विजानि ।
हासो जनोन्मादकरी च माया
दुरन्तसर्गो यदपाङ्गमोक्ष: ॥ ३१ ॥
chandāṁsy anantasya śiro gṛṇanti
daṁṣṭrā yamaḥ sneha-kalā dvijāni
hāso janonmāda-karī ca māyā
duranta-sargo yad-apāṅga-mokṣaḥ
The Vedic metres are declared to be the head of the Infinite; Yama is His fangs, and portions of affection are His teeth. Māyā, which drives people to bewilderment, is His smile, and the boundless creation proceeds from the casting of His sidelong glance.
Word-for-word

chandāṁsi — the Vedic metres and hymns; anantasya — of the Infinite; śiraḥ — the head; gṛṇanti — they declare; daṁṣṭrāḥ — fangs; yamaḥ — Yama; sneha-kalāḥ — portions or traces of affection; dvijāni — the teeth; hāsaḥ — the smile; jana-unmāda-karī — causing bewilderment or madness among people; ca — and; māyā — Māyā; duranta-sargaḥ — the boundless creation; yat-apāṅga-mokṣaḥ — the release or casting of whose sidelong glance.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

छन्दांसीति । छन्दांसि छन्दःप्रधानाः वेदाः । शिरो ब्रह्मरन्ध्रम् । स्नेहकलाः पुत्रादिस्नेहलेशाः । द्विजानि दन्ताः । क्लीबत्वमार्षम् । दुरन्तोऽपारः सर्गः इति यत् स तस्य कटाक्षः । निखिलप्रपञ्चसर्गस्यापि भगवत्कटाक्षविक्षेपाधीनत्वात् ॥ ३१ ॥

chandāṁsīti | chandāṁsi chandaḥ-pradhānā vedāḥ | śiro brahma-randhram | sneha-kalāḥ putrādi-sneha-leśāḥ | dvijāni dantāḥ | klībatvam ārṣam | duranto ’pāraḥ sarga iti yat sa tasya kaṭākṣaḥ | nikhila-prapañca-sargasyāpi bhagavat-kaṭākṣa-vikṣepādhīnatvāt ||31||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. chandāṁsi — the metres; chandaḥ-pradhānā vedāḥ — the Vedas, in which metre is prominent; śiraḥ — the head; brahma-randhram — the aperture at the crown; sneha-kalāḥ — portions of affection; putrādi-sneha-leśāḥ — traces of affection for children and the like; dvijāni — the “twice-born”; dantāḥ — the teeth; klībatvam ārṣam — the neuter usage is an archaic/ṛṣi usage; durantaḥ apāraḥ sargaḥ — creation without end or limit; sa tasya kaṭākṣaḥ — that is His sidelong glance; nikhila-prapañca-sargasya api — even the creation of the whole phenomenal cosmos; bhagavat-kaṭākṣa-vikṣepa-adhīnatvāt — because it depends upon the casting of Bhagavān’s glance.

Witness note. The TTD recension gives the fuller opening and closing explanation printed here. The Chaukhamba/GGM transcription has the same core gloss and reads ṣaṇḍhatvam ārṣam where TTD has klībatvam ārṣam.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The chandas are the Vedas, and the head is specifically the brahma-randhra. “Portions of affection” means the small attachments directed toward children and others, and the “twice-born” are the teeth. The cosmic creation, limitless in extent, is identified with the Lord’s sidelong glance because the arising of the entire phenomenal cosmos depends upon that glance.

ŚB 2.1.32

व्रीडोत्तरौष्ठोऽधर एव लोभो
धर्म: स्तनोऽधर्मपथोऽस्य पृष्ठम् ।
कस्तस्य मेढ्रं वृषणौ च मित्रौ
कुक्षि: समुद्रा गिरयोऽस्थिसङ्घा: ॥ ३२ ॥
vrīḍottarauṣṭho ’dhara eva lobho
dharmaḥ stano ’dharma-patho ’sya pṛṣṭham
kas tasya meḍhraṁ vṛṣaṇau ca mitrau
kukṣiḥ samudrā girayo ’sthi-saṅghāḥ
Modesty is His upper lip and greed His lower lip; dharma is His breast, while the path of adharma is His back. Prajāpati is His generative organ, Mitra and Varuṇa His two testicles; the oceans are His abdomen and the mountains His masses of bone.
Word-for-word

vrīḍā — modesty; uttara-oṣṭhaḥ — the upper lip; adharaḥ — the lower lip; eva — indeed; lobhaḥ — greed; dharmaḥ — dharma; stanaḥ — the breast; adharma-pathaḥ — the path of adharma; asya — His; pṛṣṭham — back; kaḥ — Prajāpati; tasya — His; meḍhram — generative organ; vṛṣaṇau — the two testicles; ca — and; mitrau — Mitra and Varuṇa; kukṣiḥ — abdomen or flank; samudrāḥ — the oceans; girayaḥ — the mountains; asthi-saṅghāḥ — masses of bone.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

व्रीडेति । अधर्ममार्गोऽस्य पृष्ठभागः । कः प्रजापतिः । मित्रौ मित्रावरुणौ ॥ ३२ ॥

vrīḍeti | adharma-mārgo ’sya pṛṣṭha-bhāgaḥ | kaḥ prajāpatiḥ | mitrau mitrā-varuṇau ||32||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. vrīḍā iti — on the verse beginning with modesty; adharma-mārgaḥ — the path of adharma; asya pṛṣṭha-bhāgaḥ — is the rear portion of His body; kaḥ — the word ka; prajāpatiḥ — means Prajāpati; mitrau — the two called Mitra; mitrā-varuṇau — are Mitra and Varuṇa.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara identifies the “path of adharma” with the back of the cosmic Person. The word ka denotes Prajāpati, and the dual mitrau refers to Mitra and Varuṇa.

ŚB 2.1.33

नद्योऽस्य नाड्योऽथ तनूरुहाणि
महीरुहा विश्वतनोर्नृपेन्द्र ।
अनन्तवीर्य: श्वसितं मातरिश्वा
गतिर्वय: कर्म गुणप्रवाह: ॥ ३३ ॥
nadyo ’sya nāḍyo ’tha tanū-ruhāṇi
mahī-ruhā viśva-tanor nṛpendra
ananta-vīryaḥ śvasitaṁ mātariśvā
gatir vayaḥ karma guṇa-pravāhaḥ
O foremost of kings, the rivers are the veins of the universal body and the trees its bodily hairs. Mātariśvan, of immeasurable power, is His breath; time is His movement, and the flowing activity of the guṇas is His play.
Word-for-word

nadyaḥ — rivers; asya — His; nāḍyaḥ — veins or channels; atha — and; tanū-ruhāṇi — bodily hairs; mahī-ruhāḥ — trees, those growing from the earth; viśva-tanoḥ — of the universal body; nṛpa-indra — O foremost of kings; ananta-vīryaḥ — of unlimited power; śvasitam — breath; mātariśvā — Mātariśvan, the wind; gatiḥ — movement; vayaḥ — time or age; karma — activity; guṇa-pravāhaḥ — the stream of the guṇas.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

नद्य इति । अनन्तं वीर्यं यस्य सः । वयः कालस्तस्य गमनम् । गुणप्रवाहः प्राणिनां संसारः । कर्म तस्य क्रीडा ॥ ३३ ॥

nadya iti | anantaṁ vīryaṁ yasya saḥ | vayaḥ kālas tasya gamanam | guṇa-pravāhaḥ prāṇināṁ saṁsāraḥ | karma tasya krīḍā ||33||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. anantam vīryam yasya saḥ — He whose power is unlimited; vayaḥ — age/time; kālaḥ — time; tasya gamanam — is His movement; guṇa-pravāhaḥ — the flow of the guṇas; prāṇinām saṁsāraḥ — the saṁsāric course of living beings; karma — activity; tasya krīḍā — is His play.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Unlimited in power” means precisely one whose potency has no limit. Time is the movement of the cosmic Person. The stream of the guṇas is the saṁsāric process experienced by living beings, and this cosmic activity is His play.

ŚB 2.1.34

ईशस्य केशान् विदुरम्बुवाहान्
वासस्तु सन्ध्यां कुरुवर्य भूम्न: ।
अव्यक्तमाहुर्हृदयं मनश्च
स चन्द्रमा: सर्वविकारकोश: ॥ ३४ ॥
īśasya keśān vidur ambuvāhān
vāsas tu sandhyāṁ kuru-varya bhūmnaḥ
avyaktam āhur hṛdayaṁ manaś ca
sa candramāḥ sarva-vikāra-kośaḥ
O best of the Kurus, the rain-bearing clouds are known as the Lord’s hair, and twilight as the garment of the all-pervading One. The unmanifest principle is said to be His heart, while the moon—repository of manifold change—is His mind.
Word-for-word

īśasya — of the Lord; keśān — hairs of the head; viduḥ — they know; ambu-vāhān — water-bearing clouds; vāsaḥ — garment; tu — while; sandhyām — twilight; kuru-varya — O best of the Kurus; bhūmnaḥ — of the vast, all-pervading One; avyaktam — the unmanifest principle; āhuḥ — they say; hṛdayam — heart; manaḥ — mind; ca — and; saḥ candramāḥ — that moon; sarva-vikāra-kośaḥ — repository of all transformations.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ईशस्येति । भूम्नो विभोः । अव्यक्तं प्रधानम् हृदयमाहुः । स प्रसिद्धश्चन्द्रमाः तदीयं मनः । सर्वेषां विकाराणां कामादीनां कोश इवाश्रयभूतम् । चैन्द्रोऽपि सर्वविकारकोश उच्यते धूमादिमार्गत्वात् ॥ ३४ ॥

īśasyeti | bhūmno vibhoḥ | avyaktaṁ pradhānaṁ hṛdayam āhuḥ | sa prasiddhaś candramās tadīyaṁ manaḥ | sarveṣāṁ vikārāṇāṁ kāmādīnāṁ kośa ivāśraya-bhūtam | caindro ’pi sarva-vikāra-kośa ucyate dhūmādi-mārgatvāt ||34||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. bhūmnaḥ — of the vast One; vibhoḥ — of the all-pervading Lord; avyaktam — the unmanifest; pradhānam — primordial nature; hṛdayam āhuḥ — they call His heart; sa prasiddhaḥ candramāḥ — that well-known moon; tadīyam manaḥ — is His mind; sarveṣām vikārāṇām — of all transformations; kāma-ādīnām — beginning with desire; kośa iva — like a repository; āśraya-bhūtam — serving as their support; caindraḥ api — the lunar realm as well; dhūmādi-mārgatvāt — because it belongs to the path beginning with smoke.

Witness note. The TTD recension preserves the final explanation concerning the lunar realm and the path beginning with smoke; the Chaukhamba/GGM transcription ends after identifying the moon as the support of the transformations.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Bhūman” is the all-pervading Lord. The unmanifest pradhāna is His heart; the moon is His mind, understood as a repository and support for transformations such as desire. The fuller TTD reading also connects the lunar sphere with the path beginning in smoke.

ŚB 2.1.35

विज्ञानशक्तिं महिमामनन्ति
सर्वात्मनोऽन्त:करणं गिरित्रम् ।
अश्वाश्वतर्युष्ट्रगजा नखानि
सर्वे मृगा: पशव: श्रोणिदेशे ॥ ३५ ॥
vijñāna-śaktiṁ mahim āmananti
sarvātmano ’ntaḥ-karaṇaṁ giritram
aśvāśvatary-uṣṭra-gajā nakhāni
sarve mṛgāḥ paśavaḥ śroṇi-deśe
They identify the mahat principle with the cognitive potency of the Self of all, and Girītra, Śrī Rudra, with His inner organ. Horses, mules, camels, and elephants are His nails, while all wild and domestic animals occupy the region of His hips.
Word-for-word

vijñāna-śaktim — cognitive or knowing power; mahim — the mahat principle; āmananti — they declare; sarva-ātmanaḥ — of the Self of all; antaḥ-karaṇam — inner organ; giritram — Girītra, Rudra; aśva — horses; aśvatarī — mules; uṣṭra — camels; gajāḥ — elephants; nakhāni — nails; sarve — all; mṛgāḥ — wild animals; paśavaḥ — domestic animals; śroṇi-deśe — in the hip-region.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

विज्ञानशक्तिमिति । विज्ञानशक्तिं ज्ञानरूपां भगवच्छक्तिं चित्तम् । महिं महत्तत्त्वम् । अन्तःकरणमहङ्कारम् । गिरित्रं श्रीरुद्रम् । गर्दभाद्वडवायामुत्पन्ना अश्वतरी ॥ ३५ ॥

vijñāna-śaktim iti | vijñāna-śaktiṁ jñāna-rūpāṁ bhagavac-chaktiṁ cittam | mahiṁ mahat-tattvam | antaḥ-karaṇam ahaṅkāram | giritraṁ śrī-rudram | gardabhād vaḍavāyām utpannā aśvatarī ||35||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. vijñāna-śaktim — cognitive potency; jñāna-rūpām bhagavat-śaktim — Bhagavān’s potency whose nature is knowledge; cittam — consciousness/mind-stuff; mahim — the word mahim; mahat-tattvam — denotes the mahat principle; antaḥ-karaṇam — the inner organ; ahaṅkāram — ego-principle; giritram — Girītra; śrī-rudram — Śrī Rudra; gardabhāt — from a donkey; vaḍavāyām — in a mare; utpannā — born; aśvatarī — a mule.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Śrīdhara glosses the cognitive potency as the consciousness that is Bhagavān’s knowledge-form potency, identifies mahim with the mahat-tattva, and takes the inner organ here as ahaṅkāra. Girītra is Śrī Rudra. He also explains aśvatarī zoologically as the offspring produced from a donkey and a mare.

ŚB 2.1.36

वयांसि तद्व्याकरणं विचित्रं
मनुर्मनीषा मनुजो निवास: ।
गन्धर्वविद्याधरचारणाप्सर:
स्वरस्मृतीरसुरानीकवीर्य: ॥ ३६ ॥
vayāṁsi tad-vyākaraṇaṁ vicitraṁ
manur manīṣā manujo nivāsaḥ
gandharva-vidyādhara-cāraṇāpsaraḥ
svara-smṛtīr asurānīka-vīryaḥ
The birds display His variegated power of differentiation; Manu is His reflective intelligence, and humankind His dwelling. Gandharvas, Vidyādharas, Cāraṇas, and Apsarases embody His tones and musical memory, while the hosts of asuras manifest His prowess.
Word-for-word

vayāṁsi — birds; tat-vyākaraṇam — His differentiation, articulation, or expression; vicitram — variegated; manuḥ — Manu; manīṣā — reflective intelligence; manujaḥ — the human being, humankind; nivāsaḥ — dwelling; gandharva — Gandharvas; vidyādhara — Vidyādharas; cāraṇa — Cāraṇas; apsaraḥ — Apsarases; svara-smṛtīḥ — tones and their remembered patterns; asura-anīka-vīryaḥ — whose prowess is represented by the hosts of asuras.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

वयांसीति । वयांसि पक्षिणस्तस्य व्याकरणं “नामरूपे व्याकरवाणि” इति श्रुत्युक्तं शिल्पनैपुण्यम् । यथाऽऽहुः—“येन शुक्लीकृता हंसाः शुकाश्च हरितीकृताः । मयूराश्चित्रिता येन स ते वृत्तिं विधास्यति” इति । मनुः स्वायम्भुवः । मनीषा बुद्धिः । मनुजः पुरुषः, निवास आश्रयः । “पुरुषत्वे चाविस्तरामात्मा” इति श्रुतेः । गन्धर्वादीनां द्वन्द्वैक्यम् । गन्धर्वादयः स्वरस्मृतीः षड्जादिस्वरस्मृतयः इत्यर्थः । असुरानीकं वीर्यं यस्य सः । “स्वरः स्मृतिरसुरानीकवर्यः” इति पाठे गन्धर्वादयः स्वरः, असुरसमूहश्रेष्ठः प्रह्लादः स्मृतिः तस्येति ॥ ३६ ॥

vayāṁsīti | vayāṁsi pakṣiṇas tasya vyākaraṇaṁ “nāma-rūpe vyākaravāṇi” iti śruty-uktaṁ śilpa-naipuṇyam | yathāhuḥ—“yena śuklīkṛtā haṁsāḥ śukāś ca haritīkṛtāḥ | mayūrāś citritā yena sa te vṛttiṁ vidhāsyati” iti | manuḥ svāyambhuvaḥ | manīṣā buddhiḥ | manujaḥ puruṣaḥ, nivāsa āśrayaḥ | “puruṣatve cāvistaram ātmā” iti śruteḥ | gandharvādīnāṁ dvandvaikyam | gandharvādayaḥ svara-smṛtīḥ ṣaḍjādi-svara-smṛtaya ity arthaḥ | asurānīkaṁ vīryaṁ yasya saḥ | “svaraḥ smṛtir asurānīka-varyaḥ” iti pāṭhe gandharvādayaḥ svaraḥ, asura-samūha-śreṣṭhaḥ prahlādaḥ smṛtiḥ tasyeti ||36||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. vayāṁsi — birds; pakṣiṇaḥ — winged creatures; tasya vyākaraṇam — His differentiation or articulation; nāma-rūpe vyākaravāṇi — “I shall differentiate name and form”; śruti-uktam — spoken in Śruti; śilpa-naipuṇyam — artistic skill; manuḥ svāyambhuvaḥ — Manu is Svāyambhuva Manu; manīṣā buddhiḥ — manīṣā means intelligence; manujaḥ puruṣaḥ — manuja means the human person; nivāsa āśrayaḥ — dwelling means support or abode; gandharvādīnām dvandvaikyam — the compound gathers Gandharvas and the following classes; ṣaḍja-ādi-svara-smṛtayaḥ — remembered tones beginning with Ṣaḍja; asurānīkam vīryam yasya — one whose prowess is the asura host; iti pāṭhe — in the alternate reading; asura-samūha-śreṣṭhaḥ prahlādaḥ — Prahlāda, best of the asura host; smṛtiḥ tasya — is His remembrance.

Witness note. Both principal witnesses preserve the alternate-reading discussion. The TTD edition additionally records manuscript omissions and additions in its apparatus around this verse.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Birds exemplify the Lord’s artistic skill in differentiating name and form. Manu is Svāyambhuva Manu; manīṣā is intelligence, and the human person is the dwelling or support. The celestial singers embody remembered musical tones beginning with Ṣaḍja. Śrīdhara also records an alternate reading in which Prahlāda, foremost among the asuras, is connected with divine remembrance.

ŚB 2.1.37

ब्रह्माननं क्षत्रभुजो महात्मा
विडूरुरङ्‌घ्रिश्रितकृष्णवर्ण: ।
नानाभिधाभीज्यगणोपपन्नो
द्रव्यात्मक: कर्म वितानयोग: ॥ ३७ ॥
brahmānanaṁ kṣatra-bhujo mahātmā
viḍ ūrur aṅghri-śrita-kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ
nānābhidhābhījya-gaṇopapanno
dravyātmakaḥ karma vitāna-yogaḥ
The brāhmaṇa is the face of the Great Person, the kṣatriya His arms, the vaiśya His thighs, and the dark-hued śūdra takes shelter at His feet. The many worshipable deities named in sacrifice are included in Him, and sacrificial action performed with its materials is His prescribed worship.
Word-for-word

brahma — the brāhmaṇa; ānanam — face; kṣatra — the kṣatriya; bhujaḥ — arms; mahātmā — the Great Person; viṭ — the vaiśya; ūruḥ — thighs; aṅghri-śrita — sheltered at the feet; kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ — the dark-hued class, the śūdra; nānā-abhidhā — bearing various names; abhījya-gaṇa — groups of worshipable deities; upapannaḥ — endowed or accompanied; dravya-ātmakaḥ — consisting of sacrificial materials; karma — ritual action; vitāna-yogaḥ — the performance or arrangement of sacrifice.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

ब्रह्मेति । ब्रह्मा विप्रः, तस्य आननं मुखम् । क्षत्रं क्षत्रियो भुजौ यस्य । विट् वैश्यः ऊरू यस्य । अङ्घ्रिश्रितः कृष्णवर्णः शूद्रो यस्य । नानाभिधाः नामानि येषां ते च तेऽभीज्या देवाः, तेषां गणैर्वसुरुद्रादिभिरुपपन्नो युक्तः । द्रव्यात्मको हविःसाध्यः वितानयोगो यज्ञप्रयोगः तस्य कर्म कार्यम् । आवश्यकोऽभिप्रेत इत्यर्थः ॥ ३७ ॥

brahmeti | brahmā vipraḥ, tasya ānanaṁ mukham | kṣatraṁ kṣatriyo bhujau yasya | viṭ vaiśyaḥ ūrū yasya | aṅghri-śritaḥ kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ śūdro yasya | nānābhidhāḥ nāmāni yeṣāṁ te ca te ’bhījyā devāḥ, teṣāṁ gaṇair vasu-rudrādibhir upapanno yuktaḥ | dravyātmako haviḥ-sādhyaḥ vitāna-yogo yajña-prayogaḥ tasya karma kāryam | āvaśyako ’bhipreta ity arthaḥ ||37||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. brahmā — the brāhmaṇa; vipraḥ — learned brāhmaṇa; tasya ānanam mukham — is His face; kṣatram kṣatriyaḥ — kṣatra means the kṣatriya; bhujau yasya — whose two arms they are; viṭ vaiśyaḥ — viṭ means the vaiśya; ūrū yasya — whose thighs they are; aṅghri-śritaḥ — taking shelter of the feet; kṛṣṇa-varṇaḥ śūdraḥ — the dark-hued class is the śūdra; nānā-abhidhāḥ — having many names; abhījyā devāḥ — deities to be worshiped in sacrifice; vasu-rudra-ādibhiḥ — by groups such as the Vasus and Rudras; upapannaḥ yuktaḥ — endowed or accompanied; dravya-ātmakaḥ — consisting of materials; haviḥ-sādhyaḥ — accomplished through oblations; vitāna-yogaḥ — sacrificial performance; tasya karma kāryam — is the action to be done for Him; āvaśyakaḥ abhipretaḥ — intended as obligatory/necessary.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The social orders are contemplated as limbs of the Great Person: brāhmaṇa as face, kṣatriya as arms, vaiśya as thighs, and śūdra at the feet. The many deities invoked under different names—such as the Vasus and Rudras—are included within Him. Sacrifice performed through offerings and proper materials is therefore action directed toward Him.

ŚB 2.1.38

इयानसावीश्वरविग्रहस्य
य: सन्निवेष: कथितो मया ते ।
सन्धार्यतेऽस्मिन् वपुषि स्थविष्ठे
मन: स्वबुद्ध्या न यतोऽस्ति किञ्चित् ॥ ३८ ॥
iyān asāv īśvara-vigrahasya
yaḥ sanniveśaḥ kathito mayā te
sandhāryate ’smin vapuṣi sthaviṣṭhe
manaḥ sva-buddhyā na yato ’sti kiñcit
