Such is the arrangement of the Lord’s form that I have described to you. With one’s own discernment the mind is to be held upon this exceedingly gross universal body, for there is nothing whatever that lies outside it.
Word-for-word

iyān — of this extent; asau — that; īśvara-vigrahasya — of the Lord’s form; yaḥ — which; sanniveśaḥ — arrangement or disposition of parts; kathitaḥ — described; mayā — by me; te — to you; sandhāryate — is held in concentration; asmin — in this; vapuṣi — body; sthaviṣṭhe — exceedingly gross; manaḥ — the mind; sva-buddhyā — by one’s own discernment; na — not; yataḥ — apart from which; asti — exists; kiñcit — anything.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

इयानिति । इयानेतावान् सन्निवेशोऽवयवसंस्थानम् । अस्मिन् स्वबुद्ध्या मनः सन्धार्यते मुमुक्षुभिः, यतो व्यतिरिक्तं किञ्चिन्नास्ति तस्मिन् ॥ ३८ ॥

iyān iti | iyān etāvān sanniveśo ’vayava-saṁsthānam | asmin sva-buddhyā manaḥ sandhāryate mumukṣubhiḥ, yato vyatiriktaṁ kiñcin nāsti tasmin ||38||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. iyān — this much, of this extent; etāvān — so extensive; sanniveśaḥ — arrangement; avayava-saṁsthānam — disposition of the limbs; asmin — in this form; sva-buddhyā — by one’s own intelligence; manaḥ sandhāryate — the mind is held in concentration; mumukṣubhiḥ — by seekers of liberation; yataḥ — because; vyatiriktam — separate from it; kiñcit na asti — nothing exists; tasmin — in relation to that all-inclusive form.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The “arrangement” is the disposition of the limbs just described. Seekers of liberation hold the mind upon this all-inclusive form through their own discriminating intelligence, since nothing that can appear to the mind exists outside the cosmic body used for this meditation.

ŚB 2.1.39

स सर्वधीवृत्त्यनुभूतसर्व
आत्मा यथा स्वप्नजनेक्षितैक: ।
तं सत्यमानन्दनिधिं भजेत
नान्यत्र सज्जेद् यत आत्मपात: ॥ ३९ ॥
sa sarva-dhī-vṛtty-anubhūta-sarva
ātmā yathā svapna-janekṣitaikaḥ
taṁ satyam ānanda-nidhiṁ bhajeta
nānyatra sajjed yata ātma-pātaḥ
He is the one inner Self by whom everything disclosed through every mode of cognition is experienced, just as one dreamer is the seer of the many persons appearing in a dream. One should worship Him, the Reality and treasury of bliss, and attach oneself nowhere else, for such attachment brings the self down into saṁsāra.
Word-for-word

saḥ — He; sarva-dhī-vṛtti — every mode or operation of cognition; anubhūta-sarvaḥ — by whom all is experienced; ātmā — the inner Self; yathā — just as; svapna-jana — persons appearing in a dream; īkṣitā — the seer; ekaḥ — one; tam — Him; satyam — the Reality; ānanda-nidhim — treasury or abode of bliss; bhajeta — one should worship; na anyatra — not elsewhere; sajjet — become attached; yataḥ — because from which; ātma-pātaḥ — fall or degradation of the self.

Śrīdhara Sanskrit

तदेवं चित्तस्थैर्यार्थं विराड्देहजीवेश्वराणामभेदेनोपासनमुक्तम् । तत्र तु देहजीवावीश्वरे प्रविलाप्य स एव ध्येय इति सदृष्टान्तं निर्धारयति—स इति । सर्वेषां धीवृत्तिभिरनुभूतं सर्वं येन, स एक एव सर्वान्तर आत्मा, तमेव सत्यं भजेत । अन्यत्रोपलक्षणे न सज्जेत । यत आसङ्गादात्मनः पातः संसारो भवति । एकस्य तत्तदिन्द्रियैः सर्वानुभूतौ दृष्टान्तः—स्वप्ने जनानामीक्षिता यथेति । स्वप्ने हि कदाचिद् बहून् देहान् प्रकल्प्य जीवस्तत्तदिन्द्रियैः सर्वं पश्यति तद्वत् । ईश्वरस्य तु विद्याशक्तित्वान्न बन्धः ॥ ३९ ॥

tad evaṁ citta-sthairyārthaṁ virāḍ-deha-jīveśvarāṇām abhedenopāsanam uktam | tatra tu deha-jīvāv īśvare pravilāpya sa eva dhyeya iti sa-dṛṣṭāntaṁ nirdhārayati—sa iti | sarveṣāṁ dhī-vṛttibhir anubhūtaṁ sarvaṁ yena, sa eka eva sarvāntara ātmā, tam eva satyaṁ bhajeta | anyatropalakṣaṇe na sajjeta | yata āsaṅgād ātmanaḥ pātaḥ saṁsāro bhavati | ekasya tat-tad-indriyaiḥ sarvānubhūtau dṛṣṭāntaḥ—svapne janānām īkṣitā yatheti | svapne hi kadācid bahūn dehān prakalpya jīvas tat-tad-indriyaiḥ sarvaṁ paśyati tadvat | īśvarasya tu vidyā-śaktitvān na bandhaḥ ||39||

Śrīdhara word-for-word. tad evam — thus, in this way; citta-sthairya-artham — for the sake of steadiness of mind; virāḍ-deha-jīveśvarāṇām — of the virāṭ body, its living principle, and the Lord; abhedena upāsanam uktam — meditation through contemplated non-difference has been taught; deha-jīvau īśvare pravilāpya — dissolving the body and individual living principle into Īśvara; sa eva dhyeyaḥ — He alone is to be meditated upon; sa-dṛṣṭāntam nirdhārayati — he establishes this with an example; sarveṣām dhī-vṛttibhiḥ — through all modes of cognition; anubhūtam sarvam yena — by whom all is experienced; sa eka eva — He alone is the one; sarva-antara ātmā — inner Self of all; tam eva satyam bhajeta — one should worship Him alone as the Reality; anyatra upalakṣaṇe na sajjeta — one should not attach oneself to the merely representative forms elsewhere; āsaṅgāt — from attachment; ātmanaḥ pātaḥ — the self’s fall; saṁsāraḥ bhavati — becomes saṁsāra; svapne — in a dream; bahūn dehān prakalpya — projecting many bodies; jīvaḥ — the individual living being; tat-tad-indriyaiḥ sarvam paśyati — sees everything through their respective senses; tadvat — likewise; īśvarasya tu — but for Īśvara; vidyā-śaktitvāt — because He possesses/is the power of knowledge; na bandhaḥ — there is no bondage.

Witness note. This reading agrees closely between the Chaukhamba/GGM transcription and the TTD printed Bhāvārtha-dīpikā. Jīva Gosvāmī’s Bhakti-sandarbha independently quotes the central Śrīdhara passage from this verse, providing a third corroboration of the reading.

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The virāṭ meditation was taught to steady the mind by contemplating the cosmic body, its living principle, and its Lord in non-separation. Śrīdhara now brings that provisional meditation to its conclusion: the body and jīva are to be resolved into Īśvara, and He alone is the ultimate object of meditation—the one inner Self through whom every cognition is possible. The dream analogy shows how one conscious subject may experience many projected bodies and their perceptions; unlike the finite dreamer, however, Īśvara is never bound, because His knowing power is intrinsic. Attachment to anything treated as independent of Him leads again to saṁsāra.

2.2 The Lord in the Heart

ŚB 2.2.1

श्रीशुक उवाच ।
एवं पुरा धारणयात्मयोनि-
र्नष्टां स्मृतिं प्रत्यवरुध्य तुष्टात् ।
तथा ससर्जेदममोघदृष्टि-
र्यथाप्ययात् प्राग् व्यवसायबुद्धिः ॥ १ ॥
Śrī Śuka said: Formerly, by this concentration, the self-born Brahmā recovered the memory lost at dissolution from the pleased Lord; possessing unfailing vision and resolute intelligence, he then created this universe as it had been before the dissolution.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śuka said; evam — thus, in this way; purā — formerly; dhāraṇayā — by concentration; ātma-yoniḥ — the self-born one, Brahmā; naṣṭām — lost; smṛtim — memory; pratyavarudhya — recovering, obtaining again; tuṣṭāt — from the pleased one; tathā — in that manner; sasarja — created; idam — this universe; amogha-dṛṣṭiḥ — one whose vision is unfailing; yathā — as; apyayāt — before dissolution; prāk — previously; vyavasāya-buddhiḥ — one whose intelligence is resolute.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dvitīye — in the second chapter; sthūla-dhāraṇātaḥ — from the gross concentration; jitam manaḥ — the conquered mind; sarva-sākṣiṇi — in the witness of all; sarveśe — in the Lord of all; viṣṇau — in Viṣṇu; dhāryam — is to be fixed; dṛśya-ālambana-rūpā — having the visible as support; vairāja-dhāraṇā — Virāṭ concentration; iha ucyate — here is stated; tat-sādhyā — accomplished by that; sarvāntaryāmi-dhāraṇā — concentration on the indwelling Lord of all; pūrvokta-dhāraṇāyāḥ — of the previously stated concentration; avāntara-phalam — subsidiary fruit; tasyā dhāraṇayā — by that concentration; tuṣṭāt hareḥ — from pleased Hari; pralaya-samaye — at the time of dissolution; naṣṭām sṛṣṭi-smṛtim — lost memory of creation; pratyavarudhya labdhvā — recovering, obtaining; brahmā — Brahmā; apyayāt prāk — as before dissolution; idam viśvam — this universe; yathā āsīt tathā sasarja — created just as it had been; vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ — intelligence of firm determination; amoghā dṛṣṭiḥ — unfailing vision; viśva-sṛṣṭi-sāmarthyam — capacity to create the universe.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śrī-śuka uvāca
evaṁ purā dhāraṇayātma-yonir
naṣṭāṁ smṛtiṁ pratyavarudhya tuṣṭāt
tathā sasarjedam amogha-dṛṣṭir
yathāpyayāt prāg vyavasāya-buddhiḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
dvitīye tu tataḥ sthūla-dhāraṇāto jitaṁ manaḥ | sarva-sākṣiṇi sarveśe viṣṇau dhāryam itīryate || dṛśyālambana-rūpaivam uktā vairāja-dhāraṇā | ihocyate tu tat-sādhyā sarvāntaryāmi-dhāraṇā || tatra tāvat pūrvokta-dhāraṇāyā avāntara-phalam āha—evaṁ yā dhāraṇā, tayā tuṣṭād dhareḥ purā pralaya-samaye naṣṭāṁ sṛṣṭi-smṛtiṁ pratyavarudhya labdhvā, brahmā apyayāt prāg idaṁ viśvaṁ yathā āsīt tathā sasarja, vyavasāyātmikā buddhir yasya saḥ | ata eva amoghā dṛṣṭir yasyeti | atas tayā dhāraṇayā viśva-sṛṣṭi-sāmarthyaṁ bhavati || 1 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
द्वितीये तु ततः स्थूलधारणातो जितं मनः । सर्वसाक्षिणि सर्वेशे विष्णौ धार्यमितीर्यते ॥

दृश्यालम्बनरूपैवमुक्ता वैराजधारणा । इहोच्यते तु तत्साध्या सर्वान्तर्यामिधारणा ॥

तत्र तावत्पूर्वोक्तधारणाया अवान्तरफलमाह—एवं या धारणा, तया तुष्टाद्धरेः पुरा प्रलयसमये नष्टां सृष्टिस्मृतिं प्रत्यवरुध्य लब्ध्वा, ब्रह्मा अप्ययात्प्रागिदं विश्वं यथा आसीत्तथा ससर्ज, व्यवसायात्मिका बुद्धिर्यस्य सः । अत एव अमोघा दृष्टिर्यस्येति । अतस्तया धारणया विश्वसृष्टिसामर्थ्यं भवति ॥ १ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In the second chapter it is stated that the mind, conquered by the preceding gross concentration, is to be fixed upon Viṣṇu, the witness of all and Lord of all. Thus the Virāṭ concentration, whose support is the visible, has been stated; here is stated the concentration upon the indwelling Lord of all that is accomplished by it. First he states a subsidiary fruit of the previously described concentration. By that concentration Brahmā, having recovered from the pleased Hari the memory of creation that had been lost at the time of dissolution, created this universe just as it had existed before dissolution. “Vyavasāya-buddhi” means one whose intelligence is of the nature of firm determination. Therefore his vision was unfailing. Thus, by that concentration, the capacity to create the universe arises.

ŚB 2.2.2

शाब्दस्य हि ब्रह्मण एष पन्था
यन्नामभिर्ध्यायति धीरपार्थैः ।
परिभ्रमंस्तत्र न विन्दतेऽर्थान्
मायामये वासनया शयानः ॥ २ ॥
Indeed, this is the path of the verbal Brahman, the Veda: the intelligence meditates through names that are without substantial purpose. Wandering there in what is made of māyā, lying as though asleep through desire, one does not obtain the realities sought.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
śābdasya — of that consisting of sound; hi — indeed; brahmaṇaḥ — of Brahman, the Veda; eṣaḥ — this; panthāḥ — path; yat — in that, whereby; nāmabhiḥ — by names; dhyāyati — meditates, thinks; dhīḥ — intelligence; apārthaiḥ — without real purpose, devoid of substance; paribhraman — wandering; tatra — there; na — not; vindate — obtains; arthān — objects, realities, ends; māyā-maye — in that made of māyā; vāsanayā — through desire, latent inclination; śayānaḥ — lying, as if asleep.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
upāsanā-phalāt api — even from the fruit of worship; viraktasya — of one detached; śuddha-ātma-dhāraṇāyām — for concentration upon the pure Self; adhikāraḥ — qualification; vairāgya-artham — for the sake of detachment; sarvam karma-phalam nindati — he censures every fruit of action; śābdam — verbal; śabda-mayam — consisting of sound; brahma — Brahman; vedaḥ — Veda; tasya eṣaḥ panthāḥ — this is its path; karma-phala-bodha-prakāraḥ — manner of teaching action’s fruits; apārthaiḥ — devoid of substance; artha-śūnyaiḥ — empty of real value; svargādi-nāmabhiḥ — by names such as heaven; sādhakasya dhīḥ — practitioner’s intelligence; dhyāyati — thinks upon; tat-tat-icchām karoti — forms desire for each; māyā-maye pathi — on the path made of māyā; sukham iti vāsanayā — through the impression “there is happiness”; śayānaḥ — lying as asleep; svapnān paśyan iva — like seeing dreams; paribhraman — wandering; arthān na vindati — does not attain the realities; tat-tat-lokam prāptaḥ api — even reaching those worlds; niravadyam sukham na labhate — does not gain faultless happiness.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
śābdasya hi brahmaṇa eṣa panthā
yan nāmabhir dhyāyati dhīr apārthaiḥ
paribhramaṁs tatra na vindate ’rthān
māyāmaye vāsanayā śayānaḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
upāsanā-phalād api viraktasya śuddhātma-dhāraṇāyām adhikāraḥ | ato vairāgyārthaṁ sarvaṁ karma-phalaṁ nindati | śābdaṁ śabda-mayaṁ brahma vedaḥ | tasyaiṣa panthāḥ karma-phala-bodha-prakāraḥ | ko ’sau ? apārthair artha-śūnyair eva svargādi-nāmabhiḥ sādhakasya dhīr dhyāyati tat-tad-icchāṁ karotīti yat | apārthatvam evāha—tatra māyā-maye pathi sukham iti vāsanayā śayānaḥ svapnān paśyann iva paribhramann arthān na vindati | tat-tal-lokaṁ prāpto ’pi niravadyaṁ sukhaṁ na labhata ity arthaḥ || 2 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उपासनाफलादपि विरक्तस्य शुद्धात्मधारणायामधिकारः । अतो वैराग्यार्थं सर्वं कर्मफलं निन्दति । शाब्दं शब्दमयं ब्रह्म वेदः । तस्यैष पन्थाः कर्मफलबोधप्रकारः । कोऽसौ ? अपार्थैरर्थशून्यैरेव स्वर्गादिनामभिः साधकस्य धीर्ध्यायति तत्तदिच्छां करोतीति यत् । अपार्थत्वमेवाह—तत्र मायामये पथि सुखमिति वासनया शयानः स्वप्नान्पश्यन्निव परिभ्रमन्नर्थान्न विन्दति । तत्तल्लोकं प्राप्तोऽपि निरवद्यं सुखं न लभत इत्यर्थः ॥ २ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. One who is detached even from the fruit of worship is qualified for concentration upon the pure Self. Therefore, for the sake of detachment, he censures every fruit of ritual action. “Verbal Brahman” is the Veda consisting of sound. This is its path, its manner of teaching the fruits of action. What is that? The practitioner’s intelligence meditates through mere names such as heaven that are devoid of real substance; that is, it forms desires for each of them. He states their lack of substance: wandering on that path made of māyā, lying as though asleep through the impression “there is happiness there,” like one seeing dreams, he does not obtain the objects. The meaning is that even after attaining those respective worlds he does not obtain faultless happiness.

ŚB 2.2.3

अतः कविर्नामसु यावदर्थः
स्यादप्रमत्तो व्यवसायबुद्धिः ।
सिद्धेऽन्यथार्थे न यतेत तत्र
परिश्रमं तत्र समीक्षमाणः ॥ ३ ॥
Therefore the wise person should have only so much need among things that are names; he should be vigilant and resolute in intelligence. When the required object is obtained otherwise, he should not strive for it, seeing the labor involved in such striving.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ataḥ — therefore; kaviḥ — the wise, discerning person; nāmasu — among names, named objects; yāvat-arthaḥ — one whose requirement is only so much as needed; syāt — should be; apramattaḥ — vigilant, not heedless; vyavasāya-buddhiḥ — with resolute intelligence; siddhe — when accomplished, obtained; anyathā — otherwise, by another means; arthe — the required object; na — not; yateta — should strive; tatra — for it; pariśramam — labor, exertion; tatra — therein; samīkṣamāṇaḥ — seeing, considering.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sarvathā karma-phala-tyāge — if all fruits of action are completely abandoned; sadyaḥ eva deha-pātaḥ syāt — the body would immediately fall; iti āśaṅkya — anticipating this objection; kaviḥ — wise person; nāmasu — among mere names; nāma-mātreṣu bhogyeṣu — enjoyments that are names only; yāvat-arthena arthaḥ — whose need is only so much; deha-nirvāhaḥ — maintenance of the body; apramattaḥ — vigilant; tāvat-mātre api anāsaktaḥ — unattached even to that much; vyavasāya-buddhiḥ — resolute intelligence; na idam sukham iti niścayavān — certain “this is not happiness”; anyathā eva siddhe — if obtained otherwise; tatra yatne pariśramam paśyan — seeing exertion in that effort; na yateta — should not strive.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ataḥ kavir nāmasu yāvad arthaḥ
syād apramatto vyavasāya-buddhiḥ
siddhe ’nyathārthe na yateta tatra
pariśramaṁ tatra samīkṣamāṇaḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tarhi sarvathā karma-phala-tyāge sadya eva deha-pātaḥ syād ity āśaṅkyāha | ataḥ kavir nāmasu nāma-mātreṣu bhogyeṣu yāvad-arthenārtho deha-nirvāho yasya sa tathā-bhūtaḥ syāt | apramattas tāvan-mātre ’py anāsaktaḥ | vyavasāya-buddhir nedaṁ sukham iti niścayavān | tadāpi tatra tasminn arthe ’nyathaiva siddhe sati tatra yatne pariśramaṁ paśyan na yateta || 3 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तर्हि सर्वथा कर्मफलत्यागे सद्य एव देहपातः स्यादित्याशङ्क्याह । अतः कविर्नामसु नाममात्रेषु भोग्येषु यावदर्थेनार्थो देहनिर्वाहो यस्य स तथाभूतः स्यात् । अप्रमत्तस्तावन्मात्रेऽप्यनासक्तः । व्यवसायबुद्धिर्नेदं सुखमिति निश्चयवान् । तदापि तत्र तस्मिन्नर्थेऽन्यथैव सिद्धे सति तत्र यत्ने परिश्रमं पश्यन्न यतेत ॥ ३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Anticipating the objection, “If all fruits of action are completely abandoned, the body would immediately fall,” he says this. Therefore the wise person should be such that, among enjoyments which are mere names, his need extends only to what maintains the body. “Vigilant” means unattached even to that much. “Resolute in intelligence” means possessing the certainty, “This is not happiness.” Even then, if that required object is obtained in another way, seeing exertion in striving for it, he should not strive.

ŚB 2.2.4

सत्यां क्षितौ किं कशिपोः प्रयासै-
र्बाहौ स्वसिद्धे ह्युपबर्हणैः किम् ।
सत्यञ्जलौ किं पुरुधान्नपात्र्या
दिग्वल्कलादौ सति किं दुकूलैः ॥ ४ ॥
When the earth is available, what need is there for efforts for a bed? When one’s own arm is already available, what need is there for pillows? When the cupped hands are available, what need is there for many kinds of food-vessels? When the directions and bark and the like are available, what need is there for fine cloth?
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
satyām — being available; kṣitau — the earth; kim — what need; kaśipoḥ — for a bed; prayāsaiḥ — by efforts; bāhau — when the arm; sva-siddhe — naturally available, one’s own and ready; hi — indeed; upabarhaṇaiḥ — for pillows, head-rests; kim — what need; sati — being available; añjalau — the cupped hands; kim — what need; purudhā — of many kinds; anna-pātryā — for a food-vessel; dik — the directions; valkala-ādau — bark and the like; sati — being available; kim — what need; dukūlaiḥ — for fine garments.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
anyathā-siddhim āha — he states obtaining it otherwise; dvābhyām — in two verses; satyām iti — beginning “when available”; kaśipoḥ — of a bed; śayyāyāḥ — a bed; svataḥ siddhe bāhau sati — when one’s arm is naturally available; upabarhaṇaiḥ — with pillows; ucchīrṣakaiḥ — head-rests; purudhā — in many ways; bahu-prakārayā — of many kinds; anna-pātryā — with a food-vessel; bhojana-pātreṇa — eating vessel.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
satyāṁ kṣitau kiṁ kaśipoḥ prayāsair
bāhau sva-siddhe hy upabarhaṇaiḥ kim
saty añjalau kiṁ purudhānna-pātryā
dig-valkalādau sati kiṁ dukūlaiḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
anyathā-siddhim āha dvābhyām—satyām iti | kaśipoḥ śayyāyāḥ | svataḥ siddhe bāhau sati upabarhaṇair ucchīrṣakaiḥ | purudhā bahu-prakārayā anna-pātryā bhojana-pātreṇa || 4 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अन्यथासिद्धिमाह द्वाभ्याम्—सत्यामिति । कशिपोः शय्यायाः । स्वतः सिद्धे बाहौ सति उपबर्हणैरुच्छीर्षकैः । पुरुधा बहुप्रकारया अन्नपात्र्या भोजनपात्रेण ॥ ४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In two verses he states obtaining what is needed by another means, beginning with “when the earth is available.” “Kaśipu” means a bed. When one’s arm is naturally available, what need is there for “upabarhaṇas,” head-rests? “Purudhā” means of many kinds; “anna-pātrī” means a vessel for eating.

ŚB 2.2.5

चीराणि किं पथि न सन्ति दिशन्ति भिक्षां
नैवाङ्घ्रिपाः परभृतः सरितोऽप्यशुष्यन् ।
रुद्धा गुहाः किमजितोऽवति नोपसन्नान्
कस्माद्भजन्ति कवयो धनदुर्मदान्धान् ॥ ५ ॥
Are there not rags on the road? Do the trees that support others not give alms? Have the rivers dried up? Are the caves closed? Does Ajita not protect those who approach Him? Why then do the wise serve those blinded by the arrogance of wealth?
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
cīrāṇi — rags, pieces of cloth; kim — are there not, what; pathi — on the road; na santi — are not; diśanti — give, bestow; bhikṣām — alms; na eva — surely not; aṅghripāḥ — trees, literally foot-drinkers; para-bhṛtaḥ — supporting others; saritaḥ — rivers; api — also; aśuṣyan — have dried up; ruddhāḥ — closed; guhāḥ — caves; kim — are they; ajitaḥ — Ajita, the unconquered Lord; avati — protects; na — not; upasannān — those who have approached, taken refuge; kasmāt — why; bhajanti — serve; kavayaḥ — the wise; dhana-durmada-andhān — those blinded by the bad pride of wealth.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
dik-sadbhāvaḥ — availability of the directions; nagnatvam eva — merely nakedness; valkalam — bark; annam — food; toyam — water; vāsa-sthānam — dwelling; yācñā-prayatnam vinā — without effort of begging; katham prāpyeta — how could it be obtained; cīrāṇi — rags; vastra-khaṇḍāni — pieces of cloth; parān bibhṛti puṣṇanti — they support and nourish others; phala-ādibhiḥ — by fruits and the like; guhāḥ — caves; giri-daryaḥ — clefts in mountains; kadācit eṣām alābhe — if sometimes these are unavailable; kim kāryam — what should be done; ajitaḥ hariḥ — Ajita, Hari; upasannān śaraṇāgatān — those who approach, those who take refuge; kim na avati na rakṣati — does He not protect; kim-śabdasya pūrvatra api sambandhaḥ — “kim” connects also with the preceding clauses; dhanena durmadaḥ — bad pride through wealth; tena andhān — thereby blinded; naṣṭa-vivekān — whose discrimination is destroyed.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
cīrāṇi kiṁ pathi na santi diśanti bhikṣāṁ
naivāṅghripāḥ para-bhṛtaḥ sarito ’py aśuṣyan
ruddhā guhāḥ kim ajito ’vati nopasannān
kasmād bhajanti kavayo dhana-durmadāndhān

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nanu dik-sadbhāvo nāma nagnatvam eva, valkalam annaṁ toyaṁ vāsa-sthānaṁ tu yācñā-prayatnaṁ vinā kathaṁ prāpyeta ? tatrāha | cīrāṇi vastra-khaṇḍāni | parān bibhrati puṣṇanti phalādibhir ye | guhā giri-daryaḥ | nanu kadācid eṣām alābhe kiṁ kāryam ? tatrāha—ajito harir upasannān śaraṇāgatān kiṁ nāvati na rakṣati ? kiṁ-śabdasya pūrvatrāpi sambandhaḥ | dhanena yo durmadas tena andhān naṣṭa-vivekān || 5 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु दिक्सद्भावो नाम नग्नत्वमेव, वल्कलमन्नं तोयं वासस्थानं तु याच्ञाप्रयत्नं विना कथं प्राप्येत ? तत्राह । चीराणि वस्त्रखण्डानि । परान् बिभ्रति पुष्णन्ति फलादिभिर्ये । गुहाः गिरिदर्यः । ननु कदाचिदेषामलाभे किं कार्यम् ? तत्राह—अजितो हरिरुपसन्नान् शरणागतान् किं नावति न रक्षति ? किंशब्दस्य पूर्वत्रापि सम्बन्धः । धनेन यो दुर्मदस्तेन अन्धान् नष्टविवेकान् ॥ ५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. An objection: the mere availability of the directions means only nakedness; but how can bark, food, water and a dwelling be obtained without the effort of begging? To this he replies. “Cīrāṇi” are pieces of cloth. The trees are those which support and nourish others with fruits and the like. The caves are clefts in mountains. Another objection: if at some time these are unavailable, what is to be done? He answers: does Ajita, Hari, not protect those who approach Him, those who have taken refuge? The word “kim,” “what/are not,” is also connected with the preceding clauses. Those blinded by wealth are those whose discrimination has been destroyed by the bad pride produced by wealth.

ŚB 2.2.6

एवं स्वचित्ते स्वत एव सिद्ध
आत्मा प्रियोऽर्थो भगवाननन्तः ।
तं निर्वृतो नियतार्थो भजेत
संसारहेतूपरमश्च यत्र ॥ ६ ॥
Thus, Bhagavān Ananta is naturally present in one’s own heart: He is the Self, dear, and the true object. Content and fixed in the real goal, one should worship Him, wherein there is also cessation of the cause of saṁsāra.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
evam — thus; sva-citte — in one’s own heart, mind; svataḥ eva — naturally, of itself; siddhaḥ — present, established; ātmā — the Self; priyaḥ — dear; arthaḥ — the true object, reality; bhagavān — Bhagavān; anantaḥ — Ananta, endless, eternal; tam — Him; nirvṛtaḥ — content, blissful; niyata-arthaḥ — fixed in the true goal, of determined purpose; bhajeta — one should worship; saṁsāra-hetu — cause of saṁsāra; uparamaḥ — cessation; ca — and; yatra — wherein.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tadā tena kim kartavyam — what then should he do; hariḥ tu sevyaḥ — Hari is to be served; evam viraktaḥ san — being thus detached; tam bhajeta — should worship Him; bhajanīyatve hetavaḥ — reasons for worthiness of worship; sva-citte svataḥ eva siddhaḥ — naturally present in one’s own heart; yataḥ ātmā — because He is the Self; ataḥ eva priyaḥ — therefore dear; priyasya sevā — service of the dear; sukha-rūpā eva — itself of the nature of happiness; arthaḥ satyaḥ — the real, true object; na tu anātma-vat mithyā — not false like non-Self; bhagavān — Bhagavān; bhajanīya-guṇaḥ — possessing qualities worthy of worship; anantaḥ nityaḥ — Ananta, eternal; niyata-arthaḥ — one whose true nature is ascertained; tad-anubhava-ānandena — through bliss of experiencing Him; nirvṛtaḥ — content; saṁsāra-hetoḥ avidyāyāḥ — of ignorance, cause of saṁsāra; uparamaḥ nāśaḥ — cessation, destruction.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
evaṁ sva-citte svata eva siddha
ātmā priyo ’rtho bhagavān anantaḥ
taṁ nirvṛto niyatārtho bhajeta
saṁsāra-hetūparamaś ca yatra

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tadā tena kiṁ kartavyam ? haris tu sevya ity āha—evaṁ viraktaḥ saṁs taṁ bhajeta | bhajanīyatve hetavaḥ—sva-citte svata eva siddhaḥ | yata ātmā, ata eva priyaḥ | priyasya ca sevā sukha-rūpaiva | arthaḥ satyo na tv anātmavan mithyā | bhagavān bhajanīya-guṇaś ca anantaś ca nityaḥ | ya evaṁ-bhūtas taṁ bhajeta | niyatārtho niścita-svarūpaḥ | tad-anubhavānandena nirvṛtaḥ sann iti svataḥ-sukhātmatvaṁ darśitam | kiṁ ca, yatra yasmin bhajane sati saṁsāra-hetor avidyāyā uparamo nāśo bhavati || 6 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदा तेन किं कर्तव्यम् ? हरिस्तु सेव्य इत्याह—एवं विरक्तः संस्तं भजेत । भजनीयत्वे हेतवः—स्वचित्ते स्वत एव सिद्धः । यत आत्मा, अत एव प्रियः । प्रियस्य च सेवा सुखरूपैव । अर्थः सत्यो न त्वनात्मवन्मिथ्या । भगवान् भजनीयगुणश्च अनन्तश्च नित्यः । य एवंभूतस्तं भजेत । नियतार्थो निश्चितस्वरूपः । तदनुभवानन्देन निर्वृतः सन्निति स्वतःसुखात्मत्वं दर्शितम् । किं च, यत्र यस्मिन् भजने सति संसारहेतोरविद्याया उपरमो नाशो भवति ॥ ६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. What then should such a person do? He says that Hari is to be served: being thus detached, he should worship Him. The reasons He is worthy of worship are these. He is naturally present in one’s own heart. Because He is the Self, He is therefore dear; and service to one who is dear is itself of the nature of happiness. He is “artha,” the real, not false like what is non-Self. He is Bhagavān, possessing qualities worthy of worship, and Ananta, eternal. One should worship Him who is such. “Niyatārtha” means one whose true nature is ascertained. By saying that he is content through the bliss of experiencing Him, His nature as happiness in itself is shown. Moreover, in that worship there occurs cessation, destruction, of ignorance, the cause of saṁsāra.

ŚB 2.2.7

कस्तां त्वनादृत्य परानुचिन्ता-
मृते पशूनसतीं नाम कुर्यात् ।
पश्यञ्जनं पतितं वैतरण्यां
स्वकर्मजान् परितापाञ्जुषाणम् ॥ ७ ॥
Who, other than those like beasts, would disregard that contemplation of the Supreme and undertake contemplation of the unreal, while seeing people fallen in the Vaitaraṇī and undergoing afflictions born from their own actions?
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kaḥ — who; tām — that; tu — indeed; anādṛtya — disregarding; para-anucintām — contemplation of the Supreme; ṛte — except, apart from; paśūn — beasts, those like beasts; asatīm — unreal, impermanent; nāma — indeed, as it were; kuryāt — would do; paśyan — seeing; janam — people; patitam — fallen; vaitaraṇyām — in Vaitaraṇī; sva-karma-jān — born from their own actions; paritāpān — afflictions; juṣāṇam — undergoing, experiencing.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
etat eva — this very point; anya-cintā-nindayā — by censuring other contemplation; dṛḍhayati — he makes firm; tām tathā-bhūtām — that which is such; parasya hareḥ anucintām — contemplation of Supreme Hari; dhāraṇām — concentration; anādṛtya — disregarding; paśūn — beasts; karma-jaḍān — inert through ritual action; vinā — apart from; asatīm viṣaya-cintām — unreal contemplation of sense objects; kaḥ nāma kuryāt — who indeed would perform; tayā cintayā — by that contemplation; vaitaraṇyām patitam — fallen into Vaitaraṇī; sva-karma-jān — born from one’s own actions; ādhyātmika-ādi-kleśān — afflictions beginning with those arising from oneself; sevamānam — experiencing; janam paśyan — seeing people; vaitaraṇī — Vaitaraṇī; yamasya dvārasthā nadī — river at Yama’s gate; tat-tulyatvāt — because of similarity to it; saṁsṛtiḥ vaitaraṇī — saṁsāra is called Vaitaraṇī.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kas tāṁ tv anādṛtya parānucintām
ṛte paśūn asatīṁ nāma kuryāt
paśyañ janaṁ patitaṁ vaitaraṇyāṁ
sva-karmajān paritāpāñ juṣāṇam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
etad evānya-cintā-nindayā dṛḍhayati—ka iti | tāṁ tathā-bhūtāṁ parasya harer anucintāṁ dhāraṇām anādṛtya paśūn karma-jaḍān vinā | yathā “paśur evāyaṁ sa devānām” iti śruteḥ | asatīṁ viṣaya-cintāṁ ko nāma kuryāt ? tayā cintayā vaitaraṇyāṁ patitaṁ, tatra ca sva-karmajān ādhyātmikādi-kleśān sevamānaṁ janaṁ paśyan | vaitaraṇī yamasya dvārasthā nadī; tat-tulyatvāt saṁsṛtir vaitaraṇī || 7 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एतदेवान्यचिन्तानिन्दया द्रढयति—क इति । तां तथाभूतां परस्य हरेरनुचिन्तां धारणामनादृत्य पशून् कर्मजडान् विना । यथा “पशुरेवायं स देवानाम्” इति श्रुतेः । असतीं विषयचिन्तां को नाम कुर्यात् ? तया चिन्तया वैतरण्यां पतितं, तत्र च स्वकर्मजानाध्यात्मिकादिक्लेशान् सेवमानं जनं पश्यन् । वैतरणी यमस्य द्वारस्था नदी; तत्तुल्यत्वात् संसृतिर्वैतरणी ॥ ७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He makes this very point firm by censuring other contemplation, with “who?” Disregarding that concentration, that contemplation of the Supreme Hari, who other than beasts, those inert through ritual action, would undertake unreal contemplation of sense objects? As the revelation says, “This person is indeed a beast of the gods.” One sees people, through that contemplation, fallen into Vaitaraṇī and there experiencing afflictions such as those arising from oneself, born from their own actions. Vaitaraṇī is the river situated at Yama’s gate; because saṁsāra is like it, saṁsāra is called Vaitaraṇī.

ŚB 2.2.8

केचित् स्वदेहान्तर्हृदयावकाशे
प्रादेशमात्रं पुरुषं वसन्तम् ।
चतुर्भुजं कञ्जरथाङ्गशङ्ख-
गदाधरं धारणया स्मरन्ति ॥ ८ ॥
Some, through concentration, remember the Puruṣa dwelling in the space of the heart within their own body, of the measure of a prādeśa, four-armed, holding lotus, discus, conch and mace.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
kecit — some; sva-deha-antaḥ — within one’s own body; hṛdaya-avakāśe — in the space, region, of the heart; prādeśa-mātram — of the measure of a prādeśa, a span; puruṣam — the Puruṣa; vasantam — dwelling; catuḥ-bhujam — four-armed; kañja — lotus; ratha-aṅga — discus, chariot-wheel; śaṅkha — conch; gadā-dharam — holding the mace; dhāraṇayā — through concentration; smaranti — they remember, meditate upon.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tām eva dhāraṇām — that very concentration; sa-viśeṣaṇām — together with its attributes; āha — he states; kecit iti ṣaḍbhiḥ — with six verses beginning “some”; sva-dehasya antar-madhye — in the inner middle of one’s own body; tat hṛdayam — that is the heart; tatra yaḥ avakāśaḥ — the space there; tasmin vasantam — dwelling in it; prādeśaḥ — a prādeśa; tarjani-aṅguṣṭhayoḥ vistāraḥ — span between index finger and thumb; saḥ eva mātrā pramāṇam — that itself is the measure; hṛdaya-parimāṇam — measurement of the heart; tatra upacaryate — figuratively applied there; kañjam — lotus; padmam — lotus; ratha-aṅgam — chariot-wheel; cakram — discus.

Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
kecit sva-dehāntar-hṛdayāvakāśe
prādeśa-mātraṁ puruṣaṁ vasantam
catur-bhujaṁ kañja-rathāṅga-śaṅkha-
gadā-dharaṁ dhāraṇayā smaranti

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tām eva dhāraṇāṁ sa-viśeṣaṇām āha—kecid iti ṣaḍbhiḥ | sva-dehasyāntar-madhye tad hṛdayaṁ, tatra yo ’vakāśas tasmin vasantam | prādeśas tarjany-aṅguṣṭhayor vistāraḥ, sa eva mātrā pramāṇaṁ yasyeti hṛdaya-parimāṇaṁ tatropacaryate | kañjaṁ padmam | rathāṅgaṁ cakram || 8 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तामेव धारणां सविशेषणामाह—केचिदिति षड्भिः । स्वदेहस्यान्तर्मध्ये तद्धृदयं, तत्र योऽवकाशस्तस्मिन् वसन्तम् । प्रादेशस्तर्जन्यङ्गुष्ठयोर्विस्तारः, स एव मात्रा प्रमाणं यस्येति हृदयपरिमाणं तत्रोपचर्यते । कञ्जं पद्मम् । रथाङ्गं चक्रम् ॥ ८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With six verses beginning “some,” he states that same concentration together with its particular attributes. Within one’s own body, in its middle, is the heart; He dwells in the space there. A “prādeśa” is the span between the index finger and thumb. Saying that He is of that measure figuratively transfers the heart’s measurement to Him. “Kañja” means lotus. “Rathāṅga” means discus.

ŚB 2.2.9

प्रसन्नवक्त्रं नलिनायतेक्षणं
कदम्बकिञ्जल्कपिशङ्गवाससम् ।
लसन्महारत्नहिरण्मयाङ्गदं
स्फुरन्महारत्नकिरीटकुण्डलम् ॥ ९ ॥
His face is gracious; His eyes are long like a lotus. His garments are yellow like the filaments of a kadamba flower. His golden armlets shine with great jewels, and His crown and earrings, made with great jewels, glitter.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
prasanna-vaktram — gracious face; nalina-āyata-īkṣaṇam — whose eyes are long like a lotus; kadamba-kiñjalka — filaments of the kadamba flower; piśaṅga-vāsasam — wearing yellow garments; lasat — shining; mahā-ratna — great jewels; hiraṇmaya-aṅgadam — golden armlets; sphurat — glittering; mahā-ratna — great jewels; kirīṭa-kuṇḍalam — crown and earrings.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
prasanna iti — on “prasanna”; nalinam — lotus; padmam — lotus; tadvat — like that; prasanne — gracious; āyate dīrghe — extended, long; īkṣaṇe — eyes; yasya — whose; kadamba-kusumasya kiñjalkaḥ — filament of the kadamba flower; kesaraḥ — stamen; tadvat — like that; piśaṅge pīte — yellow; vāsasī yasya — whose garments; lasanti mahā-ratnāni — great jewels shine; yeṣu — in which; suvarṇa-mayāni aṅgadāni yasya — whose armlets are made of gold; sphuranti — glitter; tat-mayāni kirīṭa-kuṇḍalāni — crown and earrings made of those jewels.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
prasanna-vaktraṁ nalināyatekṣaṇaṁ
kadamba-kiñjalka-piśaṅga-vāsasam
lasan-mahā-ratna-hiraṇmayāṅgadaṁ
sphuran-mahā-ratna-kirīṭa-kuṇḍalam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
prasanneti | nalinaṁ padmaṁ tadvat prasanne āyate dīrghe īkṣaṇe yasya | kadamba-kusumasya kiñjalkaḥ kesaraḥ tadvat piśaṅge pīte vāsasī yasya | lasanti mahā-ratnāni yeṣu tāni suvarṇa-mayāny aṅgadāni yasya | sphuranti ca tāni mahā-ratnāni ca tan-mayāni kirīṭa-kuṇḍalāni yasya || 9 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
प्रसन्नेति । नलिनं पद्मं तद्वत् प्रसन्ने आयते दीर्घे ईक्षणे यस्य । कदम्बकुसुमस्य किञ्जल्कः केसरः तद्वत् पिशङ्गे पीते वाससी यस्य । लसन्ति महारत्नानि येषु तानि सुवर्णमयान्यङ्गदानि यस्य । स्फुरन्ति च तानि महारत्नानि च तन्मयानि किरीटकुण्डलानि यस्य ॥ ९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Nalina” is a lotus; like that are His gracious, extended, long eyes. The filament of the kadamba flower is its stamen; like that are His yellow garments. His armlets are made of gold and have great jewels shining in them. His crown and earrings are made of those great jewels and glitter.

ŚB 2.2.10

उन्निद्रहृत्पङ्कजकर्णिकालये
योगेश्वरास्थापितपादपल्लवम् ।
श्रीलक्षणं कौस्तुभरत्नकन्धर-
मम्लानलक्ष्म्या वनमालयाचितम् ॥ १० ॥
His tender feet are placed by the lords of yoga in the whorl-abode of the fully opened lotus of the heart. He bears the mark of Śrī; the Kaustubha jewel is at His neck, and He is covered by a forest garland of unfading beauty.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
unnidra — fully opened; hṛt-paṅkaja — lotus of the heart; karṇikā-ālaye — in the whorl as an abode; yoga-īśvara — lords of yoga; āsthāpita — placed; pāda-pallavam — tender feet; śrī-lakṣaṇam — bearing the mark of Śrī; kaustubha-ratna — Kaustubha jewel; kandharam — neck; amlāna-lakṣmyā — with unfading beauty; vana-mālayā — by the forest garland; ācitam — covered.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
unnidram — opened; vikasitam — blossomed; hṛt-paṅkajam — lotus of the heart; tasya karṇikā eva ālayaḥ — its whorl itself is the abode; sthānam — place; yogeśvaraiḥ — by lords of yoga; āsthāpitau pāda-pallavau — the two tender feet are established; yasya — whose; śrīḥ eva lakṣma — Śrī herself is the mark; cihnam — sign; tad-yuktam — endowed with it; kaustubha-ratnam — Kaustubha jewel; kandharāyām — at the neck; amlānā lakṣmīḥ — unfading beauty; śobhā — splendour; vana-mālayā — by the forest garland; ācitam vyāptam — covered, pervaded.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
unnidra-hṛt-paṅkaja-karṇikālaye
yogeśvarāsthāpita-pāda-pallavam
śrī-lakṣaṇaṁ kaustubha-ratna-kandharam
amlāna-lakṣmyā vana-mālayācitam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
unnidreti | unnidraṁ vikasitaṁ yad dhṛt-paṅkajaṁ tasya karṇikaivālayaḥ sthānaṁ tasmin | yogeśvarair āsthāpitau pāda-pallavau yasya | śrīr eva lakṣma cihnaṁ tad-yuktam | pāmādi-vihito matvarthīyo na-pratyayaḥ | kaustubha-ratnaṁ kandharāyāṁ yasya | amlānā lakṣmīḥ śobhā yasyāḥ tayā vana-mālayā ācitaṁ vyāptam || 10 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
उन्निद्रेति । उन्निद्रं विकसितं यद्धृत्पङ्कजं तस्य कर्णिकैवालयः स्थानं तस्मिन् । योगेश्वरैरास्थापितौ पादपल्लवौ यस्य । श्रीरेव लक्ष्म चिह्नं तद्युक्तम् । पामादिविहितो मत्वर्थीयो नप्रत्ययः । कौस्तुभरत्नं कन्धरायां यस्य । अम्लाना लक्ष्मीः शोभा यस्याः तया वनमालया आचितं व्याप्तम् ॥ १० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Unnidra” means opened, blossomed. The lotus of the heart is opened; its whorl itself is the abode, the place, and there His two tender feet are established by the lords of yoga. Śrī herself is the “lakṣma,” the mark; He is endowed with it. The suffix na in the sense of possession is prescribed in the group beginning with pāman. The Kaustubha jewel is at His neck. The forest garland covers Him with its unfading beauty, its splendour.

ŚB 2.2.11

विभूषितं मेखलयाङ्गुलीयकै-
र्महाधनैर्नूपुरकङ्कणादिभिः ।
स्निग्धामलाकुञ्चितनीलकुन्तलै-
र्विरोचमानाननहासपेशलम् ॥ ११ ॥
He is adorned with a girdle, very precious finger-rings, anklets, bracelets and other ornaments. With glossy, spotless, curling dark-blue locks, His shining face is made lovely by its smile.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vibhūṣitam — adorned; mekhalayā — with a girdle; aṅgulīyakaiḥ — with finger-rings; mahā-dhanaiḥ — very precious; nūpura — anklets; kaṅkaṇa — bracelets; ādibhiḥ — and others; snigdha — glossy; amala — spotless; ākuñcita — curled; nīla — dark-blue; kuntalaiḥ — with locks; virocamāna — shining; ānana — face; hāsa — smile; peśalam — lovely.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
snigdhatva-ādi-viśiṣṭaiḥ — distinguished by glossiness and the other qualities; kuntalaiḥ — by locks; virocamāne ānane — on the shining face; yaḥ hāsaḥ — the smile which is there; tena — by that; peśalam — lovely; sundaram — beautiful.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vibhūṣitaṁ mekhalayāṅgulīyakair
mahā-dhanair nūpura-kaṅkaṇādibhiḥ
snigdhāmalākuñcita-nīla-kuntalair
virocamānānana-hāsa-peśalam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
vibhūṣitam iti | snigdhatvādi-viśiṣṭaiḥ kuntalair virocamāne ānane yo hāsas tena peśalaṁ sundaram || 11 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विभूषितमिति । स्निग्धत्वादिविशिष्टैः कुन्तलैर्विरोचमाने आनने यो हासस्तेन पेशलं सुन्दरम् ॥ ११ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. On “adorned”: His face shines with locks distinguished by glossiness and the other stated qualities; the smile upon that face makes it lovely, beautiful.

ŚB 2.2.12

अदीनलीलाहसितेक्षणोल्लसद्-
भ्रूभङ्गसंसूचितभूर्यनुग्रहम् ।
ईक्षेत चिन्तामयमेनमीश्वरं
यावन्मनो धारणयावतिष्ठते ॥ १२ ॥
One should behold this Lord, manifest in contemplation, whose abundant grace is indicated by the lively movements of His brows shining with His generous playful smile and glance, for as long as the mind remains fixed through concentration.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
adīna — generous; līlā — playful; hasita — smiling; īkṣaṇa — glance; ullasat — shining; bhrū-bhaṅga — movements of the eyebrows; saṁsūcita — indicated; bhūri-anugraham — abundant grace; īkṣeta — one should behold; cintā-mayam — appearing through contemplation; enam — this; īśvaram — Lord; yāvat — as long as; manaḥ — mind; dhāraṇayā — through concentration; avatiṣṭhate — remains fixed.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
adīnam — generous; udāram — liberal, noble; līlā-hasitam — playful smile; tena — with that; yad īkṣaṇam — the glance; tasmin — in it; ullasantaḥ — shining; bhrū-bhaṅgāḥ — movements of the brows; bhrū-vikṣepāḥ — motions of the eyebrows; taiḥ — by them; saṁsūcitaḥ — indicated; bhūriḥ anugrahaḥ — abundant grace; yena — by which; cintā-mayam — made of contemplation; cintayā āvirbhavantam — appearing through contemplation.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
adīna-līlā-hasitekṣaṇollasad-
bhrū-bhaṅga-saṁsūcita-bhūry-anugraham
īkṣeta cintāmayam enam īśvaraṁ
yāvan mano dhāraṇayāvatiṣṭhate

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
adīneti | adīnam udāraṁ yal līlā-hasitaṁ tena yad īkṣaṇaṁ tasminn ullasanto ye bhrū-bhaṅgā bhrū-vikṣepās taiḥ saṁsūcito bhūrir anugraho yena | cintāmayaṁ cintayā āvirbhavantam || 12 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अदीनेति । अदीनमुदारं यल्लीलाहसितं तेन यदीक्षणं तस्मिन्नुल्लसन्तो ये भ्रूभङ्गा भ्रूविक्षेपास्तैः संसूचितो भूरिरनुग्रहो येन । चिन्तामयं चिन्तया आविर्भवन्तम् ॥ १२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Adīna” means generous. The playful smile is generous; in the glance joined with it the movements of the eyebrows, the motions of the brows, shine. By them His abundant grace is indicated. “Cintāmayam” means appearing through contemplation.

ŚB 2.2.13

एकैकशोऽङ्गानि धियानुभावयेत्
पादादि यावद्धसितं गदाभृतः ।
जितं जितं स्थानमपोह्य धारयेत्
परं परं शुद्ध्यति धीर्यथा यथा ॥ १३ ॥
One should contemplate, one by one with the intelligence, the limbs of the bearer of the mace, beginning with the feet and extending to the smile. Leaving each place as it becomes mastered, one should fix upon the next and the next, as the intelligence becomes purified more and more.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
eka-ekaśaḥ — one by one; aṅgāni — limbs; dhiyā — with intelligence; anubhāvayet — should contemplate; pāda-ādi — beginning with the feet; yāvat — as far as; hasitam — smile; gadā-bhṛtaḥ — of the bearer of the mace; jitam jitam — each one mastered; sthānam — place, limb; apohya — leaving aside; dhārayet — should fix, meditate; param param — the next and the next; śuddhyati — becomes purified; dhīḥ — intelligence; yathā yathā — progressively.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
asya eva dhyānam āha — he states meditation on this very form; eka-ekaśaḥ — one by one; anubhāvayet — should contemplate; dhyāyet — should meditate; yad yat jitam — whatever has been mastered; ayatnataḥ sphuritam — appearing without effort; pāda-gulpha-ādi-sthānam avayavaḥ — limb-place such as feet, ankles and so on; tat tat apohya tyaktvā — leaving each aside; param param — the next and the next; jaṅghā-jānu-ādi — shanks, knees and so on; dhārayet dhyāyet — should fix, meditate; śuddhyati — becomes purified; tasmin niścalā bhavati — becomes steady in it.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ekaikaśo ’ṅgāni dhiyānubhāvayet
pādādi yāvad dhasitaṁ gadābhṛtaḥ
jitaṁ jitaṁ sthānam apohya dhārayet
paraṁ paraṁ śuddhyati dhīr yathā yathā

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
asyaiva dhyānam āha—ekaikaśa iti | anubhāvayed dhyāyet | yad yaj jitam ayatnataḥ sphuritaṁ pāda-gulphādi-sthānam avayavas tat tad apohya tyaktvā paraṁ paraṁ jaṅghā-jānvādi dhārayed dhyāyet | śuddhyati tasmin niścalā bhavati || 13 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अस्यैव ध्यानमाह—एकैकश इति । अनुभावयेद्ध्यायेत् । यद्यज्जितमयत्नतः स्फुरितं पादगुल्फादिस्थानमवयवस्तत्तदपोह्य त्यक्त्वा परं परं जङ्घाजान्वादि धारयेद्ध्यायेत् । शुद्ध्यति तस्मिन्निश्चला भवति ॥ १३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states meditation on this very form with “one by one.” “Anubhāvayet” means one should meditate. Whatever limb-place—feet, ankles and so on—has been mastered and appears without effort, one should leave that aside and meditate upon the next and the next, the shanks, knees and so on. “Becomes purified” means the intelligence becomes steady in it.

ŚB 2.2.14

यावन्न जायेत परावरेऽस्मिन्
विश्वेश्वरे द्रष्टरि भक्तियोगः ।
तावत् स्थवीयः पुरुषस्य रूपं
क्रियावसाने प्रयतः स्मरेत ॥ १४ ॥
As long as bhakti-yoga has not arisen toward this Lord of the universe, the seer who is above the higher and lower, one should, with disciplined attention, remember the grosser form of the Puruṣa after completing prescribed activity.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yāvat — as long as; na — not; jāyeta — should arise; para-avare — higher and lower; asmin — in this; viśva-īśvare — Lord of the universe; draṣṭari — in the seer; bhakti-yogaḥ — bhakti-yoga; tāvat — until then; sthavīyaḥ — grosser; puruṣasya — of the Puruṣa; rūpam — form; kriyā-avasāne — at the end of prescribed activity; prayataḥ — disciplined; smareta — should remember.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
pūrva-ukta-vairāja-dhāraṇāyāḥ — of the previously stated Virāṭ concentration; etat-dhāraṇā-aṅgatvam — its being a limb of this concentration; āha — he states; pare brahma-ādayaḥ — Brahmā and others are higher beings; avare yasmāt — lower than Him; viśveśvare — in the Lord of the universe; draṣṭari — in the seer; na tu dṛśye — not in the seen; caitanya-ghanatvāt — because He is a mass of consciousness; bhakti-yogaḥ — bhakti-yoga; prema-lakṣaṇaḥ — characterized by love; kriyā-avasāne — at the end of activity; āvaśyaka-karma-anuṣṭhāna-anantaram — after performing necessary prescribed actions; karma api — action also; bhakti-yoga-paryantam eva — extends only as far as bhakti-yoga; prayataḥ — disciplined; niyama-tatparaḥ — intent upon discipline.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yāvan na jāyeta parāvare ’smin
viśveśvare draṣṭari bhakti-yogaḥ
tāvat sthavīyaḥ puruṣasya rūpaṁ
kriyāvasāne prayataḥ smareta

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
pūrvokta-vairāja-dhāraṇāyā etad-dhāraṇāṅgatvam āha—yāvad iti | pare brahmādayo ’vare yasmāt | kutaḥ ? viśveśvare draṣṭari na tu dṛśye, caitanya-ghanatvāt | bhakti-yogaḥ prema-lakṣaṇaḥ | kriyāvasāne āvaśyaka-karmānuṣṭhānānantaram | anena karmāpi bhakti-yoga-paryantam evety uktam | prayato niyama-tatparaḥ || 14 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
पूर्वोक्तवैराजधारणाया एतद्धारणाङ्गत्वमाह—यावदिति । परे ब्रह्मादयोऽवरे यस्मात् । कुतः ? विश्वेश्वरे द्रष्टरि न तु दृश्ये, चैतन्यघनत्वात् । भक्तियोगः प्रेमलक्षणः । क्रियावसाने आवश्यककर्मानुष्ठानानन्तरम् । अनेन कर्मापि भक्तियोगपर्यन्तमेवेत्युक्तम् । प्रयतो नियमतत्परः ॥ १४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that the previously stated Virāṭ concentration is a limb of this concentration, with “as long as.” Brahmā and the others are lower than Him. Why? He is the Lord of the universe and the seer, not the seen, because He is a mass of consciousness. Bhakti-yoga is characterized by love. “At the end of activity” means after performing the necessary prescribed action. By this it is also said that action extends only as far as bhakti-yoga. “Prayataḥ” means intent upon discipline.

ŚB 2.2.15

स्थिरं सुखं चासनमास्थितो यति-
र्यदा जिहासुरिममङ्ग लोकम् ।
काले च देशे च मनो न सज्जयेत्
प्राणान् नियच्छेन्मनसा जितासुः ॥ १५ ॥
O King, when the yogin, seated on a firm and comfortable seat, desires to leave this world, he should not attach the mind to time or place. Having mastered the life-air, he should restrain the vital functions and senses by the mind.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
sthiram — firm; sukham — comfortable; ca — and; āsanam — seat; āsthitaḥ — seated upon; yatiḥ — yogin; yadā — when; jihāsuḥ — desiring to leave; imam — this; aṅga — O King; lokam — world, body; kāle — in time; deśe — in place; manaḥ — mind; na sajjayet — should not attach; prāṇān — vital functions; niyacchet — should restrain; manasā — by the mind; jita-asuḥ — having mastered the life-air.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
āsanna-mṛtyoḥ puṁsaḥ — of a person whose death is near; kṛtyam uktam — duty has been stated; idānīm — now; svayam deham tyaktum icchataḥ — of one wishing personally to abandon the body; kṛtyam āha — he states the duty; sthiram iti sārdhaiḥ caturbhiḥ — with four and a half verses beginning “firm”; aṅga he rājan — O King; imam lokam deham — this world, the body; jihāsuḥ — desiring to abandon; deśe puṇya-kṣetre — to a place such as a holy field; kāle uttara-ayana-ādau — to a time such as the northern course; manaḥ na sajjayet — should not attach the mind; saṅgam na prāpayet — should not enter attachment; deśa-kālau — place and time; yoginaḥ siddhi-hetū na — are not causes of accomplishment for a yogin; yogaḥ eva — yoga alone; dṛḍha-niścayaḥ — firm certainty; sthiram sukha-karam āsanam — firm and comfortable seat; prāṇān niyacchet — should restrain the vital functions.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sthiraṁ sukhaṁ cāsanam āsthito yatir
yadā jihāsur imam aṅga lokam
kāle ca deśe ca mano na sajjayet
prāṇān niyacchen manasā jitāsuḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
evaṁ tāvad āsanna-mṛtyoḥ puṁsaḥ kṛtyam uktam | idānīṁ tasyaiva svayaṁ dehaṁ tyaktum icchataḥ kṛtyam āha—sthiram iti sārdhaiś caturbhiḥ | aṅga he rājan, evaṁ-bhūto yatir yadā imaṁ lokaṁ dehaṁ jihāsur hātum icchati, tadā deśe puṇya-kṣetre kāle cottarāyaṇādau mano na sajjayet saṅgaṁ na prāpayet | na hi deśa-kālau yoginaḥ siddhi-hetū, kiṁtu yoga eveti dṛḍha-niścayo bhūtvā sthiraṁ sukha-karaṁ cāsanam āsthitaḥ prāṇān niyacched ity arthaḥ || 15 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एवं तावदासन्नमृत्योः पुंसः कृत्यमुक्तम् । इदानीं तस्यैव स्वयं देहं त्यक्तुमिच्छतः कृत्यमाह—स्थिरमिति सार्धैश्चतुर्भिः । अङ्ग हे राजन्, एवंभूतो यतिर्यदा इमं लोकं देहं जिहासुर्हातुमिच्छति, तदा देशे पुण्यक्षेत्रे काले चोत्तरायणादौ मनो न सज्जयेत् सङ्गं न प्रापयेत् । न हि देशकालौ योगिनः सिद्धिहेतू, किंतु योग एवेति दृढनिश्चयो भूत्वा स्थिरं सुखकरं चासनमास्थितः प्राणान् नियच्छेदित्यर्थः ॥ १५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Thus the duty of a person whose death is near has so far been stated. Now he states the duty of that same person when he wishes to abandon the body himself, with “firm,” in four and a half verses. “Aṅga,” O King: when such a yogin desires to abandon this world, the body, he should not attach the mind to a place such as a holy field or to a time such as the northern course of the sun. He should not enter into attachment. Place and time are not causes of accomplishment for a yogin; yoga alone is. Having this firm certainty, seated on a firm and comfortable seat, he should restrain the vital functions.

ŚB 2.2.16

मनः स्वबुद्ध्यामलया नियम्य
क्षेत्रज्ञ एतां निनयेत् तमात्मनि ।
आत्मानमात्मन्यवरुध्य धीरो
लब्धोपशान्तिर्विरमेत कृत्यात् ॥ १६ ॥
Having restrained the mind by his own pure intelligence, he should dissolve that intelligence into the knower of the field, and that knower into the Self. Having confined the self in the Self, the steady one, having attained tranquility, should cease from what remains to be done.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
manaḥ — mind; sva-buddhyā — by one’s own intelligence; amalayā — pure; niyamya — restraining; kṣetra-jñe — into the knower of the field; etām — this intelligence; ninayet — should lead, dissolve; tam — that knower; ātmani — into the self; ātmānam — the self; ātmani — in the Self, Brahman; avarudhya — confining, uniting; dhīraḥ — steady one; labdha-upaśāntiḥ — having attained tranquility; virameta — should cease; kṛtyāt — from what is to be done.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tat-tat-gṛhīta-viṣayam manaḥ — the mind that has taken up its respective objects; buddhyā — by intelligence; niścaya-rūpayā — of the nature of certainty; niyamya — restraining; tat-mātram kṛtvā — making it of that alone; etām buddhim — this intelligence; kṣetrajñe — into the knower of the field; buddhy-ādi-draṣṭari — the seer of intelligence and the rest; ninayet pravilāpayet — should lead, dissolve; tam kṣetrajñam — that knower of the field; ātmani śuddhe — into the pure self; śuddham ātmānam — pure self; ātmā eva aham iti — regarding it as “I am the Self”; śuddhe brahmaṇi — in pure Brahman; avarudhya ekīkṛtya — confining, uniting; labdha-upaśāntiḥ — having attained tranquility; prāpta-nirvṛtiḥ — having obtained cessation; kṛtyāt virameta — should cease from action; tataḥ param prāpya-abhāvāt — because beyond that there is nothing further to be attained.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
manaḥ sva-buddhyāmalayā niyamya
kṣetra-jña etāṁ ninayet tam ātmani
ātmānam ātmany avarudhya dhīro
labdhopaśāntir virameta kṛtyāt

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
mana iti | tat-tad-gṛhīta-viṣayaṁ mano buddhyā niścaya-rūpayā niyamya tan-mātraṁ kṛtvā, etāṁ buddhiṁ kṣetrajñe buddhy-ādi-draṣṭari ninayet pravilāpayet | taṁ ca kṣetrajñam ātmani śuddhe | taṁ ca śuddham ātmānam ātmaivāham ity ātmani śuddhe brahmaṇy avarudhya ekīkṛtya labdhopaśāntiḥ prāpta-nirvṛtiḥ san kṛtyād viramet, tataḥ paraṁ prāpyābhāvāt || 16 ||
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
मन इति । तत्तद्गृहीतविषयं मनो बुद्ध्या निश्चयरूपया नियम्य तन्मात्रं कृत्वा, एतां बुद्धिं क्षेत्रज्ञे बुद्ध्यादिद्रष्टरि निनयेत् प्रविलापयेत् । तं च क्षेत्रज्ञमात्मनि शुद्धे । तं च शुद्धमात्मानमात्मैवाहमित्यात्मनि शुद्धे ब्रह्मण्यवरुध्य एकीकृत्य लब्धोपशान्तिः प्राप्तनिर्वृतिः सन् कृत्याद्विरमेत्, ततः परं प्राप्याभावात् ॥ १६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. On “mind”: having restrained by intelligence, which is of the nature of certainty, the mind that has taken up its respective objects, and having made it of that alone, he should lead this intelligence into the knower of the field, the seer of intelligence and the rest—that is, dissolve it. He should dissolve that knower of the field into the pure self. Then, regarding the pure self as “I am the Self,” he should confine it in pure Brahman, unite it with Brahman. Having attained tranquility, having obtained cessation, he should cease from action, because beyond that there is nothing further to be attained.

ŚB 2.2.17

न यत्र कालोऽनिमिषां परः प्रभुः
कुतो नु देवा जगतां य ईशिरे ।
न यत्र सत्त्वं न रजस्तमश्च
न वै विकारो न महान् प्रधानम् ॥ १७ ॥
There, time—the supreme ruler even of the sleepless gods—has no power; how then could the gods who rule living beings have power there? There are neither sattva, rajas nor tamas there, nor ahaṅkāra, nor mahat, nor pradhāna.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na — not; yatra — where; kālaḥ — time; animiṣām — of the sleepless ones, the gods; paraḥ — supreme; prabhuḥ — ruler, master; kutaḥ nu — how then; devāḥ — gods; jagatām — of living beings, worlds; ye — who; īśire — rule; sattvam — sattva; rajaḥ — rajas; tamaḥ — tamas; ca — and; vikāraḥ — transformation, ahaṅkāra; mahān — mahat; pradhānam — primordial nature.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tad eva āha — he states that very thing; ātma-svarūpe — in the reality of the Self; kālaḥ — time; na prabhuḥ — is not master; kim api kartum na samarthaḥ — unable to do anything; animiṣām devānām — of the sleepless gods; paraḥ prabhuḥ — supreme master; kutaḥ nu devāḥ prabhaveyuḥ — how then could the gods have power; deva-niyamyānām — of those ruled by the gods; jagatām prāṇinām — living beings; nirupādhikatvāt — because of being without limiting adjuncts; jagat-kāraṇāni api — even the causes of the universe; sṛṣṭi-ādau — in creation and the rest; vikāraḥ — transformation; ahaṅkāraḥ — ego.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
na yatra kālo ’nimiṣāṁ paraḥ prabhuḥ
kuto nu devā jagatāṁ ya īśire
na yatra sattvaṁ na rajas tamaś ca
na vai vikāro na mahān pradhānam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evāha—na yatreti. yatra ātma-svarūpe kālo na prabhuḥ, kim api kartuṁ na samarthaḥ. ata eva devā na prabhava ity āha. animiṣāṁ devānāṁ paraḥ prabhuḥ kālo ’pi yatra na prabhuḥ tatra kuto nu devāḥ prabhaveyuḥ. deva-niyamyānāṁ tu jagatāṁ prāṇināṁ kā vārtā ity āha—jagatām iti. kuto na prabhavantīti apekṣāyāṁ nirupādhikatvād ity āha. yad vā jagat-kāraṇāny api na yasya sṛṣṭyādau prabhavantīty āha—na yatreti. vikāro ’haṅkāraḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदेवाऽऽह—न यत्रेति । यत्र आत्मस्वरूपे कालो न प्रभुः, किमपि कर्तुं न समर्थः । अत एव देवा न प्रभव इत्याह । अनिमिषां देवानां परः प्रभुः कालोऽपि यत्र न प्रभुः तत्र कुतो नु देवाः प्रभवेयुः । देवनियम्यानां तु जगतां प्राणिनां का वार्ता इत्याह—जगतामिति । कुतो न प्रभवन्तीति अपेक्षायां निरुपाधिकत्वादित्याह । यद्वा जगत्कारणान्यपि न यस्य सृष्ट्यादौ प्रभवन्तीत्याह—न यत्रेति । विकारोऽहङ्कारः ॥ १७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states that very thing with “where not.” In that reality of the Self, time is not master; it is unable to do anything. Therefore he says that the gods have no power. If even time, the supreme master of the sleepless gods, has no power there, how could the gods have power? What then need be said of living beings ruled by those gods? In answer to why they have no power, it is because that state is without limiting adjuncts. Or he says “where not” to show that even the causes of the universe do not exercise power over it in creation and the rest. “Vikāra” means ahaṅkāra.

ŚB 2.2.18

परं पदं वैष्णवमामनन्ति तद्
यन्नेति नेतीत्यतदुत्सिसृक्षवः ।
विसृज्य दौरात्म्यमनन्यसौहृदा
हृदोपगुह्यार्हपदं पदे पदे ॥ १८ ॥
Those who wish to cast away what is other than That through “not this, not this,” abandoning identification of the self with what is not the Self and having friendship with no other, declare that to be the supreme Vaiṣṇava state, embracing in the heart at every moment the feet of the Worshipful One.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
param — supreme; padam — state, station; vaiṣṇavam — belonging to Viṣṇu; āmananti — they declare, teach; tat — that; yat — because, which; na iti na iti — “not this, not this”; atat — what is other than That; utsisṛkṣavaḥ — wishing to cast away; visṛjya — abandoning; daurātmyam — taking non-Self as Self; ananya-sauhṛdāḥ — having friendship with no other; hṛdā — with the heart; upaguhya — embracing; arha-padam — feet of the Worshipful One; pade pade — at every step, every moment.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nirupādhikatvam — freedom from limiting adjuncts; kutaḥ — whence; ātma-vyatiriktam — other than the Self; neti neti — “not this, not this”; utsraṣṭum icchavaḥ — wishing to cast away; daurātmyam — identification with non-Self; deha-ādi-ātmatvam — taking body and the rest as Self; visṛjya — abandoning; arhasya pūjyasya — of the worshipful one; śrī-viṣṇoḥ padam — feet of Śrī Viṣṇu; pade pade — at every step; kṣaṇe kṣaṇe — every moment; hṛdā upāśliṣya — embracing with the heart; na anyasmin sauhṛdam — friendship with no other; vaiṣṇavam padam — Vaiṣṇava state; param — supreme; sarvataḥ śreṣṭham — highest of all.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
paraṁ padaṁ vaiṣṇavam āmananti tad
yan neti netīty atad utsisṛkṣavaḥ
visṛjya daurātmyam ananya-sauhṛdā
hṛdopaguhyārha-padaṁ pade pade

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nirupādhikatvaṁ kuta ity ata āha—param iti. yat yasmād etad ātma-vyatiriktaṁ “neti neti” ity evam utsraṣṭum icchavo daurātmyaṁ dehādy-ātmatvaṁ visṛjya, arhasya pūjyasya śrī-viṣṇoḥ padaṁ pade pade kṣaṇe kṣaṇe hṛdā upāśliṣya, na anyasmin sauhṛdaṁ yeṣāṁ tathā-bhūtāḥ santaḥ, tad vaiṣṇavaṁ padam ata eva paraṁ sarvataḥ śreṣṭham āmanantīty anvayaḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
निरुपाधिकत्वं कुत इत्यत आह—परमिति । यत् यस्मादेतदात्मव्यतिरिक्तं “नेति नेति” इत्येवमुत्स्रष्टुमिच्छवो दौरात्म्यं देहाद्यात्मत्वं विसृज्य, अर्हस्य पूज्यस्य श्रीविष्णोः पदं पदे पदे क्षणे क्षणे हृदा उपाश्लिष्य, न अन्यस्मिन् सौहृदं येषां तथाभूताः सन्तः, तद्वैष्णवं पदम् अत एव परं सर्वतः श्रेष्ठमामनन्तीत्यन्वयः ॥ १८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Whence is its freedom from limiting adjuncts? To this he says “supreme.” Because those who desire to cast away what is other than the Self through “not this, not this,” abandoning the taking of the body and the rest as the Self, embracing with the heart at every moment the feet of Śrī Viṣṇu, the Worshipful One, and having friendship with no other, declare that Vaiṣṇava state to be supreme, highest of all.

ŚB 2.2.19

इत्थं मुनिस्तूपरमेद् व्यवस्थितो
विज्ञानदृग्वीर्यसुरन्धिताशयः ।
स्वपार्ष्णिनापीड्य गुदं ततोऽनिलं
स्थानेषु षट्सून्नमयेज्जितक्लमः ॥ १९ ॥
Thus established, the sage should withdraw; his latent desires thoroughly burned by the power of the vision born of knowledge. Pressing the anus with his own heel, he should then raise the vital air through the six stations, having overcome fatigue.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ittham — thus; muniḥ — sage; tu — but; uparamet — should withdraw, cease; vyavasthitaḥ — firmly established; vijñāna-dṛk — vision born of knowledge; vīrya — power; su-randhita — thoroughly burned; āśayaḥ — latent disposition, desire; sva-pārṣṇinā — with one’s own heel; āpīḍya — pressing; gudam — anus; tataḥ — then; anilam — vital air; sthāneṣu — through stations; ṣaṭsu — six; unnamayet — should raise; jita-klamaḥ — having conquered fatigue.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tasmāt — therefore; ittham — thus; brahmatvena vyavasthitaḥ — established in Brahman; muniḥ — sage; uparamet — should withdraw; tu-śabdena — by the word “tu”; sa-kāmataḥ viśeṣaḥ — distinction from one with desire; vijñānam — knowledge-source; śāstram — scripture; tena jātā dṛk — vision born from it; jñānam — knowledge; tasyāḥ vīryam — its power; balam — strength; tena — by it; nirdagdhāḥ — thoroughly burned; āśayāḥ — latent dispositions; viṣaya-vāsanāḥ — desires for objects; deha-tyāga-prakāram — method of abandoning the body; sva-pārṣṇinā — by one’s own heel; pāda-mūlena — base of the foot; gudam — anus; mūlādhāram — mūlādhāra; āpīḍya nirudhya — pressing and closing; anilam — vital air; prāṇam — prāṇa; unnamayet — should raise; ūrdhvam nayet — lead upward; ṣaṭsu sthāneṣu — through six stations; nābhi-ādiṣu — beginning with the navel.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
itthaṁ munis tūparamed vyavasthito
vijñāna-dṛg-vīrya-surandhitāśayaḥ
sva-pārṣṇināpīḍya gudaṁ tato ’nilaṁ
sthāneṣu ṣaṭsūnnamayej jita-klamaḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ittham iti. tasmād itthaṁ brahmatvena vyavasthito munir uparamet. tu-śabdena “yadi prayāsyan” iti vakṣyamāṇāt sa-kāmato viśeṣa uktaḥ. tam āha—vijñāyate aneneti vijñānaṁ śāstraṁ, tena jātā dṛk jñānaṁ, tasyā vīryaṁ balaṁ, tena su-randhitāḥ nirdagdhā āśayā viṣaya-vāsanāḥ yasya saḥ. idānīṁ tasya deha-tyāga-prakāram āha. sva-pārṣṇinā pāda-mūlena gudaṁ mūlādhāram āpīḍya nirudhya, anilaṁ prāṇam unnamayed ūrdhvaṁ nayet. jitaḥ klamo yena. ṣaṭsu sthāneṣu nābhyādiṣu.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इत्थमिति । तस्मादित्थं ब्रह्मत्वेन व्यवस्थितो मुनिरुपरमेत् । तुशब्देन “यदि प्रयास्यन्” इति वक्ष्यमाणात् सकामतो विशेष उक्तः । तमाह—विज्ञायते अनेनेति विज्ञानं शास्त्रं, तेन जाता दृक् ज्ञानं, तस्या वीर्यं बलं, तेन सुरन्धिताः निर्दग्धा आशया विषयवासनाः यस्य सः । इदानीं तस्य देहत्यागप्रकारमाह । स्वपार्ष्णिना पादमूलेन गुदं मूलाधारमापीड्य निरुध्य, अनिलं प्राणमुन्नमयेदूर्ध्वं नयेत् । जितः क्लमो येन । षट्सु स्थानेषु नाभ्यादिषु ॥ १९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Therefore the sage established in this way in Brahman should withdraw. The word “tu” distinguishes him from the one with desire described later. “Vijñāna” is scripture, that by which something is known; from it arises vision, knowledge, and by the power of that vision his latent desires for objects are thoroughly burned. Now he states the method of abandoning the body: pressing and closing the anus, the mūlādhāra, with his own heel, he should raise the vital air upward through the six stations beginning with the navel.

ŚB 2.2.20

नाभ्यां स्थितं हृद्यधिरोप्य तस्मा-
दुदानगत्योरसि तं नयेन्मुनिः ।
ततोऽनुसन्धाय धिया मनस्वी
स्वतालुमूलं शनकैर्नयेत् ॥ २० ॥
The sage should place the vital air situated at the navel into the heart; from there, by the movement of udāna, he should lead it into the chest. Then, carefully directing it with intelligence, the self-controlled one should slowly lead it to the root of his palate.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
nābhyām — at the navel; sthitam — situated; hṛdi — in the heart; adhiropya — placing, raising; tasmāt — from there; udāna-gatyā — by the movement of udāna; urasi — in the chest; tam — it, the vital air; nayet — should lead; muniḥ — sage; tataḥ — then; anusandhāya — directing carefully; dhiyā — with intelligence; manasvī — self-controlled; sva-tālu-mūlam — root of his own palate; śanakaiḥ — slowly; nayeta — should lead.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
nābhyām — at the navel; maṇipūrake — in the maṇipūra; sthitam prāṇam — the vital air situated; hṛdi — in the heart; anāhata-cakre — in the anāhata cakra; adhiropya — placing; tasmāt — from there; udāna-gatyā — by the movement of udāna; udānasya sañcāra-krameṇa — by the course of udāna; urasi — in the chest; kaṇṭhāt adho-deśe — below the throat; viśuddhi-cakre — in the viśuddhi cakra; nayet — should lead; tataḥ — then; anusandhāya dhiyā — carefully directing with intelligence; manasvī — self-controlled; jita-cittaḥ — with mind mastered; sva-tālu-mūlam — root of his palate; cakrasya agra-pradeśam — upper region of that cakra; śanakaiḥ — slowly; bahudhā-gamana-sambhavāt — because movement in many directions is possible.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
nābhyāṁ sthitaṁ hṛdy adhiropya tasmād
udāna-gatyorasi taṁ nayen muniḥ
tato ’nusandhāya dhiyā manasvī
sva-tālu-mūlaṁ śanakair nayeta

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
nābhyām iti. nābhyāṁ maṇipūrake sthitaṁ prāṇaṁ hṛdy anāhata-cakre ’dhiropya, tasmād udāna-gatyā udānasya sañcāra-krameṇa urasi kaṇṭhād adho-deśe sthite viśuddhi-cakre taṁ nayet. tato ’nusandhāya dhiyā manasvī jita-cittaḥ sva-tālu-mūlaṁ tasyaiva cakrasyāgra-pradeśaṁ śanakair nayet. tato bahudhā-gamana-sambhavāt śanakair ity uktam.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नाभ्यामिति । नाभ्यां मणिपूरके स्थितं प्राणं हृद्यनाहतचक्रेऽधिरोप्य, तस्मादुदानगत्या उदानस्य सञ्चारक्रमेण उरसि कण्ठादधोदेशे स्थिते विशुद्धिचक्रे तं नयेत् । ततोऽनुसन्धाय धिया मनस्वी जितचित्तः स्वतालुमूलं तस्यैव चक्रस्याग्रप्रदेशं शनकैर्नयेत् । ततो बहुधागमनसम्भवात् शनकैरित्युक्तम् ॥ २० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having placed the vital air situated at the navel, in the maṇipūra, into the heart, the anāhata-cakra, he should lead it from there by the movement of udāna into the viśuddhi-cakra situated in the chest below the throat. Then, carefully directing it with intelligence, the self-controlled one should lead it slowly to the root of his palate, the upper region of that same cakra. Because movement in many directions is possible from there, “slowly” is specified.

ŚB 2.2.21

तस्माद् भ्रुवोरन्तरमुन्नयेत
निरुद्धसप्तायतनोऽनपेक्षः ।
स्थित्वा मुहूर्तार्धमकुण्ठदृष्टि-
र्निर्भिद्य मूर्धन् विसृजेत्परं गतः ॥ २१ ॥
From there he should raise it to the space between the eyebrows. Having blocked the seven openings and being without expectation, he should remain there for half a muhūrta; with unobstructed vision, having reached the Supreme, he should pierce the crown and release the body and senses.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tasmāt — from there; bhruvoḥ antaram — space between the eyebrows; unnayeta — should raise; niruddha-sapta-āyatanaḥ — having blocked the seven openings; anapekṣaḥ — without expectation; sthitvā — remaining; muhūrta-ardham — half a muhūrta; akuṇṭha-dṛṣṭiḥ — with unobstructed vision; nirbhidya — piercing; mūrdhan — at the crown; visṛjet — should release; param — Supreme; gataḥ — having reached.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tasmāt — from there; bhruvoḥ antaram — space between the eyebrows; ājñā-cakram — ājñā cakra; niruddhāni — blocked; indriyāṇi — sense openings; śrotre — two ears; netre — two eyes; nāsike — two nostrils; mukham — mouth; sapta-āyatanāni — seven openings; prāṇa-mārgāḥ — paths of the vital air; anapekṣaḥ — without expectation; param brahma gataḥ — having reached supreme Brahman; mūrdhani — at the crown; brahma-randhram — brahmarandhra; nirbhidya — piercing; deham indriyāṇi ca — body and senses; visṛjet — should release; saptamī-lopaḥ — omission of locative ending; ārṣaḥ — archaic usage.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tasmād bhruvor antaram unnayeta
niruddha-saptāyatano ’napekṣaḥ
sthitvā muhūrtārdham akuṇṭha-dṛṣṭir
nirbhidya mūrdhan visṛjet paraṁ gataḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tasmād iti. tasmād bhruvor antaram ājñā-cakram. niruddhāni indriyāṇi śrotre netre nāsike mukhaṁ cety evaṁ saptāyatanāni prāṇa-mārgā yena saḥ. anapekṣaḥ san paraṁ brahma-gataḥ san mūrdhan mūrdhani—atra saptamī-lopa ārṣaḥ—brahma-randhraṁ nirbhidya deham indriyāṇi ca visṛjet.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तस्मादिति । तस्माद् भ्रुवोरन्तरमाज्ञाचक्रम् । निरुद्धानि इन्द्रियाणि श्रोत्रे नेत्रे नासिके मुखं चेत्येवं सप्तायतनानि प्राणमार्गा येन सः । अनपेक्षः सन् परं ब्रह्मगतः सन् मूर्धन् मूर्धनि—अत्र सप्तमीलोप आर्षः—ब्रह्मरन्ध्रं निर्भिद्य देहमिन्द्रियाणि च विसृजेत् ॥ २१ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The space between the eyebrows is the ājñā-cakra. The seven openings, the paths of the vital air, are the two ears, two eyes, two nostrils and mouth. Being without expectation and having reached supreme Brahman, he should pierce the brahmarandhra at the crown and release the body and senses. In “mūrdhan,” the omission of the locative ending is an archaic usage.

ŚB 2.2.22

यदि प्रयास्यन् नृप पारमेष्ठ्यं
वैहायसानामुत यद् विहारम् ।
अष्टाधिपत्यं गुणसन्निवाये
सहैव गच्छेन्मनसेन्द्रियैश्च ॥ २२ ॥
But, O King, if he intends to go to the station of Parameṣṭhin, or to the playground of the sky-traveling Siddhas, or to the eightfold mastery within the aggregate of the guṇas, then he should go together with the mind and senses.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yadi — if; prayāsyan — intending to go; nṛpa — O King; pārameṣṭhyam — station of Parameṣṭhin, Brahmā; vaihāyasānām — of sky-travelers; uta — or; yat — which; vihāram — playground, place of enjoyment; aṣṭa-ādhipatyam — eightfold mastery, the eight siddhis; guṇa-sannivāye — within the aggregate of the guṇas, the cosmos; saha eva — together indeed; gacchet — should go; manasā — with the mind; indriyaiḥ — with the senses; ca — and.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sadyo-muktim uktvā — having stated immediate liberation; krama-mukti-prakāram — method of gradual liberation; āha — he states; daśabhiḥ — in ten verses; pārameṣṭhyam padam — station of Parameṣṭhin; brahmaṇaḥ sthānam — Brahmā’s abode; vaihāyasānām — of sky-travelers; khecarāṇām siddhānām — sky-moving Siddhas; vihāram — playground; krīḍā-sthānam — place of enjoyment; aṣṭau ādhipatyāni — eight masteries; aṇimā-ādi-aiśvaryāṇi — powers beginning with aṇimā; guṇa-sannivāye — in the aggregate of guṇas; brahmāṇḍe sarvatra — throughout the cosmic egg; deha-tyāga-avasare — at time of abandoning body; manaḥ ca indriyāṇi ca — mind and senses; na tyajet — should not abandon; taiḥ sarvaiḥ saha — together with all of them; tat-tat-loka-bhoga-artham — for enjoyment in those worlds; gacchet — should go.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yadi prayāsyan nṛpa pārameṣṭhyaṁ
vaihāyasānām uta yad vihāram
aṣṭādhipatyaṁ guṇa-sannivāye
sahaiva gacchen manasendriyaiś ca

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
sadyo-muktim uktvā krama-mukti-prakāram āha—yadīti daśabhiḥ. yadi tu pārameṣṭhyaṁ padaṁ brahmaṇaḥ sthānaṁ prayāsyan bhavati, uta vaihāyasānāṁ khecarāṇāṁ siddhānāṁ yad vihāraṁ krīḍā-sthānam. kīdṛśam? aṣṭāv ādhipatyāny aṇimādy-aiśvaryāṇi yasmin tad api prayāsyan. guṇa-sannivāye guṇa-samudāya-rūpe brahmāṇḍe sarvatra ity arthaḥ. tarhi deha-tyāgāvasare manaś cendriyāṇi ca na tyajet, kiṁtu taiḥ sarvais tat-tal-loka-bhogārthaṁ gacchet.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
सद्योमुक्तिमुक्त्वा क्रममुक्तिप्रकारमाह—यदीति दशभिः । यदि तु पारमेष्ठ्यं पदं ब्रह्मणः स्थानं प्रयास्यन् भवति, उत वैहायसानां खेचराणां सिद्धानां यद्विहारं क्रीडास्थानम् । कीदृशम् ? अष्टावाधिपत्यान्यणिमाद्यैश्वर्याणि यस्मिन् तदपि प्रयास्यन् । गुणसन्निवाये गुणसमुदायरूपे ब्रह्माण्डे सर्वत्र इत्यर्थः । तर्हि देहत्यागावसरे मनश्चेन्द्रियाणि च न त्यजेत्, किन्तु तैः सर्वैस्तत्तल्लोकभोगार्थं गच्छेत् ॥ २२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having stated immediate liberation, he states the method of gradual liberation in the ten verses beginning “if.” If he intends to go to Brahmā’s station, to the playground of the sky-traveling Siddhas, or elsewhere throughout the cosmic egg where the eight masteries such as aṇimā are available, then at the time of abandoning the body he should not abandon mind and senses; he should go together with them for enjoyment in those respective worlds.

ŚB 2.2.23

योगेश्वराणां गतिमाहुरन्त-
र्बहिस्त्रिलोक्याः पवनान्तरात्मनाम् ।
न कर्मभिस्तां गतिमाप्नुवन्ति
विद्यातपोयोगसमाधिभाजाम् ॥ २३ ॥
They declare that the masters of yoga, whose subtle body has the wind within it, move both within and beyond the three worlds. Those who possess knowledge, austerity, yoga and samādhi do not attain that movement by ritual actions.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
yoga-īśvarāṇām — of masters of yoga; gatim — movement, course; āhuḥ — they declare; antaḥ — within; bahiḥ — outside, beyond; tri-lokyāḥ — the three worlds; pavana-antar-ātmanām — whose inner body has wind as its interior; na — not; karmabhiḥ — by ritual actions; tām — that; gatim — movement; āpnuvanti — attain; vidyā — knowledge, worship; tapaḥ — austerity; yoga — yoga; samādhi — samādhi; bhājām — of those who possess, practice.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
karma-gati-vat — like the course attained by ritual action; na yoga-gatiḥ paricchinnā — yoga’s course is not limited; yoga-īśvarāṇām — of masters of yoga; tri-lokyāḥ antaḥ-bahiḥ — within and beyond the three worlds; mahar-loka-ādiṣu — in Maharloka and the others; brahmāṇḍāt bahiḥ — beyond the cosmic egg; gatim āhuḥ — they declare movement; pavanasya antar-ātmā — whose inner self is wind; liṅga-śarīram — subtle body; vidyā — worship; upāsanā — worship; tapaḥ — austerity; bhagavad-dharmaḥ — dharma of Bhagavān; yogaḥ — yoga; aṣṭāṅgaḥ — eight-limbed; samādhiḥ — samādhi; jñānam — knowledge; karma-bhiḥ na āpnuvanti — not attained by ritual actions.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
yogeśvarāṇāṁ gatim āhur antar-
bahis-tri-lokyāḥ pavanāntar-ātmanām
na karmabhis tāṁ gatim āpnuvanti
vidyā-tapo-yoga-samādhi-bhājām

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
yataḥ karma-gativan na yoga-gatiḥ paricchinnety āha—yogeśvarāṇām iti. yogeśvarāṇāṁ tri-lokyā antar-bahiś ca mahar-lokādiṣu brahmāṇḍād bahiś ca gatim āhuḥ. atra hetuḥ—pavanasyāntar-ātmā liṅga-śarīraṁ yeṣām iti. vidyā upāsanā, tapo bhagavad-dharmaḥ, yogo ’ṣṭāṅgaḥ, samādhir jñānam; tān ye bhajanti teṣāṁ yā gatis tāṁ karmabhir nāpnuvanti.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यतः कर्मगतिवन्न योगगति परिच्छिन्नेत्याह—योगेश्वराणामिति । योगेश्वराणां त्रिलोक्या अन्तर्बहिश्च महर्लोकादिषु ब्रह्माण्डाद्बहिश्च गतिमाहुः । अत्र हेतुः—पवनस्यान्तरात्मा लिङ्गशरीरं येषामिति । विद्या उपासना, तपो भगवद्धर्मः, योगोऽष्टाङ्गः, समाधिर्ज्ञानम्; तान् ये भजन्ति तेषां या गतिस्तां कर्मभिर्नाप्नुवन्ति ॥ २३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Because the course attained by yoga is not limited like the course attained by ritual action, he says “of the masters of yoga.” Their movement is within the three worlds, in Maharloka and the others, and beyond the cosmic egg. The reason is that their inner self is the wind—that is, the subtle body. “Vidyā” is worship; “tapas” is the dharma of Bhagavān; “yoga” is the eight-limbed discipline; and “samādhi” is knowledge. Their course is not attained by ritual actions.

ŚB 2.2.24

वैश्वानरं याति विहायसा गतः
सुषुम्णया ब्रह्मपथेन शोचिषा ।
विधूतकल्कोऽथ हरेरुदस्तात्
प्रयाति चक्रं नृप शैशुमारम् ॥ २४ ॥
Having gone through the sky by the luminous Suṣumṇā, the path to Brahman, he reaches Vaiśvānara. With impurity shaken off, he then, O King, proceeds higher to Hari’s Śiśumāra circle.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
vaiśvānaram — Vaiśvānara, the fire deity; yāti — reaches; vihāyasā — through the sky; gataḥ — having gone; suṣumṇayā — by the Suṣumṇā; brahma-pathena — by the path to Brahman; śociṣā — luminous, shining; vidhūta-kalkaḥ — with impurity shaken off; atha — then; hareḥ — of Hari; udastāt — higher, above; prayāti — proceeds; cakram — circle; nṛpa — O King; śaiśumāram — Śiśumāra.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tām āha — he states that course; aṣṭabhiḥ — in eight verses; vihāyasā — through the sky; ākāśena — through space; brahma-loka-pathena — by the path to Brahmaloka; gataḥ — traveling; prathamam — first; vaiśvānaram — Vaiśvānara; agni-abhimāninīm devatām — deity presiding over fire; yāti — reaches; suṣumṇā nāḍyā — by Suṣumṇā nāḍī; dehāt bahiḥ api vitatā — extending even outside the body; śociṣā — luminous; jyotir-mayā — made of light; vidhūta-kalkaḥ — impurity shaken off; tyaktaḥ malaḥ — stain cast away; kvāpi asajjamānaḥ — attached nowhere; udastāt — higher up; hareḥ sambandhi tārā-rūpam — Hari’s star-form; nārāyaṇa-adhiṣṭhitam — presided over by Nārāyaṇa; śaiśumāram cakram — Śiśumāra circle; jyotiḥ-cakram — wheel of luminaries; āditya-ādi-dhruva-antāni padāni — stations from sun through Dhruva; prayāti — proceeds.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
vaiśvānaraṁ yāti vihāyasā gataḥ
suṣumṇayā brahma-pathena śociṣā
vidhūta-kalko ’tha harer udastāt
prayāti cakraṁ nṛpa śaiśumāram

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tām āha—vaiśvānaram ity aṣṭabhiḥ. vihāyasā ākāśena. tatra ca brahma-loka-pathena gataḥ san prathamaṁ vaiśvānaram agny-abhimāninīṁ devatāṁ yāti. kenopāyena? suṣumṇā nāḍyā. sā ca dehād bahir api vitatāstīty āha—śociṣā jyotir-mayyā. vidhūta-kalko vidhūtas tyaktaḥ kalko malo yena saḥ, kvāpy asajjamāna ity arthaḥ. udastād upariṣṭād vartamānaṁ hareḥ sambandhi tārā-rūpaṁ nārāyaṇādhiṣṭhitam ity arthaḥ, śaiśumāraṁ cakraṁ pañcama-skandhe vakṣyamāṇaṁ śiśumārākāraṁ jyotiś-cakram. cakra-sthāny ādityādi-dhruvāntāni padāni prayātīty arthaḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तामाह—वैश्वानरमित्यष्टभिः । विहायसा आकाशेन । तत्र च ब्रह्मलोकपथेन गतः सन् प्रथमं वैश्वानरमग्न्यभिमानिनीं देवतां याति । केनोपायेन ? सुषुम्णा नाड्या । सा च देहाद्बहिरपि वितताऽस्तीत्याह—शोचिषा ज्योतिर्मय्या । विधूतकल्को विधूतस्त्यक्तः कल्को मलो येन सः, क्वाप्यसज्जमान इत्यर्थः । उदस्तादुपरिष्टाद्वर्तमानं हरेः सम्बन्धि तारारूपं नारायणाधिष्ठितमित्यर्थः, शैशुमारं चक्रं पञ्चमस्कन्धे वक्ष्यमाणं शिशुमाराकारं ज्योतिश्चक्रम् । चक्रस्थान्यादित्यादिध्रुवान्तानि पदानि प्रयातीत्यर्थः ॥ २४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states that course with the eight verses beginning “Vaiśvānara.” Traveling through space on the path to Brahmaloka, he first reaches Vaiśvānara, the deity presiding over fire, by the Suṣumṇā nāḍī, which extends even outside the body and is luminous. “With impurity shaken off” means attached nowhere. Higher up is Hari’s star-form, presided over by Nārāyaṇa: the Śiśumāra circle, the wheel of luminaries in the form of a śiśumāra. He proceeds through its stations from the sun up to Dhruva.

ŚB 2.2.25

तद् विश्वनाभिं त्वतिवर्त्य विष्णो-
रणीयसा विरजेनात्मनैकः ।
नमस्कृतं ब्रह्मविदामुपैति
कल्पायुषो यद् विबुधा रमन्ते ॥ २५ ॥
Having crossed that navel of the universe belonging to Viṣṇu, alone, with a subtler stainless self, he reaches the place revered by knowers of Brahman, where the wise of a kalpa-long life delight.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tat — that; viśva-nābhim — navel of the universe; tu — then, but; ativartya — having crossed beyond; viṣṇoḥ — of Viṣṇu; aṇīyasā — with the subtler; virajena — stainless; ātmanā — by the self, subtle body; ekaḥ — alone; namaskṛtam — revered, saluted; brahma-vidām — by knowers of Brahman; upaiti — reaches; kalpa-āyuṣaḥ — having a kalpa as lifespan; yat — where; vibudhāḥ — the wise; ramante — delight.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tat — that; viṣṇoḥ cakram — wheel of Viṣṇu; viśvasya nābhi — navel of the universe; sūrya-ādi-āśraya-bhūtam — serving as the support of the sun and others; āviṣṭa-liṅgatvāt — because of the grammatical gender of the compound; nābhi-śabdasya — of the word nābhi; na klībatvam — not neuter; ativartya — having passed beyond; atikramya — having crossed; parataḥ — beyond; svargiṇām gati-abhāvāt — because heavenly beings have no further course; ekaḥ eva — alone; nirmalena liṅga-śarīreṇa — with a stainless subtle body; brahma-vidām sthānam — abode of knowers of Brahman; anyaiḥ namaskṛtam — revered by others; mahar-lokam upaiti — reaches Maharloka; yat yasmin — where; vibudhāḥ — wise ones; kalpa-āyuṣaḥ — living for a kalpa; bhṛgu-ādayaḥ — Bhṛgu and others.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tad viśva-nābhiṁ tv ativartya viṣṇor
aṇīyasā virajenātmanaikaḥ
namaskṛtaṁ brahma-vidām upaiti
kalpāyuṣo yad vibudhā ramante

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad iti. tad viṣṇoś cakraṁ viśvasya nābhi sūryādy-āśraya-bhūtam. āviṣṭa-liṅgatvān nābhi-śabdasya na klībatvam. ativartya atikramya, parataḥ svargiṇāṁ gaty-abhāvāt. eka eva nirmalena liṅga-śarīreṇa brahma-vidāṁ sthānam anyair namaskṛtaṁ mahar-lokam upaiti. yat yasmin vibudhāḥ kalpāyuṣo bhṛgv-ādayaḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
