तदिति । तद् विष्णोश्चक्रं विश्वस्य नाभि सूर्याद्याश्रयभूतम् । आविष्टलिङ्गत्वान्नाभिशब्दस्य न क्लीबत्वम् । अतिवर्त्य अतिक्रम्य, परतः स्वर्गिणां गत्यभावात् । एक एव निर्मलेन लिङ्गशरीरेण ब्रह्मविदां स्थानमन्यैर्नमस्कृतं महर्लोकमुपैति । यत् यस्मिन् विबुधाः कल्पायुषो भृग्वादयः ॥ २५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. That is Viṣṇu’s wheel, the navel of the universe, serving as the support of the sun and the others. Having crossed beyond it, since heavenly beings have no further course, he alone, with a stainless subtle body, reaches Maharloka, the abode of knowers of Brahman and revered by others, where Bhṛgu and other wise ones live for a kalpa.

ŚB 2.2.26

अथो अनन्तस्य मुखानलेन
दन्दह्यमानं स निरीक्ष्य विश्वम् ।
निर्याति सिद्धेश्वरजुष्टधिष्ण्यं
यद् द्वैपरार्ध्यं तदु पारमेष्ठ्यम् ॥ २६ ॥
Then, seeing the universe being intensely burned by the fire from Ananta’s mouth, he departs for that supreme station of Parameṣṭhin which lasts for two parārdhas and whose abodes are frequented by the lords of siddhi.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
atho — then, thereafter; anantasya — of Ananta; mukha-analena — by the fire of the mouth; dandahyamānam — being intensely burned; saḥ — he; nirīkṣya — seeing; viśvam — the universe; niryāti — departs; siddha-īśvara-juṣṭa-dhiṣṇyam — whose stations are frequented by lords of siddhi; yat — which; dvai-parārdhyam — lasting for two parārdhas; tat — that; u — indeed; pārameṣṭhyam — station of Parameṣṭhin, Brahmā.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
atho — thereafter; anantaram — immediately after; kalpa-ante — at the end of the kalpa; saḥ — he; viśvam — the universe; trai-lokyam — the three worlds; atiśayena dandahyamānam — being intensely burned; nirīkṣya — seeing; tatra api ūṣma-prāpteḥ — because heat reaches even there; yat — which; dvi-parārdha-sthāyi — lasting two parārdhas; tat pārameṣṭhyam padam — that station of Parameṣṭhin; prati niryāti — departs toward; siddha-īśvaraiḥ — by lords of Siddhas; juṣṭāni — frequented; dhiṣṇyāni — stations; vimānāni — aerial vehicles; yasmin — in which; u — particle; śraiṣṭhyam sūcitam — indicates excellence.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
atho anantasya mukhānalena
dandahyamānaṁ sa nirīkṣya viśvam
niryāti siddheśvara-juṣṭa-dhiṣṇyaṁ
yad dvai-parārdhyaṁ tad u pārameṣṭhyam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
atho iti. atho anantaraṁ kalpānte sa viśvaṁ trai-lokyam atiśayena dandahyamānaṁ nirīkṣya, tatrāpy ūṣma-prāpteḥ, yad dvi-parārdha-sthāyi tat pārameṣṭhyaṁ padaṁ prati niryāti. siddheśvarair juṣṭāni dhiṣṇyāni vimānāni yasmin tat. u iti śraiṣṭhyaṁ sūcitam.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अथो इति । अथो अनन्तरं कल्पान्ते स विश्वं त्रैलोक्यमतिशयेन दन्दह्यमानं निरीक्ष्य, तत्राप्यूष्मप्राप्तेः, यद् द्विपरार्धस्थायि तत् पारमेष्ठ्यं पदं प्रति निर्याति । सिद्धेश्वरैर्जुष्टानि धिष्ण्यानि विमानानि यस्मिन् तत् । उ इति श्रैष्ठ्यं सूचितम् ॥ २६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Thereafter, at the end of the kalpa, seeing the three worlds intensely burned, because the heat reaches even there, he departs toward the station of Parameṣṭhin which lasts for two parārdhas. Its stations or aerial vehicles are frequented by lords among the Siddhas. The particle u indicates excellence.

ŚB 2.2.27

न यत्र शोको न जरा न मृत्यु-
र्नार्तिर्न चोद्वेग ऋते कुतश्चित् ।
यच्चित्ततोऽदः कृपयानिदंविदां
दुरन्तदुःखप्रभवानुदर्शनात् ॥ २७ ॥
There are neither grief, old age, death, pain nor fear there from any cause, except that pang of mind which arises from compassion on repeatedly seeing the birth of endless suffering among those who do not know this.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
na — not; yatra — where; śokaḥ — grief; jarā — old age; mṛtyuḥ — death; ārtiḥ — pain, distress; ca — and; udvegaḥ — fear, agitation; ṛte — except; kutaścit — from anything; yat — which; citta-todaḥ — pang of the mind; adaḥ — that; kṛpayā — through compassion; an-idam-vidām — for those who do not know this; duranta-duḥkha — endless suffering; prabhava — arising, birth; anudarśanāt — from repeatedly seeing.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tad eva āha — he states that very thing; ārtiḥ — pain; duḥkham — suffering; udvegaḥ — agitation; bhayam — fear; citta-todaḥ — pang of the mind; manaḥ-pīḍā — mental pain; an-idam-vidām — of those not knowing this; bhagavataḥ dhyānam — meditation on Bhagavān; ajānatām prāṇinām — of beings not knowing it; duranta-duḥkhaḥ — endless suffering; yaḥ prabhavaḥ — whose arising; janma — birth; tasya anudarśanāt — from seeing it repeatedly; teṣām kṛpayā — through compassion for them; brahma-lokam gatānām — of those reaching Brahmaloka; tri-vidhā gatiḥ — threefold course; puṇya-utkarṣeṇa gatāḥ — those arriving by great merit; adhikārikāḥ — office-holders; hiraṇyagarbha-ādi-upāsanā-balena — by strength of Hiraṇyagarbha worship; brahmaṇā saha mucyante — liberated with Brahmā; bhagavad-upāsakāḥ — worshipers of Bhagavān; svechayā — by their own will; brahmāṇḍam bhittvā — piercing the cosmic egg; vaiṣṇavam padam āruhanti — ascend to the Vaiṣṇava abode.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
na yatra śoko na jarā na mṛtyur
nārtir na codvega ṛte kutaścit
yac citta-todo ’daḥ kṛpayānidaṁ-vidāṁ
duranta-duḥkha-prabhavānudarśanāt

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evāha—na yatreti. ārtir duḥkham. udvego bhayam. kiṁtu citta-to hetor yad duḥkham ada ṛte tad ekaṁ vinā tat kuto bhavati? anidaṁ-vidām idaṁ bhagavato dhyānam ajānatāṁ prāṇināṁ duranta-duḥkho yaḥ prabhavo janma tasyānudarśanāt teṣāṁ kṛpayā. yad vā citta-todo manaḥ-pīḍeti yat tad vinā kutaścid api yatra śokādayo na santīti. tatra brahma-lokaṁ gatānāṁ tri-vidhā gatiḥ. ye puṇyotkarṣeṇa gatās te kalpāntare puṇya-tāratamyenādhikārikā bhavanti. ye tu hiraṇyagarbhādy-upāsanā-balena gatās te brahmaṇā saha mucyante. ye tu bhagavad-upāsakās te svecchayā brahmāṇḍaṁ bhittvā vaiṣṇavaṁ padam āruhanti.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदेवाह—न यत्रेति । आर्तिर्दुःखम् । उद्वेगो भयम् । किन्तु चित्ततो हेतोर्यद्दुःखमद ऋते तदेकं विना तत् कुतो भवति ? अनिदंविदामिदं भगवतो ध्यानमजानतां प्राणिनां दुरन्तदुःखो यः प्रभवो जन्म तस्यानुदर्शनात् तेषां कृपया । यद्वा चित्ततोदो मनःपीडेति यत् तद्विना कुतश्चिदपि यत्र शोकादयो न सन्तीति । तत्र ब्रह्मलोकं गतानां त्रिविधा गतिः । ये पुण्योत्कर्षेण गतास्ते कल्पान्तरे पुण्यतारतम्येनाधिकारिका भवन्ति । ये तु हिरण्यगर्भाद्युपासनाबलेन गतास्ते ब्रह्मणा सह मुच्यन्ते । ये तु भगवदुपासकास्ते स्वेच्छया ब्रह्माण्डं भित्त्वा वैष्णवं पदमारुहन्ति ॥ २७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Ārti” is suffering and “udvega” is fear. Apart from the pang of mind arising through compassion for beings who do not know this meditation on Bhagavān and repeatedly undergo births that produce endless suffering, grief and the other afflictions do not exist there. Those who reach Brahmaloka have three courses: some later receive offices according to merit; worshipers of Hiraṇyagarbha are liberated with Brahmā; worshipers of Bhagavān may pierce the cosmic egg and ascend to the Vaiṣṇava abode.

ŚB 2.2.28

ततो विशेषं प्रतिपद्य निर्भय-
स्तेनात्मनापोऽनलमूर्तिरत्वरन् ।
ज्योतिर्मयो वायुमुपेत्य काले
वाय्वात्मना खं बृहदात्मलिङ्गम् ॥ २८ ॥
Then, entering the particular earth element, fearless, with that earth-self he enters water; becoming fire-bodied and unhurried, becoming luminous he reaches air in due course, and with air as his self he reaches the great ether, marked by its own characteristic.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tataḥ — then; viśeṣam — the particular, earth element; pratipadya — having entered; nirbhayaḥ — fearless; tena ātmanā — with that self; āpaḥ — waters; anala-mūrtiḥ — having fire as form; atvaran — not hurrying; jyotiḥ-mayaḥ — luminous; vāyum — air; upetya — having reached; kāle — in due course; vāyu-ātmanā — with air as self; kham — ether; bṛhat — great; ātma-liṅgam — having its own distinctive mark.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
bhagavad-bhaktasya — of the devotee of Bhagavān; brahmāṇḍa-bhedana-prakāram — method of piercing the cosmic egg; āha — he states; prakṛteḥ — from prakṛti; īśvara-adhiṣṭhitāyāḥ — presided over by the Lord; mahattattvam — mahat principle; ahaṅkāraḥ — ego; śabda-tanmātra-dvārā — through the subtle element sound; nabhaḥ — ether; sparśa-tanmātra-dvārā — through touch; vāyuḥ — air; rūpa-tanmātra-dvārā — through form; tejaḥ — fire; rasa-tanmātra-dvārā — through taste; āpaḥ — waters; gandha-tanmātra-dvārā — through smell; pṛthivī — earth; caturdaśa-bhuvana-ātmakam — consisting of fourteen worlds; virāṭ-śarīram — Virāṭ body; pṛthivī — earth; prathama-āvaraṇam — first covering; uttara-uttaram daśa-guṇāni — each succeeding ten times greater; prakṛti-āvaraṇam — covering of prakṛti; vyāpakam — all-pervading; viśeṣam pratipadya — attaining the particular element; liṅga-dehena — with subtle body; pṛthivī-ātmatām prāpya — attaining earth-nature; nirbhayaḥ — fearless; śaṅkā-śūnyaḥ — free from doubt; tena ātmanā — in that form; pṛthivī-rūpeṇa — in the form of earth; āpaḥ pratipadya — entering water; atvaran — not hurrying; kleśa-dāha-ādi-śaṅkā-abhāvāt — due to absence of fear of wetting or burning; jyotiḥ-mayaḥ — luminous; kāle — in due course; bhoga-avasāne — at the end of enjoyment; bṛhat-ātma-liṅgam — sign of the Great Self.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tato viśeṣaṁ pratipadya nirbhayas
tenātmanāpo ’nala-mūrtir atvaran
jyotir-mayo vāyum upetya kāle
vāyv-ātmanā khaṁ bṛhad-ātma-liṅgam

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tatra prastutasya bhagavad-bhaktasya brahmāṇḍa-bhedana-prakāram āha—tata ity ādinā. taittirīyaṁ prakriyā. īśvarādhiṣṭhitāyāḥ prakṛteḥ kenacid aṁśena mahat-tattvaṁ bhavati, tasyāṁśenāhaṅkāraḥ, tasyāṁśena śabda-tanmātra-dvārā nabhaḥ, tasyāṁśena sparśa-tanmātra-dvārā vāyuḥ, tasyāṁśena rūpa-tanmātra-dvārā tejaḥ, tasyāṁśena rasa-tanmātra-dvārā āpaḥ, tāsām aṁśena gandha-tanmātra-dvārā pṛthivī. taiś ca militaiś caturdaśa-bhuvanātmakaṁ virāṭ-śarīram. tasya pañcāśat-koṭi-yojana-viśālasya pṛthivy evāṇḍa-kaṭāha-śabda-vācyā koṭi-yojana-viśālaṁ prathamāvaraṇam. pañcāśat-koṭi-yojana-viśālam ity eke. tataś cābādīnāṁ ye pariṇatā aṁśās tāny evottarottaraṁ daśa-guṇāny āvaraṇāni. aṣṭamaṁ tu prakṛty-āvaraṇaṁ vyāpakam eva. tad evaṁ sthite pṛthivy-ādy-āvaraṇa-bhedana-prakāraḥ kathyate. tato viśeṣaṁ pratipadya liṅga-dehena pṛthivy-ātmatāṁ prāpyety arthaḥ. evam uttaratrāpi draṣṭavyam. nirbhayaḥ kathaṁ yāsyāmīti śaṅkā-śūnyaḥ. tenātmanā pṛthivī-rūpeṇa tan-nirantarā āpaḥ pratipadya. atvaran tvarām akurvan tat-tad-ātmakatvena kleda-dāhādi-śaṅkābhāvād yatheṣṭaṁ bhogān bhuñjāna ity arthaḥ. evaṁ jyotir-mayaḥ san kāle bhogāvasāne bṛhad-ātma-liṅgaṁ paramātma-mūrtitvenopāsaneṣu veda-śabdātmanā tasya pramāpakam iti.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र प्रस्तुतस्य भगवद्भक्तस्य ब्रह्माण्डभेदनप्रकारमाह—तत इत्यादिना । तैत्तिरीयं प्रक्रिया । ईश्वराधिष्ठितायाः प्रकृतेः केनचिदंशेन महत्तत्त्वं भवति, तस्यांशेनाहङ्कारः, तस्यांशेन शब्दतन्मात्रद्वारा नभः, तस्यांशेन स्पर्शतन्मात्रद्वारा वायुः, तस्यांशेन रूपतन्मात्रद्वारा तेजः, तस्यांशेन रसतन्मात्रद्वारा आपः, तासामंशेन गन्धतन्मात्रद्वारा पृथ्वी । तैश्च मिलितैश्चतुर्दशभुवनात्मकं विराट्शरीरम् । तस्य पञ्चाशत्कोटियोजनविशालस्य पृथिव्येवाण्डकटाहशब्दवाच्या कोटियोजनविशालं प्रथमावरणम् । पञ्चाशत्कोटियोजनविशालमित्येके । ततश्च अबादीनां ये परिणता अंशास्तान्येवोत्तरोत्तरं दशगुणान्यावरणानि । अष्टमं तु प्रकृत्यावरणं व्यापकमेव । तदेवं स्थिते पृथिव्याद्यावरणभेदनप्रकारः कथ्यते । ततो विशेषं प्रतिपद्य लिङ्गदेहेन पृथिव्यात्मतां प्राप्य इत्यर्थः । एवमुत्तरत्रापि द्रष्टव्यम् । निर्भयः कथं यास्यामीति शङ्काशून्यः । तेनात्मना पृथिवीरूपेण तन्निरन्तरा अपः प्रतिपद्य । अत्वरन् त्वरामकुर्वन् तत्तदात्मकत्वेन क्लेददाहादिशङ्काभावाद्यथेष्टं भोगान् भुञ्जान इत्यर्थः । एवं ज्योतिर्मयः सन् काले भोगावसाने बृहदात्मलिङ्गं परमात्ममूर्तित्वेनोपासनेषु वेदशब्दात्मना तस्य प्रमापकमिति ॥ २८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states how the devotee of Bhagavān pierces the cosmic coverings. From prakṛti presided over by the Lord arise in succession mahat, ahaṅkāra, ether through sound, air through touch, fire through form, water through taste, and earth through smell. Combined, these form the Virāṭ body of the fourteen worlds. Earth is the first covering; the succeeding coverings are progressively ten times greater, while the covering of prakṛti is all-pervading. “Attaining the particular” means attaining earth-nature with the subtle body; the same principle applies to what follows. Fearless, he enters the surrounding waters in the form of earth and proceeds without haste, free from fear of wetting, burning and the like. In due course, at the end of enjoyment, he reaches the element called the sign of the Great Self.

ŚB 2.2.29

घ्राणेन गन्धं रसनेन वै रसं
रूपं च दृष्ट्या श्वसनं त्वचैव ।
श्रोत्रेण चोपेत्य नभोगुणत्वं
प्राणेन चाकूतिमुपैति योगी ॥ २९ ॥
The yogin reaches smell through the faculty of smell, taste through the faculty of taste, form through sight, touch through the skin, the quality of ether—sound—through hearing, and intention through the active faculty.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ghrāṇena — by the faculty of smell; gandham — smell; rasanena — by the faculty of taste; vai — indeed; rasam — taste; rūpam — form; ca — and; dṛṣṭyā — by sight; śvasanam — touch; tvacā — by the skin; eva — indeed; śrotreṇa — by hearing; ca — and; upetya — having reached; nabhaḥ-guṇatvam — the quality of ether, sound; prāṇena — by the active faculty; ca — and; ākūtim — intention, action; upaiti — reaches; yogī — the yogin.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
indriya-arthānām — of sense objects; bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām — subtle elements; atikramam — crossing beyond; āha — he states; ghrāṇena adhiṣṭhitena — by the presiding smell-faculty; gandham upetya — reaching smell; śvasanam — “śvasana”; sparśanam — touch; nabhaḥ-guṇatvam — quality of ether; śabda-ātmatām — nature of sound; prāṇena — by prāṇa; tat-tat-karma-indriyeṇa — by the respective organ of action; ākūtim — intention/action; tat-tat-kriyām — corresponding action.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ghrāṇena gandhaṁ rasanena vai rasaṁ
rūpaṁ ca dṛṣṭyā śvasanaṁ tvacaiva
śrotreṇa copetya nabho-guṇatvaṁ
prāṇena cākūtim upaiti yogī

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
indriyārthānāṁ bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām atikramam āha—ghrāṇeneti. ghrāṇenādhiṣṭhitena gandham upetya, śvasanaṁ sparśanam, nabho-guṇatvaṁ śabdātmatām, prāṇena tat-tat-karmendriyeṇa ākūtiṁ tat-tat-kriyām.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इन्द्रियार्थानां भूतसूक्ष्माणामतिक्रममाह—घ्राणेनेति । घ्राणेनाधिष्ठितेन गन्धमुपेत्य, श्वसनं स्पर्शनम्, नभोगुणत्वं शब्दात्मताम्, प्राणेन तत्तत्कर्मेन्द्रियेण आकूतिं तत्तत्क्रियाम् ॥ २९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He states the crossing beyond the subtle elements that are the objects of the senses. By the presiding faculty of smell he reaches smell. “Śvasana” means touch. “The quality of ether” means the nature of sound. Through the respective organ of action he reaches its corresponding action.

ŚB 2.2.30

स भूतसूक्ष्मेन्द्रियसंनिकर्षं
मनोमयं देवमयं विकार्यम् ।
संसाद्य गत्या सह तेन याति
विज्ञानतत्त्वं गुणसंनिरोधम् ॥ ३० ॥
By that course the yogin reaches the mutable ahaṅkāra, the meeting-place of the subtle elements and senses, consisting of mind and deities; together with that he goes to the principle of vijñāna, and then to that in which the guṇas are restrained.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
saḥ — he; bhūta-sūkṣma — subtle elements; indriya — senses; sannikarṣam — meeting-place, place of dissolution; manaḥ-mayam — consisting of mind; deva-mayam — consisting of deities; vikāryam — mutable, ahaṅkāra; saṁsādya — having reached; gatyā — by the course; saha — together; tena — with it; yāti — goes; vijñāna-tattvam — principle of vijñāna, mahat; guṇa-sannirodham — that in which guṇas are restrained, pradhāna.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
sthūla-sūkṣma-bhūta-atikramam — crossing gross and subtle elements; uktvā — having stated; tat-āvaraṇa-bhūta-ahaṅkāra-prāptyā — through reaching ahaṅkāra, their covering; mahat-ādi-prāptim — attainment of mahat and others; āha — he states; sa yogī — that yogin; vikāryam — mutable principle; saṁsādya — having reached; vijñāna-tattvam yāti — goes to vijñāna principle; vividham kāryam yasya — having varied effects; ahaṅkāraḥ — ego; tri-vidhaḥ — threefold; tāmasaḥ — tamasic; rājasaḥ — rajasic; sāttvikaḥ — sattvic; tāmasāt — from tamasic; bhūta-sūkṣmāṇi — subtle elements; rājasāt — from rajasic; daśa-indriyāṇi — ten senses; sāttvikāt — from sattvic; manaḥ indriya-devatāḥ — mind and sense-deities; layaḥ — dissolution; tat-tat-ahaṅkāre — into respective ahaṅkāra; sannikarṣam — meeting place; laya-sthānam — place of dissolution; tena ahaṅkāreṇa saha — together with ahaṅkāra; vijñāna-tattvam — mahat; tataḥ — then; guṇānām sannirodhaḥ — restraint/dissolution of guṇas; pradhānam — pradhāna.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
sa bhūta-sūkṣmendriya-sannikarṣaṁ
mano-mayaṁ deva-mayaṁ vikāryam
saṁsādya gatyā saha tena yāti
vijñāna-tattvaṁ guṇa-sannirodham

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
tad evaṁ sthūla-sūkṣma-bhūtātikramam uktvā tad-āvaraṇa-bhūtāhaṅkāra-prāptyā mahad-ādi-prāptim āha—sa iti. sa yogī vikāryaṁ saṁsādya samprāpya vijñāna-tattvaṁ yāti. vividhaṁ kāryaṁ yasyeti vikāryo ’haṅkāraḥ. sa tri-vidhaḥ—tāmaso rājasaḥ sāttvika iti. tatra tāmasāj jaḍāni bhūta-sūkṣmāṇi jāyante, rājasād bahir-mukhāni daśendriyāṇi, sāttvikān mana indriya-devatāś ca. teṣāṁ layaś ca tat-tad-ahaṅkāre. tatra bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām indriyāṇāṁ ca sannikarṣaṁ laya-sthānam. tāmasaṁ rājasaṁ ca, mano-mayaṁ deva-mayaṁ ca sāttvikaṁ prāpya, gatyā evaṁ gamanena tenāhaṅkāreṇa saha vijñāna-tattvaṁ mahat-tattvaṁ yāti. tataḥ guṇānāṁ sannirodho layo yasmin tat pradhānaṁ yāti.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदेवं स्थूलसूक्ष्मभूतातिक्रममुक्त्वा तदावरणभूताहङ्कारप्राप्त्या महदादिप्राप्तिमाह—स इति । स योगी विकार्यं संसाद्य सम्प्राप्य विज्ञानतत्त्वं याति । विविधं कार्यं यस्येति विकार्योऽहङ्कारः । स त्रिविधः—तामसो राजसः सात्त्विक इति । तत्र तामसाज्जडानि भूतसूक्ष्माणि जायन्ते, राजसाद् बहिर्मुखानि दशेन्द्रियाणि, सात्त्विकान्मन इन्द्रियदेवताश्च । तेषां लयश्च तत्तदहङ्कारे । तत्र भूतसूक्ष्माणामिन्द्रियाणां च सन्निकर्षं लयस्थानम् । तामसं राजसं च, मनोमयं देवमयं च सात्त्विकं प्राप्य, गत्या एवं गमनेन तेनाहङ्कारेण सह विज्ञानतत्त्वं महत्तत्त्वं याति । ततः गुणानां सन्निरोधो लयो यस्मिन् तत् प्रधानं याति ॥ ३० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Having described the crossing of gross and subtle elements, he states the attainment of mahat and the others through ahaṅkāra, which forms their covering. Ahaṅkāra is threefold: tamasic, rajasic and sattvic. From these arise respectively the subtle elements, the ten senses, and mind together with the sense-deities, and each dissolves back into its corresponding ahaṅkāra. Together with ahaṅkāra the yogin reaches mahat, the principle of vijñāna, and then pradhāna, in which the guṇas are restrained and dissolved.

ŚB 2.2.31

तेनात्मनात्मानमुपैति शान्त-
मानन्दमानन्दमयोऽवसाने ।
एतां गतिं भागवतीं गतो यः
स वै पुनर्नेह विषज्जतेऽङ्ग ॥ ३१ ॥
At the end, by that self, being full of bliss, he attains the tranquil, blissful Supreme Self. Whoever has attained this path belonging to Bhagavān indeed does not again become attached here, O dear one.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
tena ātmanā — by that self, in that state; ātmānam — the Supreme Self; upaiti — attains; śāntam — tranquil, unchanging; ānandam — blissful; ānanda-mayaḥ — full of bliss; avasāne — at the end; etām — this; gatim — course; bhāgavatīm — belonging to Bhagavān; gataḥ — having attained; yaḥ — whoever; saḥ — he; vai — indeed; punaḥ — again; na — not; iha — here; viṣajjate — becomes attached, returns; aṅga — O dear one.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
tena ātmanā — by that self; pradhāna-rūpeṇa — in the form of pradhāna; ānanda-mayaḥ san — being full of bliss; upādhīnām avasāne — at the cessation of limiting adjuncts; śāntam — peaceful; avikṛtam — unchanging; ānandam — blissful; paramātmānam — Supreme Self; upaiti — attains; na viṣajjate — does not become attached; na āvartate — does not return; iti arthaḥ — this is the meaning.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
tenātmanātmānam upaiti śāntam
ānandam ānanda-mayo ’vasāne
etāṁ gatiṁ bhāgavatīṁ gato yaḥ
sa vai punar neha viṣajjate ’ṅga

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
teneti. tenātmanā pradhāna-rūpeṇa ānanda-mayaḥ sann upādhīnām avasāne śāntam avikṛtam ānandaṁ paramātmānam upaiti. na viṣajjate nāvartata ity arthaḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तेनेति । तेनात्मना प्रधानरूपेण आनन्दमयः सन्नुपाधीनामवसाने शान्तमविकृतमानन्दं परमात्मानमुपैति । न विषजते नावर्तत इत्यर्थः ॥ ३१ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With that self in the form of pradhāna, being full of bliss, at the cessation of the limiting adjuncts he attains the peaceful, unchanging, blissful Supreme Self. “He does not become attached” means he does not return.

ŚB 2.2.32

एते सृती ते नृप वेदगीते
त्वयाभिपृष्टे च सनातने च ।
ये वै पुरा ब्रह्मण आह तुष्ट
आराधितो भगवान् वासुदेवः ॥ ३२ ॥
These two paths, O King, sung by the Vedas and eternal, about which you inquired, were formerly spoken to Brahmā by Bhagavān Vāsudeva, pleased by being worshiped.
Word-for-word
Bhāgavatam — Word-for-word
ete — these; sṛtī — two paths, courses; te — to you; nṛpa — O King; veda-gīte — sung, stated by the Veda; tvayā — by you; abhipṛṣṭe — properly inquired about; ca — and; sanātane — eternal; ca — and; ye — which two; vai — indeed; purā — formerly; brahmaṇe — to Brahmā; āha — spoke; tuṣṭaḥ — pleased; ārādhitaḥ — having been worshiped; bhagavān — Bhagavān; vāsudevaḥ — Vāsudeva.

Śrīdhara — Word-for-word
ete iti — on “these”; sṛtī — two paths; mārgau — paths; prakārau — modes; sadyo-muktiḥ — immediate liberation; ekā sṛtiḥ — one path; krama-muktiḥ — gradual liberation; dvitīyā sṛtiḥ — second path; veda-gīte — stated by the Veda; na tu sva-utprekṣite — not imagined by oneself; yadā sarve pramucyante kāmāḥ — “when all desires are released”; sadyo-muktiḥ — immediate liberation; te arciḥ abhisambhavanti — “they reach the flame”; krama-muktiḥ — gradual liberation; dakṣiṇa-mārgaḥ — southern path; api sūcitaḥ — also indicated; tvayā abhipṛṣṭe — asked by you; yat śrotavyam — “what is to be heard”; mukti-viṣaye — concerning liberation; dve api sṛtī — both paths; pṛṣṭe — asked; brūhi yad vā viparyayam — “tell what is contrary”; dakṣiṇa-mārgaḥ api — southern path also; arthāt pṛṣṭaḥ — implicitly asked.
Transliteration
Bhāgavatam — Transliteration
ete sṛtī te nṛpa veda-gīte
tvayābhipṛṣṭe ca sanātane ca
ye vai purā brahmaṇa āha tuṣṭa
ārādhito bhagavān vāsudevaḥ

Śrīdhara — Transliteration
ete iti. sṛtī mārgau prakārāv ity arthaḥ. he nṛpa! “anapekṣo…nirbhidya mūrdhan visṛjet paraṁ gataḥ” iti yā sadyo-muktiḥ saikā sṛtiḥ. “yadi prayāsyan” ity ādinā krama-muktiś ca dvitīyā sṛtiḥ. ete sṛtī vedena gīte ukte, na tu svotprekṣite. tatra “yadā sarve pramucyante kāmā ye ’sya hṛdi śritāḥ, atha martyo ’mṛto bhavaty atra brahma samaśnute” iti sadyo-muktiḥ. “te ’rcir abhisambhavanti” ity ādinā krama-muktiś ca vedena ukte. yad vā “na karmabhis tāṁ gatim āpnuvanti” iti dakṣiṇa-mārgasyāpi sūcitatvād ete sṛtī iti dvi-vacanam. tvayābhipṛṣṭe “yac chrotavyam” ity ādi-praśnād arthān mukti-viṣaye dve api sṛtī pṛṣṭe; “brūhi yad vā viparyayam” iti dakṣiṇa-mārgo ’py arthāt pṛṣṭaḥ. evaṁ pṛṣṭe yac chrotavyam ity ādi-praśne ete sṛtī ity anvayaḥ.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एते इति । सृती मार्गौ प्रकारावित्यर्थः । हे नृप ! “अनपेक्षो…निर्भिद्य मूर्धन् विसृजेत्परं गतः” इति या सद्योमुक्तिः सैका सृतिः । “यदि प्रयास्यन्” इत्यादिना क्रममुक्तिश्च द्वितीया सृतिः । एते सृती वेदेन गीते उक्ते, न तु स्वोत्प्रेक्षिते । तत्र “यदा सर्वे प्रमुच्यन्ते कामा येऽस्य हृदि श्रिताः । अथ मर्त्योऽमृतो भवत्यत्र ब्रह्म समश्नुते” इति सद्योमुक्तिः । “तेऽर्चिरभिसम्भवन्ति” इत्यादिना क्रममुक्तिश्च वेदेन उक्ते । यद्वा “न कर्मभिस्तां गतिमाप्नुवन्ति” इति दक्षिणमार्गस्यापि सूचितत्वादेते सृती इति द्विवचनम् । त्वयाभिपृष्टे “यच्छ्रोतव्यम्” इत्यादिप्रश्नादर्थान्मुक्तिविषये द्वे अपि सृती पृष्टे; “ब्रूहि यद्वा विपर्ययम्” इति दक्षिणमार्गोऽप्यर्थात् पृष्टः । एवं पृष्टे यच्छ्रोतव्यमित्यादिप्रश्ने एते सृती इत्यन्वयः ॥ ३२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Sṛtī” means two paths or modes. Immediate liberation is one path; gradual liberation beginning with “if he intends to go” is the second. These are taught by the Veda, not invented. The Upaniṣadic statements about the release of all desires and the path beginning with light establish these two courses. The southern path is also implicitly indicated, and your earlier questions asked, directly or by implication, about these courses.

ŚB 2.2.33

न ह्यतोऽन्यः शिवः पन्था विशतः संसृताविह ।
वासुदेवे भगवति भक्तियोगो यतो भवेत् ॥ ३३ ॥
na hy ato ’nyaḥ śivaḥ panthā viśataḥ saṁsṛtāv iha
vāsudeve bhagavati bhakti-yogo yato bhavet
Word-for-word
na hi — indeed there is not; ataḥ — than this; anyaḥ — another; śivaḥ — auspicious, beneficial; panthāḥ — path; viśataḥ — for one entering, moving within; saṁsṛtau — saṁsāra; iha — here; vāsudeve — toward Vāsudeva; bhagavati — Bhagavān; bhakti-yogaḥ — bhakti-yoga; yataḥ — from which, because; bhavet — arises.
Translation
For one moving within saṁsāra there is no other auspicious path than this, because from it bhakti-yoga toward Bhagavān Vāsudeva arises.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तर्हि वैराग्यमार्गोऽप्यस्ति किमिति भक्तियोग एवोपदिष्ट इति ? तत्राह—न हीति । यस्माद् वासुदेवे भगवति भक्तियोगो भवेत्; अतो भक्तियोगादन्यः शिवः पन्था न ह्यस्तीत्यन्वयः ॥ ३३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Then why is bhakti-yoga alone taught—does not a path of detachment also exist? To this he says, “there is not.” Because bhakti-yoga arises toward Bhagavān Vāsudeva, there is indeed no other auspicious path than bhakti-yoga.

ŚB 2.2.34

भगवान् ब्रह्म कार्त्स्न्येन त्रिरन्वीक्ष्य मनीषया ।
तदध्यवस्यत्कूटस्थो रतिरात्मन् यतो भवेत् ॥ ३४ ॥
bhagavān brahma kārtsnyena trir anvīkṣya manīṣayā
tad adhyavasyat kūṭa-stho ratir ātman yato bhavet
Word-for-word
bhagavān — the venerable Brahmā; brahma — the Veda; kārtsnyena — completely; triḥ — three times, in three ways; anvīkṣya — examining; manīṣayā — with intelligence; tat — that; adhyavasyat — determined, ascertained; kūṭa-sthaḥ — changeless, immutable; ratiḥ — love, devotion; ātman — in the Self; yataḥ — from which; bhavet — may arise.
Translation
The venerable Brahmā, examining the Veda completely in three ways with intelligence, determined that its true purport is that love should arise for the changeless Self.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तत्र ब्रह्मणः कर्मादिमार्गाः किमर्थमुक्ता इति ? तत्राह—भगवानिति । ब्रह्म वेदं त्रिरन्वीक्ष्य पर्यालोच्य । प्रकारत्रयेणेत्यर्थः । कूटस्थो निर्विकारः परमेश्वर एवात्मा, तस्मिन् रतिर्भक्तिर्यतो भवेत्; तदेव कर्मादिभिस्तात्पर्येणाध्यवस्यत् निश्चितवान् ॥ ३४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Why then are the paths of karma and the rest taught by the Veda? He answers with “Bhagavān.” Brahmā examined the Veda three times—that is, in three ways. The changeless Supreme Lord alone is the Self; he determined that the intended purport of karma and the other teachings is that love, devotion, arise toward Him.

ŚB 2.2.35

भगवान् सर्वभूतेषु लक्षितः स्वात्मना हरिः ।
दृश्यैर्बुद्ध्यादिभिर्द्रष्टा लक्षणैरनुमापकैः ॥ ३५ ॥
bhagavān sarva-bhūteṣu lakṣitaḥ svātmanā hariḥ
dṛśyair buddhy-ādibhir draṣṭā lakṣaṇair anumāpakaiḥ
Word-for-word
bhagavān — Bhagavān; sarva-bhūteṣu — in all beings; lakṣitaḥ — recognized, indicated; sva-ātmanā — as their own Self, as the indwelling Self; hariḥ — Hari; dṛśyaiḥ — by things seen; buddhi-ādibhiḥ — by intelligence and the rest; draṣṭā — the Seer; lakṣaṇaiḥ — by signs; anumāpakaiḥ — inferential indicators.
Translation
Bhagavān Hari is recognized in all beings as their indwelling Self; the Seer is inferred from visible signs such as intelligence and the other faculties.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तमेवात्मनि प्रतीतं विषयेष्वपि प्रतीयादिति तस्य सर्वत्र स्फुरणमाह—भगवानिति । स्वात्मना सर्वभूतान्तर्यामित्वेन सर्वत्र लक्षितः । कुतः ? द्रष्टा तत्कालिकं वर्तमानं च दृश्यं विना न भवति । तेन सदातनस्य द्रष्टुः सदातनं दृश्यं जीवचेष्टादिकमवश्यं कल्पनीयम् । तच्च स्वप्नादावन्तर्यामिणं विनाऽनुपपन्नमिति दृश्यैर्लिङ्गैर्बुद्ध्यादिभिरेवानुमापकैः ॥ ३५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that the same Lord, apprehended in the Self, should also be recognized in objects, and thus states His manifestation everywhere. He is recognized everywhere as the indwelling Self of all beings. Why? A seer does not exist without something seen, whether past or present. Therefore for the eternal Seer an eternal field of what is seen, such as the activities of living beings, must be admitted; and in dream and similar states those activities cannot be accounted for without an inner controller. Thus visible things—intelligence and the rest—serve as inferential signs.

ŚB 2.2.36

तस्मात्सर्वात्मना राजन् हरिः सर्वत्र सर्वदा ।
श्रोतव्यः कीर्तितव्यश्च स्मर्तव्यो भगवान् नृणाम् ॥ ३६ ॥
tasmāt sarvātmanā rājan hariḥ sarvatra sarvadā
śrotavyaḥ kīrtitavyaś ca smartavyo bhagavān nṛṇām
Word-for-word
tasmāt — therefore; sarva-ātmanā — with one’s whole being, in every manner; rājan — O King; hariḥ — Hari; sarvatra — everywhere; sarvadā — always; śrotavyaḥ — to be heard; kīrtitavyaḥ — to be praised, chanted; ca — and; smartavyaḥ — to be remembered; bhagavān — Bhagavān; nṛṇām — by human beings.
Translation
Therefore, O King, in every manner, everywhere and always, human beings should hear of, praise and remember Bhagavān Hari.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तस्मादिति । सर्वात्मना सर्वप्रकारेण ॥ ३६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Therefore.” “With the whole self” means in every manner.

ŚB 2.2.37

पिबन्ति ये भगवत आत्मनः सतां
कथामृतं श्रवणपुटेषु सम्भृतम् ।
पुनन्ति ते विषयविदूषिताशयं
व्रजन्ति तच्चरणसरोरुहान्तिकम् ॥ ३७ ॥
pibanti ye bhagavata ātmanaḥ satāṁ
kathāmṛtaṁ śravaṇa-puṭeṣu sambhṛtam
punanti te viṣaya-vidūṣitāśayaṁ
vrajanti tac-caraṇa-saroruhāntikam
Word-for-word
pibanti — drink; ye — those who; bhagavataḥ — of Bhagavān; ātmanaḥ — who is the very life, Self; satām — of the good; kathā-amṛtam — nectar of narration; śravaṇa-puṭeṣu — in the hollows of the ears; sambhṛtam — collected, held; punanti — purify; te — they; viṣaya — by sense objects; vidūṣita — especially polluted; āśayam — inner disposition, mind; vrajanti — go; tat-caraṇa-saroruha — His lotus feet; antikam — near, vicinity.
Translation
Those who drink the nectar of the narration of Bhagavān, the very life of the good, gathered in the hollows of their ears, purify the heart polluted by sense objects and go near His lotus feet.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एवं श्रवणादिसाधनमेव सर्वोत्तममित्युक्तम् । तत्र श्रवणादेव सर्वसिद्धिमाह—पिबन्तीति । सतामात्मनः प्राणभूतस्य भगवतः कथैवामृतम् । श्रवणपुटेषु सम्भृतं सम्पुटवत्तृप्तिर्यावद्धृतम् । तां पिबन्ति । ते च विषयैर्विशेषेण दूषिताशयं चित्तं पुनन्ति । ततश्च तस्य चरणसरोरुहस्य अन्तिकं समीपं वैकुण्ठं व्रजन्ति ॥ ३७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Thus hearing and the other practices have been declared highest of all. He now says that all perfection comes from hearing alone. The narration of Bhagavān, who is the very life of the good, is itself nectar. Held in the hollows of the ears like a container until satisfaction, they drink it. Such persons purify the mind especially polluted by sense objects and then go near His lotus feet—to Vaikuṇṭha.

2.3 Pure Devotional Service: The Change in Heart

ŚB 2.3.1

श्रीशुक उवाच ।
एवमेतन्निगदितं पृष्टवान् यद्भवान् मम ।
नृणां यन्म्रियमाणानां मनुष्येषु मनीषिणाम् ॥ १ ॥
śrī-śuka uvāca
evam etan nigaditaṁ pṛṣṭavān yad bhavān mama
nṛṇāṁ yan mriyamāṇānāṁ manuṣyeṣu manīṣiṇām
Word-for-word
śrī-śukaḥ uvāca — Śrī Śuka said; evam — thus; etat — this; nigaditam — has been stated; pṛṣṭavān — you asked; yat — which; bhavān — you; mama — of me; nṛṇām — for human beings; yat — which; mriyamāṇānām — who are dying; manuṣyeṣu — among humans; manīṣiṇām — of the intelligent.
Translation
Śrī Śuka said: Thus I have stated to you what you asked concerning intelligent human beings who are approaching death.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तृतीये विष्णुभक्तेषु वैशिष्ट्यं शृण्वतां मुनेः । भक्त्युद्रेकेण तत्कर्मश्रवणादर ईर्यते ॥ इदानीमन्यदेवताभजनस्यापि पुत्रादिभजनवदेव तुच्छफलत्वेन हेयत्वं वक्तुं पूर्वोक्तमनुवदति—एवमिति । ममेति माम् । कदाचिद्दैवयोगेन मनुष्यत्वं प्राप्तेषु जीवेषु ये मनीषिणस्तेषां तत्रापि ये म्रियमाणास्तेषां विशेषत एवमेतद्धरिकथाश्रवणादिकं निगदितं विहितमित्यर्थः ॥ १ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In the third chapter the special excellence of Viṣṇu’s devotees is stated, and the sage’s eagerness to hear His acts arises from an increase of devotion. Now, in order to show that worship of other deities is to be rejected because its fruit is trivial, like service rendered merely for sons and other limited ends, he restates what was said before. “Mama” means “me.” Among beings who by some conjunction of providence have attained human birth, those who are intelligent—and especially those among them who are dying—have been enjoined to hear Hari’s narrations and perform the other practices described in this way.

ŚB 2.3.2

ब्रह्मवर्चसकामस्तु यजेत ब्रह्मणः पतिम् ।
इन्द्रमिन्द्रियकामस्तु प्रजाकामः प्रजापतीन् ॥ २ ॥
brahma-varcasa-kāmas tu yajeta brahmaṇaḥ patim
indram indriya-kāmas tu prajā-kāmaḥ prajāpatīn
Word-for-word
brahma-varcasa-kāmaḥ — one desiring the brilliance of sacred learning; tu — indeed; yajeta — should worship; brahmaṇaḥ patim — the lord of the Veda, Brahmā; indram — Indra; indriya-kāmaḥ — one desiring strength of the senses; prajā-kāmaḥ — one desiring offspring; prajāpatīn — the Prajāpatis.
Translation
One who desires the brilliance of sacred learning should worship the lord of the Veda, Brahmā; one who desires strength of the senses should worship Indra, and one who desires offspring should worship the Prajāpatis.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ब्रह्मणस्पतिं वेदस्य पतिं ब्रह्माणम् । इन्द्रियपाटवकामस्त्विन्द्रम् । प्रजाकामः प्रजापतीन् दक्षादीन् ॥ २ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “The lord of Brahman” means Brahmā, the lord of the Veda. One desiring keenness of the senses should worship Indra. One desiring offspring should worship the Prajāpatis such as Dakṣa.

ŚB 2.3.3

देवीं मायां तु श्रीकामस्तेजस्कामो विभावसुम् ।
वसुकामो वसून् रुद्रान् वीर्यकामोऽथ वीर्यवान् ॥ ३ ॥
devīṁ māyāṁ tu śrī-kāmas tejas-kāmo vibhāvasum
vasu-kāmo vasūn rudrān vīrya-kāmo ’tha vīryavān
Word-for-word
devīm māyām — the goddess Māyā, Durgā; tu — but; śrī-kāmaḥ — one desiring prosperity; tejas-kāmaḥ — one desiring splendour or power; vibhāvasum — fire; vasu-kāmaḥ — one desiring wealth; vasūn — the Vasus; rudrān — the Rudras; vīrya-kāmaḥ — one desiring prowess; atha — then; vīryavān — being powerful.
Translation
One desiring prosperity should worship the goddess Māyā, Durgā; one desiring splendour should worship fire; one desiring wealth should worship the Vasus; and one desiring prowess should worship the Rudras.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
मायां दुर्गाम् । विभावसुमग्निम् । वसुकामो धनार्थी । वीर्यं प्रभावस्तत्कामो वीर्यवान् सन् रुद्रान् यजेत् ॥ ३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Māyā” means Durgā. “Vibhāvasu” means fire. One desiring vasu is one seeking wealth. “Vīrya” means power or influence; one desiring it should worship the Rudras.

ŚB 2.3.4

अन्नाद्यकामस्त्वदितिं स्वर्गकामोऽदितेः सुतान् ।
विश्वान् देवान् राज्यकामः साध्यान् संसाधको विशाम् ॥ ४ ॥
annādya-kāmas tv aditiṁ svarga-kāmo ’diteḥ sutān
viśvān devān rājya-kāmaḥ sādhyān saṁsādhako viśām
Word-for-word
annādya-kāmaḥ — one desiring food and sustenance; aditim — Aditi; svarga-kāmaḥ — one desiring heaven; aditeḥ sutān — Aditi’s sons, the Ādityas; viśvān devān — the Viśvadevas; rājya-kāmaḥ — one desiring royal dominion; sādhyān — the Sādhyas; saṁsādhakaḥ — one desiring to bring under control; viśām — the people of a territory.
Translation
One desiring food should worship Aditi; one desiring heaven, Aditi’s sons, the twelve Ādityas; one desiring royal dominion should worship the Viśvadevas, and one seeking control of the people should worship the Sādhyas.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अन्नाद्यं भोज्यं भक्ष्यं च । अदितेः सुतान् द्वादशादित्यान् । विशां देशस्थप्रजानां स्वाधीनतामिच्छन् साध्यान् यजेत् ॥ ४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Annādya” includes food that is eaten and food that is chewed. Aditi’s sons are the twelve Ādityas. One who desires the inhabitants of a region to be under his control should worship the Sādhyas.

ŚB 2.3.5

आयुष्कामोऽश्विनौ देवौ पुष्टिकाम इलां यजेत् ।
प्रतिष्ठाकामः पुरुषो रोदसी लोकमातरौ ॥ ५ ॥
āyuṣ-kāmo ’śvinau devau puṣṭi-kāma ilāṁ yajet
pratiṣṭhā-kāmaḥ puruṣo rodasī loka-mātarau
Word-for-word
āyuṣ-kāmaḥ — one desiring long life; aśvinau devau — the two Aśvin gods; puṣṭi-kāmaḥ — one desiring nourishment; ilām — the earth; yajet — should worship; pratiṣṭhā-kāmaḥ — one desiring stability in position; puruṣaḥ — a person; rodasī — heaven and earth; loka-mātarau — the two mothers of the worlds.
Translation
One desiring long life should worship the two Aśvins; one desiring nourishment should worship the earth. A person desiring stability in his position should worship heaven and earth, the two mothers of the worlds.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इलां पृथ्वीम् । प्रतिष्ठा स्थानादप्रच्युतिः । रोदसी द्यावाभूमी ॥ ५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Ilā” means the earth. “Pratiṣṭhā” means not falling from one’s position. “Rodasī” means heaven and earth.

ŚB 2.3.6

रूपाभिकामो गन्धर्वान् स्त्रीकामोऽप्सर उर्वशीम् ।
आधिपत्यकामः सर्वेषां यजेत परमेष्ठिनम् ॥ ६ ॥
rūpābhikāmo gandharvān strī-kāmo ’psara urvaśīm
ādhipatya-kāmaḥ sarveṣāṁ yajeta parameṣṭhinam
Word-for-word
rūpa-abhikāmaḥ — one greatly desiring beauty; gandharvān — the Gandharvas; strī-kāmaḥ — one desiring a woman; apsaraḥ urvaśīm — the Apsaras Urvaśī; ādhipatya-kāmaḥ — one desiring lordship; sarveṣām — over all; yajeta — should worship; parameṣṭhinam — Parameṣṭhin, Brahmā.
Translation
One desiring beauty should worship the Gandharvas; one desiring a woman should worship the Apsaras Urvaśī; and one desiring lordship over all should worship Parameṣṭhin, Brahmā.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अप्सराश्चासावुर्वशी च ताम् ॥ ६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. She is both an Apsaras and Urvaśī; thus the expression means the Apsaras Urvaśī.

ŚB 2.3.7

यज्ञं यजेद्यशस्कामः कोशकामः प्रचेतसम् ।
विद्याकामस्तु गिरिशं दाम्पत्यार्थ उमां सतीम् ॥ ७ ॥
yajñaṁ yajed yaśas-kāmaḥ kośa-kāmaḥ pracetasam
vidyā-kāmas tu giriśaṁ dāmpatyārtha umāṁ satīm
Word-for-word
yajñam — Yajña, Viṣṇu under the designation of sacrifice; yajet — should worship; yaśas-kāmaḥ — one desiring fame; kośa-kāmaḥ — one desiring a treasury; pracetasam — Pracetas, Varuṇa; vidyā-kāmaḥ — one desiring knowledge; giriśam — Giriśa, Śiva; dāmpatya-arthaḥ — one whose aim is mutual marital affection; umām satīm — the chaste Umā.
Translation
One desiring fame should worship Yajña, Viṣṇu; one desiring a treasury should worship Pracetas, Varuṇa; one desiring knowledge should worship Giriśa; and one seeking mutual affection in marriage should worship the chaste Umā.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यज्ञं यज्ञोपाधिं विष्णुम् । कोशो वसुसञ्चयः । वसुकाम इत्यत्र धनमात्रमिति भेदः । दाम्पत्यमन्योन्यप्रीतिस्तदेवार्थो यस्य सः ॥ ७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Yajña” is Viṣṇu under the designation of sacrifice. “Kośa” is an accumulation of wealth; this differs from the earlier “one desiring wealth,” where wealth in general was intended. “Dāmpatya” is mutual affection between husband and wife; one whose aim is precisely that should worship Umā.

ŚB 2.3.8

धर्मार्थ उत्तमश्लोकं तन्तुस्तन्वन् पितॄन् यजेत् ।
रक्षाकामः पुण्यजनान् ओजस्कामो मरुद्गणान् ॥ ८ ॥
dharmārtha uttama-ślokaṁ tantus tanvan pitṝn yajet
rakṣā-kāmaḥ puṇya-janān ojas-kāmo marud-gaṇān
Word-for-word
dharma-arthaḥ — one whose aim is dharma; uttama-ślokam — Viṣṇu under the designation Uttamaśloka; tantum tanvan — desiring to extend the family line; pitṝn — the ancestors; yajet — should worship; rakṣā-kāmaḥ — one desiring protection; puṇya-janān — the Yakṣas; ojas-kāmaḥ — one desiring strength; marut-gaṇān — the hosts of Maruts.
Translation
One desiring dharma should worship Uttamaśloka, Viṣṇu; one wishing to extend the family line should worship the ancestors; one desiring protection should worship the Yakṣas, and one desiring strength should worship the hosts of Maruts.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
धर्मार्थो धर्मकामः । उत्तमश्लोकोपाधिं विष्णुम् । तन्तुं तन्वन् सन्तानवृद्धिमन्विच्छन् । रक्षा बाधानिवृत्तिस्तत्कामः । पुण्यजनान् यक्षान् । ओजो बलं तत्कामो मरुद्गणान् देवान् ॥ ८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Dharmārtha” means one who desires dharma. One should worship Viṣṇu under the designation Uttamaśloka. “Extending the thread” means desiring growth of one’s progeny. Protection is the removal of obstruction; one desiring it should worship the Yakṣas, called Puṇyajanas. “Ojas” means strength; one desiring it should worship the gods known as the Marut hosts.

ŚB 2.3.9

राज्यकामो मनून् देवान् निरृतिं त्वभिचरन् यजेत् ।
कामकामो यजेत्सोममकामः पुरुषं परम् ॥ ९ ॥
rājya-kāmo manūn devān nirṛtiṁ tv abhicaran yajet
kāma-kāmo yajet somam akāmaḥ puruṣaṁ param
Word-for-word
rājya-kāmaḥ — one desiring kingship; manūn devān — the divine Manus, protectors of the manvantaras; nirṛtim — Nirṛti; tu — but; abhicaran — performing hostile rites, desiring an enemy’s death; yajet — should worship; kāma-kāmaḥ — one desiring sensual enjoyment; somam — Soma; akāmaḥ — one without worldly desire, desiring detachment; puruṣam param — the Supreme Puruṣa.
Translation
One desiring kingship should worship the divine Manus, the protectors of the manvantaras. One performing hostile rites to bring about an enemy’s death should worship Nirṛti. One desiring enjoyment should worship Soma; one without such desire should worship the Supreme Puruṣa.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
राज्यं राजत्वं तत्कामो मनून् देवान् मन्वन्तरपालान् । राज्ञः कर्म राज्यं तत्कामो विश्वान् देवानिति विशेषः । अभिचरन् शत्रुमरणमिच्छन् निरृतिं राक्षसम् । कामकामो भोगेच्छुः । अकामो वैराग्यकामः । पुरुषं परं प्रकृतिव्यतिरेकोपाधिमीश्वरम् ॥ ९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Rājya” here means kingship; one desiring it should worship the divine Manus, protectors of the manvantaras. This differs from the earlier desire for the activity or dominion of a king, for which the Viśvadevas were prescribed. One practicing hostile rites, desiring an enemy’s death, should worship the Rākṣasa Nirṛti. “Kāma-kāma” means one desiring enjoyment. “Akāma” means one desiring detachment; he should worship the Supreme Puruṣa, the Lord indicated as distinct from prakṛti.

ŚB 2.3.10

अकामः सर्वकामो वा मोक्षकाम उदारधीः ।
तीव्रेण भक्तियोगेन यजेत पुरुषं परम् ॥ १० ॥
akāmaḥ sarva-kāmo vā mokṣa-kāma udāra-dhīḥ
tīvreṇa bhakti-yogena yajeta puruṣaṁ param
Word-for-word
akāmaḥ — without material desire; sarva-kāmaḥ — desiring everything; vā — or; mokṣa-kāmaḥ — desiring liberation; udāra-dhīḥ — broad-minded, noble in understanding; tīvreṇa — intense, undivided; bhakti-yogena — by bhakti-yoga; yajeta — should worship; puruṣam param — the Supreme Puruṣa.
Translation
Whether one is without material desire, full of desires, or desirous of liberation, a broad-minded person should worship the Supreme Puruṣa through intense bhakti-yoga.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अकाम इति । अकामः एकान्तभक्तः, उक्तानुक्तसर्वकामो वा । पुरुषं पूर्णं परं निरुपाधिम् ॥ १० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. On “without desire”: one without desire is an exclusive devotee; or one may possess every desire, whether already mentioned or not. “Puruṣa” is the Full One; “supreme” means free from limiting adjuncts.

ŚB 2.3.11

एतावानेव यजतामिह निःश्रेयसोदयः ।
भगवत्यचलो भावो यद्भागवतसङ्गतः ॥ ११ ॥
etāvān eva yajatām iha niḥśreyasodayaḥ
bhagavaty acalo bhāvo yad bhāgavata-saṅgataḥ
Word-for-word
etāvān eva — this alone, just so much; yajatām — of those who worship; iha — here; niḥśreyasa-udayaḥ — arising of the highest good; bhagavati — toward Bhagavān; acalaḥ — unwavering; bhāvaḥ — disposition, devotion; yat — which; bhāgavata-saṅgataḥ — from association with devotees of Bhagavān.
Translation
For those who worship here, the true arising of the highest good is precisely this: from association with devotees there comes unwavering devotion toward Bhagavān.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
पूर्वोक्तनानादेवतायजनस्यापि संयोगपृथक्त्वेन भक्तियोगफलत्वमाह—एतावानिति । इन्द्रादीनपि यजतामिह तत्तद्देवतायजने भागवतानां सङ्गतो भगवत्यचलो भावो भक्तिर्भवतीति यदेतावानेव निःश्रेयसस्य परमपुरुषार्थस्य उदयो लाभः । अन्यत्तु स्वर्गादि सर्वं तुच्छमित्यर्थः ॥ ११ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He says that even the previously described worship of many deities can, through indirect connection, bear bhakti-yoga as its fruit. Even for those worshiping Indra and the others, the true rise of the highest human good is this alone: through association with devotees, unwavering devotion to Bhagavān arises. Every other result, such as heaven, is trivial.

ŚB 2.3.12

ज्ञानं यदा प्रतिनिवृत्तगुणोर्मिचक्र-
मात्मप्रसाद उत यत्र गुणेष्वसङ्गः ।
कैवल्यसम्मतपथस्त्वथ भक्तियोगः
को निर्वृतो हरिकथासु रतिं न कुर्यात् ॥ १२ ॥
jñānaṁ yadā pratinivṛtta-guṇormi-cakram
ātma-prasāda uta yatra guṇeṣv asaṅgaḥ
kaivalya-sammata-pathas tv atha bhakti-yogaḥ
ko nirvṛto hari-kathāsu ratiṁ na kuryāt
Word-for-word
jñānam — knowledge; yadā — when, wherein; pratinivṛtta — completely withdrawn, ceased; guṇa-ūrmi-cakram — wheel of waves produced by the guṇas; ātma-prasādaḥ — serenity of the self, mind; uta — moreover; yatra — wherein; guṇeṣu — toward the qualities and their objects; asaṅgaḥ — nonattachment; kaivalya — liberation; sammata — approved; pathaḥ — path; tu atha — and then indeed; bhakti-yogaḥ — bhakti-yoga; kaḥ — who; nirvṛtaḥ — fulfilled, delighted; hari-kathāsu — in narrations of Hari; ratim — love, relish; na kuryāt — would not develop.
Translation
When knowledge arises in which the circling waves of the guṇas have ceased, there is serenity of mind and nonattachment to their objects; thereafter bhakti-yoga becomes the path approved for liberation. Who, fulfilled by hearing, would not develop love for the narrations of Hari?
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भागवतसङ्गत इत्यनेन सूचितां हरिकथारतिं स्तौति—ज्ञानमिति । यद् यासु कथासु ज्ञानं भवति । कीदृशम् ? सर्वतः प्रतिनिवृत्तमुपरतं गुणोर्मीणां रागद्वेषादीनां चक्रं समूहो यस्मात् तत् । उत अनन्तरं तद्धेतुरात्मप्रसादश्च यत्र यासु मनःप्रसादहेतुः गुणेषु विषयेष्वसङ्गो वैराग्यं च । उभयत्रेति पाठे इहामुत्र च गुणेष्वसङ्गः । अथ कैवल्यमित्येव सम्मतः पन्थाः एतादृशो यो भक्तियोगः । निर्वृत्तः श्रवणसुखेन, अन्यत्रानिर्वृत इति वा । तासु हरिकथासु को रतिं न कुर्यात् ॥ १२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The words “association with devotees” imply delight in Hari’s narrations, and he praises that delight. In those narrations knowledge arises in which the entire wheel or mass of waves produced by the guṇas—attachment, aversion and the rest—has ceased. There too arises serenity of mind, the cause of nonattachment and detachment from objects. In the variant reading “in both,” nonattachment is to objects both here and hereafter. Thereafter bhakti-yoga of this kind is the path accepted as leading to liberation. “Fulfilled” can mean fulfilled by the happiness of hearing, or unfulfilled anywhere else. Who would not love such narrations of Hari?

ŚB 2.3.13

शौनक उवाच ।
इत्यभिव्याहृतं राजा निशम्य भरतर्षभः ।
किमन्यत्पृष्टवान् भूयो वैयासकिमृषिं कविम् ॥ १३ ॥
śaunaka uvāca
ity abhivyāhṛtaṁ rājā niśamya bharatarṣabhaḥ
kim anyat pṛṣṭavān bhūyo vaiyāsakim ṛṣiṁ kavim
Word-for-word
śaunakaḥ uvāca — Śaunaka said; iti — thus; abhivyāhṛtam — spoken; rājā — the King; niśamya — having heard; bharata-ṛṣabhaḥ — the best of the Bharatas; kim anyat — what else; pṛṣṭavān — did he ask; bhūyaḥ — again; vaiyāsakim — the son of Vyāsa; ṛṣim — the seer; kavim — the sage learned in revealed speech.
Translation
Śaunaka said: Having heard what was spoken thus, what else did the King, the best of the Bharatas, again ask Vyāsa’s son, the seer and sage?
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इतीति । अभिव्याहृतमुक्तम् । ऋषिं परब्रह्मदर्शिनं कविं शब्दब्रह्मनिष्णातम् ॥ १३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Spoken thus” means stated. He is an “ṛṣi,” a seer of the Supreme Brahman, and a “kavi,” one deeply versed in the verbal Brahman, the Veda.

ŚB 2.3.14

एतच्छुश्रूषतां विद्वन् सूत नोऽर्हसि भाषितुम् ।
कथा हरिकथोदर्काः सतां स्युः सदसि ध्रुवम् ॥ १४ ॥
etac chuśrūṣatāṁ vidvan sūta no ’rhasi bhāṣitum
kathā hari-kathodarkāḥ satāṁ syuḥ sadasi dhruvam
Word-for-word
etat — this; śuśrūṣatām — of us desiring to hear; vidvan — O learned one; sūta — O Sūta; naḥ — to us; arhasi — you ought; bhāṣitum — to speak; kathāḥ — narrations; hari-kathā-udarkāḥ — whose later fruit is Hari’s narration, centered ultimately in Hari’s story; satām — of the good, devotees; syuḥ — are; sadasi — in an assembly; dhruvam — certainly.
Translation
O learned Sūta, you should tell this to us who desire to hear it, for in an assembly of devotees the narrations certainly culminate in the narration of Hari.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एतदिति । श्रवणेच्छायां हेतुः । हरिकथा एव उदर्क उत्तरफलं यासु ताः कथाः । सतां भागवतानां सदसि सभायां स्युः । अत एतच्छुश्रूषतां श्रोतुकामानां नः ॥ १४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He gives the reason for their desire to hear. These are narrations whose later fruit is precisely the narration of Hari. Such narrations arise in the assembly of the good, the devotees. Therefore speak it to us who desire to hear this.

ŚB 2.3.15

स वै भागवतो राजा पाण्डवेयो महारथः ।
बालक्रीडनकैः क्रीडन् कृष्णक्रीडां य आददे ॥ १५ ॥
sa vai bhāgavato rājā pāṇḍaveyo mahā-rathaḥ
bāla-krīḍanakaiḥ krīḍan kṛṣṇa-krīḍāṁ ya ādade
Word-for-word
saḥ — he; vai — indeed; bhāgavataḥ — a devotee of Bhagavān; rājā — the King; pāṇḍaveyaḥ — descendant of the Pāṇḍavas; mahā-rathaḥ — great chariot-warrior; bāla-krīḍanakaiḥ — with children’s playthings; krīḍan — playing; kṛṣṇa-krīḍām — play centered on Kṛṣṇa, worship of Kṛṣṇa; yaḥ — who; ādade — adopted, took up.
Translation
That King, descendant of the Pāṇḍavas and a great warrior, was indeed a devotee of Bhagavān: even while playing with children’s toys, he adopted play centered on Kṛṣṇa.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
एतत्प्रपञ्चयति स वा इति द्वाभ्याम् । कृष्णपूजादिरूपां क्रीडां यः स्वीकृतवान् ॥ १५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He expands this point in two verses beginning “that King.” The King adopted play whose form was worship of Kṛṣṇa and similar service.

ŚB 2.3.16

वैयासकिश्च भगवान् वासुदेवपरायणः ।
उरुगायगुणोदाराः सतां स्युर्हि समागमे ॥ १६ ॥
vaiyāsakiś ca bhagavān vāsudeva-parāyaṇaḥ
urugāya-guṇodārāḥ satāṁ syur hi samāgame
Word-for-word
vaiyāsakiḥ — the son of Vyāsa, Śuka; ca — and; bhagavān — venerable, endowed with spiritual excellence; vāsudeva-parāyaṇaḥ — wholly devoted to Vāsudeva; urugāya — the widely praised Lord; guṇa — qualities; udārāḥ — great, exalted; satām — of the good; syuḥ — arise; hi — indeed; samāgame — in a meeting.
Translation
And the venerable son of Vyāsa was wholly devoted to Vāsudeva. In a meeting of devotees, exalted narrations concerning the qualities of the widely praised Lord naturally arise.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
वैयासकिरिति । उरुगायस्य भगवतो गुणैरुदाराः महत्यः कथाः स्युः ॥ १६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “The son of Vyāsa.” In such a meeting there arise great and exalted narrations through the qualities of Urugāya, Bhagavān.

ŚB 2.3.17

आयुर्हरति वै पुंसामुद्यन्नस्तं च यन्नसौ ।
तस्यर्ते यत्क्षणो नीत उत्तमश्लोकवार्तया ॥ १७ ॥
āyur harati vai puṁsām udyann astaṁ ca yan asau
tasyarte yat-kṣaṇo nīta uttama-śloka-vārtayā
Word-for-word
āyuḥ — lifespan; harati — takes away; vai — indeed; puṁsām — of people; udyan — rising; astam — to setting; ca — and; yan — going; asau — that sun; tasya ṛte — except for that; yat-kṣaṇaḥ — whatever moment; nītaḥ — passed, spent; uttama-śloka-vārtayā — with discussion of Uttamaśloka.
Translation
That sun, rising and setting, indeed takes away the lifespan of human beings, except for each moment that has been spent in discussion of Uttamaśloka.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
किञ्च वृथैव क्षीयमाणमायुः हरिकथायामेव सफलं कुरु इत्याशयेनाह त्रिभिः—आयुरिति । असौ सूर्य उद्यन्नुदयं प्राप्नुवन्नस्तं च यन् तिरोधानं प्राप्नुवन्, उत्तमश्लोकस्य भगवतो वार्तया कथया यत्क्षणो नीतस्तस्य ऋते तं क्षणं विना आयुर्हरति । स तु क्षणो न हृतः सफल इत्यर्थः ॥ १७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With three verses he says, in effect: make a lifespan that otherwise diminishes in vain fruitful precisely through Hari’s narration. As the sun rises and sets, it takes away life, except for the moment spent in narration concerning Bhagavān Uttamaśloka. That moment is not taken away; it has become fruitful.

ŚB 2.3.18

तरवः किं न जीवन्ति भस्त्राः किं न श्वसन्त्युत ।
न खादन्ति न मेहन्ति किं ग्रामपशवोऽपरे ॥ १८ ॥
taravaḥ kiṁ na jīvanti bhastrāḥ kiṁ na śvasanty uta
na khādanti na mehanti kiṁ grāma-paśavo ’pare
Word-for-word
taravaḥ — trees; kim na — do they not; jīvanti — live; bhastrāḥ — bellows, leather bags; kim na — do they not; śvasanti — breathe; uta — moreover; na khādanti — do they not eat; na mehanti — do they not discharge urine and semen; kim — do not; grāma-paśavaḥ — domestic animals; apare — others.
Translation
Do not trees also live? Do not bellows breathe? And do not the other domestic animals eat, discharge waste and mate?
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
ननु जीवनमेव तदायुषः फलमस्तु । तत्राह—तरव इति । उत्पत्तिनाशमध्यस्थितिर्जीवनम् । ननु तेषां श्वासो नास्ति ? तत्राह—भस्त्रा इति । चर्मकोशा न श्वसन्ति किं ? ननु तासामाहारादिकं नास्ति ? तत्राह—ग्राम्याः पशवोऽपरे आयुर्व्ययं कुर्वतश्च न खादन्ति नादन्ति न मेहन्ति न रेतः सिञ्चन्ति मैथुनं न कुर्वन्ति किमित्यर्थः ॥ १८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Suppose mere living were itself the fruit of such a lifespan. He answers with “the trees”: life is simply the state between arising and destruction. But do they not breathe? He answers with “bellows,” leather bags that also breathe in and out. But bellows do not eat and so forth. He therefore points to ordinary animals: do those that spend their lives merely eating, excreting and mating not do the same? Mere biological living is therefore not the intended human fulfillment.

ŚB 2.3.19

श्वविड्वराहोष्ट्रखरैः संस्तुतः पुरुषः पशुः ।
न यत्कर्णपथोपेतो जातु नाम गदाग्रजः ॥ १९ ॥
śva-viḍ-varāhoṣṭra-kharaiḥ saṁstutaḥ puruṣaḥ paśuḥ
na yat-karṇa-pathopeto jātu nāma gadāgrajaḥ
Word-for-word
śva — dog; viṭ-varāha — village hog that eats refuse; uṣṭra — camel; kharaiḥ — and asses; saṁstutaḥ — praised, counted as comparable with; puruṣaḥ — a human being; paśuḥ — an animal; na — not; yat-karṇa-patha-upetaḥ — entered the path of whose ears; jātu — ever, at any time; nāma — the name; gadāgrajaḥ — of Gadāgraja, Bhagavān.
Translation
A human being into whose ears the name of Gadāgraja has never entered is an animal, counted as comparable with dogs, refuse-eating hogs, camels and asses.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तमपि नराकारं पशुं मत्वाऽऽह—श्वेति । विड्वराहो ग्रामसूकरः । श्वादिभिः सदृशत्वेन संस्तुतो निरूपितः सः क इत्यत आह—नेति । जातु कदाचिदपि गदाभृतो भगवतो नाम न यत्कर्णपथोपेतं, न यस्य कर्णपथमुपेतं, यस्य कर्णमार्गं न प्राप्नोति स इत्यर्थः ॥ १९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Taking even such a human-shaped person to be an animal, he says “dog and the rest.” A viḍ-varāha is a village hog. The person described as comparable with dogs and the others is identified by the next words: one into whose ear-path the name of Bhagavān, the bearer of the mace, has never at any time entered.

ŚB 2.3.20

बिले बतोरुक्रमविक्रमान् ये
न शृण्वतः कर्णपुटे नरस्य ।
जिह्वासती दार्दुरिकेव सूत
न चोपगायत्युरुगायगाथाः ॥ २० ॥
bile batorukrama-vikramān ye
na śṛṇvataḥ karṇa-puṭe narasya
jihvāsatī dārdurikeva sūta
na copagāyaty urugāya-gāthāḥ
Word-for-word
bile — mere holes; bata — alas; urukrama-vikramān — the mighty deeds of Urukrama; ye — which; na śṛṇvataḥ — of one who does not hear; karṇa-puṭe — ear-cavities; narasya — of a person; jihvā — tongue; asatī — useless, bad; dārdurikā iva — like the tongue of a frog; sūta — O Sūta; na ca upagāyati — and does not sing; urugāya-gāthāḥ — the songs and narrations of Urugāya.
Translation
Alas, the ear-cavities of a person who does not hear the mighty deeds of Urukrama are mere holes. O Sūta, a tongue that does not sing the narrations of Urugāya is useless, like a frog’s tongue.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तमेव निन्दति—बिले इति । उरुक्रमस्य भगवतो विक्रमान् व्यापारान् न शृण्वतोऽशृण्वतो नरस्य ये कर्णपुटे ते बिले वृथारन्ध्रे । हे सूत ! उरुगायगाथा भगवत्कथा न चोपगायति तस्य जिह्वाऽसती दुष्टा दार्दुरिकीव मण्डूकजिह्वेव ॥ २० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He continues the censure with “mere holes.” The ear-cavities of a person who does not hear the deeds and activities of Bhagavān Urukrama are useless holes. O Sūta, the tongue of one who does not sing the narrations of Bhagavān Urugāya is useless and bad, like the tongue of a frog.

ŚB 2.3.21

भारः परं पट्टकिरीटजुष्ट-
मप्युत्तमाङ्गं न नमेन्मुकुन्दम् ।
शावौ करौ नो कुरुतः सपर्यां
हरेर्लसत्काञ्चनकङ्कणौ वा ॥ २१ ॥
bhāraḥ paraṁ paṭṭa-kirīṭa-juṣṭam
apy uttamāṅgaṁ na namen mukundam
śāvau karau no kurutaḥ saparyāṁ
harer lasat-kāñcana-kaṅkaṇau vā
Word-for-word
bhāraḥ — a burden; param — only; paṭṭa-kirīṭa-juṣṭam — adorned with ceremonial cloth and crown; api — even; uttama-aṅgam — the head; na namet — if it does not bow; mukundam — to Mukunda; śāvau — corpse-like; karau — two hands; no kurutaḥ — if they do not perform; saparyām — worship, service; hareḥ — of Hari; lasat-kāñcana-kaṅkaṇau — though adorned with shining golden bracelets; vā — even.
Translation
A head, even adorned with ceremonial cloth and a crown, is merely a burden if it does not bow to Mukunda. Two hands, even bearing shining golden bracelets, are corpse-like if they do not perform service to Hari.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भार इति । पट्टवस्त्रोष्णीषेण पट्टेन अभिषेककाले शिरसि निक्षिप्तेन राज्याधिकारसूचकेन वस्त्रेण किरीटेन च जुष्टमपि शिरो यदि न नमेत् तर्हि केवलं भार एव । शवो मृतकस्तत्करतुल्यौ । लसती काञ्चनकङ्कणे ययोस्तौ । अप्यर्थे वाशब्दः ॥ २१ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Even a head furnished with the ceremonial cloth placed upon it at consecration to indicate royal authority, and with a crown, is merely a burden if it does not bow. “Śava” means a dead body; the hands are comparable with the hands of a corpse. They may even bear shining golden bracelets. The word “vā” has the sense of “even.”

ŚB 2.3.22

बर्हायिते ते नयने नराणां
लिङ्गानि विष्णोर्न निरीक्षतो ये ।
पादौ नृणां तौ द्रुमजन्मभाजौ
क्षेत्राणि नानुव्रजतो हरेर्यौ ॥ २२ ॥
barhāyite te nayane narāṇāṁ
liṅgāni viṣṇor na nirīkṣato ye
pādau nṛṇāṁ tau druma-janma-bhājau
kṣetrāṇi nānuvrajato harer yau
Word-for-word
barhāyite — like the eye-patterns on peacock feathers; te — those; nayane — two eyes; narāṇām — of people; liṅgāni — forms, images, marks; viṣṇoḥ — of Viṣṇu; na nirīkṣataḥ — do not behold; ye — which; pādau — two feet; nṛṇām — of people; tau — those; druma-janma-bhājau — sharing the birth of trees, like tree-roots; kṣetrāṇi — sacred places; na anuvrajataḥ — do not go after, visit; hareḥ — of Hari; yau — which.
Translation
Eyes that do not behold the forms of Viṣṇu are like the eye-patterns on peacock feathers. Human feet that do not visit Hari’s sacred places are like the roots of trees.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
बर्हायित इति । ये नयने विष्णोर्मूर्तीर्न निरीक्षेते ते बर्हायिते मयूरपिच्छनेत्रतुल्ये; शिखिपिञ्छवर्तिनेत्रस्य नेत्राकारसद्भावेऽपि यथा दर्शनायोगादनर्थक्यमिति भावः । द्रुमवज्जन्म भजत इति तथा वृक्षमूलतुल्यावित्यर्थः ॥ २२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Eyes that do not behold the forms of Viṣṇu are like the eye-marks on a peacock feather: though an eye-shape is present there, it is useless because it cannot see. Feet that fail to visit Hari’s sacred places are similarly like the roots of trees.

ŚB 2.3.23

जीवञ्छवो भागवताङ्घ्रिरेणुं
न जातु मर्त्योऽभिलभेत यस्तु ।
श्रीविष्णुपद्या मनुजस्तुलस्याः
श्वसञ्छवो यस्तु न वेद गन्धम् ॥ २३ ॥
jīvañ chavo bhāgavatāṅghri-reṇuṁ
na jātu martyo ’bhilabheta yas tu
śrī-viṣṇu-padyā manujas tulasyāḥ
śvasañ chavo yas tu na veda gandham
Word-for-word
jīvan — living; śavaḥ — corpse; bhāgavata-aṅghri-reṇum — dust from the feet of a devotee of Bhagavān; na jātu — never; martyaḥ — mortal human; abhilabheta — obtains, touches, bears; yaḥ tu — who; śrī-viṣṇu-padyāḥ — of that which is attached to Śrī Viṣṇu’s feet; manujaḥ — human being; tulasyāḥ — of Tulasī; śvasan — breathing; śavaḥ — corpse; yaḥ tu — who; na veda — does not know, appreciate; gandham — fragrance.
Translation
A mortal who never receives or bears the dust of a devotee’s feet is a living corpse. A person who, though breathing, does not appreciate the fragrance of Tulasī attached to Śrī Viṣṇu’s feet is likewise a corpse.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
जीवन्निति । नाभिलभेत अभितो न स्पृशेत् न धारयेत् । श्रीविष्णुपद्याः श्रीविष्णुपदलग्नायास्तुलस्याः गन्धं न वेद, अभिघ्राय नाभिनन्दतीत्यर्थः ॥ २३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Would not obtain” means would neither touch it fully nor bear it. Of Tulasī attached to Śrī Viṣṇu’s feet, “does not know the fragrance” means that even after smelling it he does not delight in it.

ŚB 2.3.24

तदश्मसारं हृदयं बतेदं
यद्गृह्यमाणैर्हरिनामधेयैः ।
न विक्रियेताथ यदा विकारो
नेत्रे जलं गात्ररुहेषु हर्षः ॥ २४ ॥
tad aśma-sāraṁ hṛdayaṁ batedaṁ
yad gṛhyamāṇair hari-nāma-dheyaiḥ
na vikriyetātha yadā vikāro
netre jalaṁ gātra-ruheṣu harṣaḥ
Word-for-word
tat — that; aśma-sāram — whose substance is stone, stone-hard; hṛdayam — heart; bata — alas; idam — this; yat — which; gṛhyamāṇaiḥ — when being taken up, remembered or chanted; hari-nāma-dheyaiḥ — by Hari’s names; na vikriyeta — does not undergo transformation; atha — but, then; yadā — when; vikāraḥ — transformation occurs; netre — in the two eyes; jalam — water, tears; gātra-ruheṣu — in the bodily hairs; harṣaḥ — thrill, erection of the hairs.
Translation
Alas, that heart is stone-hard which does not transform when Hari’s names are taken up. When such transformation truly occurs, tears appear in the eyes and the bodily hairs rise in delight.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदिति । अश्मवत् सारो बलं काठिन्यं यस्य । विक्रियालक्षणमाह—अथेति । गात्ररुहेषु रोमसु हर्ष उद्गमः । यस्मादभक्तस्य सर्वमेतद्व्यर्थम् ॥ २४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Its substance or strength is like stone—hardness. He states the signs of transformation with “then”: water in the eyes and the rising of the bodily hairs. Without devotion all these bodily faculties are useless.

ŚB 2.3.25

अथाभिधेह्यङ्ग मनोऽनुकूलं
प्रभाषसे भागवतप्रधानः ।
यदाह वैयासकिरात्मविद्या-
विशारदो नृपतिं साधु पृष्टः ॥ २५ ॥
athābhidhehy aṅga mano-’nukūlaṁ
prabhāṣase bhāgavata-pradhānaḥ
yad āha vaiyāsakir ātma-vidyā-
viśārado nṛpatiṁ sādhu pṛṣṭaḥ
Word-for-word
atha — therefore, now; abhidhehi — please state; aṅga — O dear one; manaḥ-anukūlam — pleasing, favorable to the mind; prabhāṣase — you speak; bhāgavata-pradhānaḥ — foremost among devotees; yat — what; āha — said; vaiyāsakiḥ — Vyāsa’s son, Śuka; ātma-vidyā-viśāradaḥ — expert in knowledge of the Self; nṛpatim — to the King; sādhu pṛṣṭaḥ — properly questioned.
Translation
Therefore, dear Sūta, please tell us what Vyāsa’s son, expert in knowledge of the Self, said to the King when properly questioned. You are foremost among devotees, and what you speak is pleasing to the mind.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
अथेति । मनसोऽनुकूलं प्रियं च प्रभाषसे । अथ अतः कारणान्नृपतिना साधु पृष्टः सन् वैयासकिर्नृपतिं प्रति यदाह तदभिधेहीति ॥ २५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. You speak what is pleasing and agreeable to the mind. Therefore, for that reason, tell us what Vyāsa’s son said to the King when he had been properly questioned by the King.

2.4 The Process of Creation

ŚB 2.4.1

सूत उवाच ।
वैयासकेरिति वचस्तत्त्वनिश्चयमात्मनः ।
उपधार्य मतिं कृष्णे औत्तरेयः सतीं व्यधात् ॥ १ ॥
sūta uvāca
vaiyāsaker iti vacas tattva-niścayam ātmanaḥ
upadhārya matiṁ kṛṣṇe auttareyaḥ satīṁ vyadhāt
Word-for-word
sūtaḥ uvāca — Sūta said; vaiyāsakeḥ — of Vyāsa’s son, Śuka; iti — thus; vacaḥ — words; tattva-niścayam — producing certainty about reality; ātmanaḥ — of the Self; upadhārya — having received and considered; matim — mind, understanding; kṛṣṇe — upon Kṛṣṇa; auttareyaḥ — the son of Uttarā, Parīkṣit; satīm — true, excellent; vyadhāt — fixed, established.
Translation
Sūta said: Having received the words of Vyāsa’s son, which established certainty concerning the truth of the Self, Parīkṣit, the son of Uttarā, fixed his excellent understanding upon Kṛṣṇa.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तुर्ये परीक्षिता पृष्टं सृष्ट्यादि हरिचेष्टितम् । शुकेन ब्रह्मतत्पुत्रसंवादेनोपवर्ण्यते ॥

राज्ञः प्रश्नं कथयितुं तस्य प्राक्तनीं स्थितिमाह—वैयासकेरिति चतुर्भिः । वैयासकेः शुकस्य इत्येवंभूतमात्मनस्तत्त्वस्य निश्चयो यस्मात्तद्वच उपधार्य आकलय्य सतीं श्रेष्ठां मतिं कृष्ण एव सेव्यो नान्य इत्येवंभूताम् ॥ १ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. In the fourth chapter, the acts of Hari beginning with creation, about which Parīkṣit asks, are described by Śuka through the dialogue between Brahmā and his son. To introduce the King’s question, he states in four verses the King’s preceding condition. Having considered the words of Vyāsa’s son Śuka, from which certainty concerning the truth of the Self arises, he formed the excellent understanding: Kṛṣṇa alone is to be served, not another.

ŚB 2.4.2

आत्मजायासुतागारपशुद्रविणबन्धुषु ।
राज्ये चाविकले नित्यं विरूढां ममतां जहौ ॥ २ ॥
ātma-jāyā-sutāgāra-paśu-draviṇa-bandhuṣu
rājye cāvikale nityaṁ virūḍhāṁ mamatāṁ jahau
Word-for-word
ātma — body; jāyā — wife; suta — son; agāra — house; paśu — animals; draviṇa — wealth; bandhuṣu — among relatives; rājye — in the kingdom; ca — and; avikale — unimpaired; nityam — long established; virūḍhām — deeply rooted; mamatām — sense of “mine,” possessiveness; jahau — abandoned.
Translation
He abandoned the deeply rooted sense of possession toward his body, wife, sons, house, animals, wealth, relatives, and even his unimpaired kingdom.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
आत्मेति । आत्मा देहः । पशवो गजादयः । अविकले वैरिभिरनुपद्रुते राज्ये च ॥ २ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Ātman” here means the body. “Animals” means elephants and the like. “Unimpaired” means the kingdom was not troubled by enemies.

ŚB 2.4.3

पप्रच्छ चेममेवार्थं यन्मां पृच्छथ सत्तमाः ।
कृष्णानुभावश्रवणे श्रद्दधानो महामनाः ॥ ३ ॥
papraccha cemam evārthaṁ yan māṁ pṛcchatha sattamāḥ
kṛṣṇānubhāva-śravaṇe śraddadhāno mahā-manāḥ
Word-for-word
papraccha — asked; ca — and; imam eva — this very; artham — subject; yat — which; mām — me; pṛcchatha — you ask; sattamāḥ — O best of the good; kṛṣṇa-anubhāva-śravaṇe — in hearing Kṛṣṇa’s power and glory; śraddadhānaḥ — having faith; mahā-manāḥ — great-minded.
Translation
O best of the good, the great-minded King, full of faith in hearing Kṛṣṇa’s glory, asked this very subject which you are now asking me.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
पप्रच्छेति । इममेवार्थं हरिलीलालक्षणम् ॥ ३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “This very subject” means the subject characterized by the līlās of Hari.

ŚB 2.4.4

संस्थां विज्ञाय सन्न्यस्य कर्म त्रैवर्गिकं च यत् ।
वासुदेवे भगवति आत्मभावं दृढं गतः ॥ ४ ॥
saṁsthāṁ vijñāya sannyasya karma traivargikaṁ ca yat
vāsudeve bhagavati ātma-bhāvaṁ dṛḍhaṁ gataḥ
Word-for-word
saṁsthām — the end, death; vijñāya — having known; sannyasya — renouncing; karma — action; traivargikam — belonging to the three aims of dharma, artha and kāma; ca yat — whatever; vāsudeve bhagavati — in Bhagavān Vāsudeva; ātma-bhāvam — supreme love, identification of Him as one’s very Self; dṛḍham — firm; gataḥ — having attained.
Translation
Knowing that death was at hand, he renounced every action directed toward dharma, artha and kāma, and became firmly established in supreme love for Bhagavān Vāsudeva as his very Self.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
संस्थामिति । संस्थां मृत्युं विज्ञाय । त्रैवर्गिकं धर्मार्थकामप्रधानम् । सन्न्यस्य त्यक्त्वा आत्मभावं परमप्रेम्णा भगवदात्मत्वं गतः प्राप्तः सन् पप्रच्छ ॥ ४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Saṁsthā” means death. Having known it, he renounced what belongs chiefly to the three aims—dharma, artha and kāma. Having attained ātma-bhāva, supreme love by which Bhagavān is taken as one’s very Self, he asked his question.

ŚB 2.4.5

राजोवाच ।
समीचीनं वचो ब्रह्मन् सर्वज्ञस्य तवानघ ।
तमो विशीर्यते मह्यं हरेः कथयतः कथाम् ॥ ५ ॥
rājovāca
samīcīnaṁ vaco brahman sarva-jñasya tavānagha
tamo viśīryate mahyaṁ hareḥ kathayataḥ kathām
Word-for-word
rājā uvāca — the King said; samīcīnam — fitting, excellent; vacaḥ — speech; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; sarva-jñasya — of one who knows all; tava — your; anagha — O sinless one; tamaḥ — darkness, ignorance; viśīryate — is shattered, disappears; mahyam — for me; hareḥ — of Hari; kathayataḥ — as you narrate; kathām — the story.
Translation
The King said: O sinless brāhmaṇa, the words of you who know all are excellent. As you narrate Hari’s story, the darkness of ignorance is shattered for me.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
समीचीनमिति । मह्यं मम तमोऽज्ञानं विशीर्यते नश्यति तव कथयतः सतः; अतः समीचीनम् ॥ ५ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. For me, my darkness—ignorance—is being shattered, destroyed, while you speak; therefore your speech is excellent.

ŚB 2.4.6

भूय एव विवित्सामि भगवानात्ममायया ।
यथेदं सृजते विश्वं दुर्विभाव्यमधीश्वरैः ॥ ६ ॥
bhūya eva vivitsāmi bhagavān ātma-māyayā
yathedaṁ sṛjate viśvaṁ durvibhāvyam adhīśvaraiḥ
Word-for-word
bhūyaḥ eva — yet again, further; vivitsāmi — I desire to know; bhagavān — Bhagavān; ātma-māyayā — by His own māyā; yathā — how; idam — this; sṛjate — creates; viśvam — universe; durvibhāvyam — difficult to conceive or understand; adhīśvaraiḥ — even by great lords such as Brahmā.
Translation
I wish to know further how Bhagavān, through His own māyā, creates this universe, which is difficult even for the great cosmic lords to comprehend.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
भूय इति । पुनश्च विवित्सामि वेदितुमिच्छामि । इदं दुर्विभाव्यमवितर्क्यं विश्वं यथा सृजते ॥ ६ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Further” means again I desire to know. I wish to know how He creates this universe, which is difficult to conceive and beyond ordinary reasoning.

ŚB 2.4.7

यथा गोपायति विभुर्यथा संयच्छते पुनः ।
यां यां शक्तिमुपाश्रित्य पुरुशक्तिः परः पुमान् ।
आत्मानं क्रीडयन् क्रीडन् करोति विकरोति च ॥ ७ ॥
yathā gopāyati vibhur yathā saṁyacchate punaḥ
yāṁ yāṁ śaktim upāśritya puru-śaktiḥ paraḥ pumān
ātmānaṁ krīḍayan krīḍan karoti vikaroti ca
Word-for-word
yathā — how; gopāyati — protects, maintains; vibhuḥ — the all-pervading Lord; saṁyacchate — withdraws, dissolves; punaḥ — again; yām yām śaktim — whichever power; upāśritya — taking as support; puru-śaktiḥ — possessing many powers; paraḥ pumān — the Supreme Person; ātmānam — Himself; krīḍayan — causing to play, manifesting Himself in forms such as Brahmā; krīḍan — playing; karoti — makes; vikaroti — makes variously, transforms; ca — and.
Translation
I wish to know how the all-pervading Lord protects this universe and again withdraws it, and how the Supreme Person of many powers, taking up His various powers, plays and causes Himself to play through different forms, making and diversifying the cosmos.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यथेति । गोपयति पालयति । संयच्छते संहरते । पुरुशक्तिर्बहुशक्तिमान् । क्रीडन् यथा करोति आत्मानं ब्रह्मादिरूपिणं क्रीडयन् विकरोति विविधं करोति ॥ ७ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Gopāyati” means He protects; “saṁyacchate” means He withdraws. “Puru-śakti” means possessing many powers. Playing, He acts; causing Himself in forms such as Brahmā to play, He makes things various and manifold.

ŚB 2.4.8

नूनं भगवतो ब्रह्मन् हरेरद्भुतकर्मणः ।
दुर्विभाव्यमिवाभाति कविभिश्चापि चेष्टितम् ॥ ८ ॥
nūnaṁ bhagavato brahman harer adbhuta-karmaṇaḥ
durvibhāvyam ivābhāti kavibhiś cāpi ceṣṭitam
Word-for-word
nūnam — certainly; bhagavataḥ — of Bhagavān; brahman — O brāhmaṇa; hareḥ — of Hari; adbhuta-karmaṇaḥ — whose acts are wondrous; durvibhāvyam — difficult to comprehend; iva — as if; ābhāti — appears; kavibhiḥ — even by sages, learned seers; ca api — even; ceṣṭitam — activity.
Translation
O brāhmaṇa, the activity of Bhagavān Hari, whose acts are wondrous, certainly appears difficult to comprehend even for learned seers.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नूनमिति । भगवतो हरेरद्भुतकर्मणश्चेष्टितं कविभिरपि दुर्विभाव्यमिवाभाति ॥ ८ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The activity of Bhagavān Hari, whose acts are wondrous, appears difficult to comprehend even by sages and learned seers.

ŚB 2.4.9

यथा गुणांस्तु प्रकृतेर्युगपत्क्रमशोऽपि वा ।
बिभर्ति भूरिशस्त्वेकः कुर्वन् कर्माणि जन्मभिः ॥ ९ ॥
yathā guṇāṁs tu prakṛter yugapat kramaśo ’pi vā
bibharti bhūriśas tv ekaḥ kurvan karmāṇi janmabhiḥ
Word-for-word
yathā — how; guṇān — the guṇas; tu — but; prakṛteḥ — of prakṛti; yugapat — simultaneously; kramaśaḥ — successively; api vā — or; bibharti — assumes, bears; bhūriśaḥ — in many ways; tu — yet; ekaḥ — the one Lord; kurvan — performing; karmāṇi — acts; janmabhiḥ — through births, avatāras.
Translation
How does the one Lord, while performing many acts through His avatāras, bear the guṇas of prakṛti—simultaneously, successively, or otherwise?
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
यथेति । एकः पुरुषरूपेण युगपज्जन्मभिर्ब्रह्माद्यवतारैः क्रमशो वा यथा प्रकृतेर्गुणान् गृह्णाति ॥ ९ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. The question is how the one Lord, in the form of the Puruṣa simultaneously or through successive births and avatāras such as Brahmā, takes up the guṇas of prakṛti.

ŚB 2.4.10

विचिकित्सितमेतन्मे ब्रवीतु भगवान् यथा ।
शाब्दे ब्रह्मणि निष्णातः परस्मिंश्च भवान् खलु ॥ १० ॥
vicikitsitam etan me bravītu bhagavān yathā
śābde brahmaṇi niṣṇātaḥ parasmiṁś ca bhavān khalu
Word-for-word
vicikitsitam — what is doubtful, the matter requiring examination; etat — this; me — for me; bravītu — may he please explain; bhagavān — your venerable self; yathā — correctly, as it is; śābde brahmaṇi — in Brahman in the form of revealed sound, the Veda; niṣṇātaḥ — thoroughly immersed, fully versed; parasmin — in the Supreme Brahman; ca — and; bhavān — you; khalu — indeed.
Translation
May your venerable self explain this matter of doubt to me exactly as it is, for you are indeed thoroughly versed both in the verbal Brahman, the Veda, and in the Supreme Brahman.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विचिकित्सितमिति । विचिकित्सितं सन्दिग्धम् । शाब्दे ब्रह्मणि विचारेण निष्णातो नितरां स्नातः, परस्मिन्ननुभवेन ॥ १० ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Vicikitsitam” means doubtful, a matter of uncertainty. You are thoroughly immersed in the verbal Brahman through inquiry, and in the Supreme Brahman through direct experience.

ŚB 2.4.11

सूत उवाच ।
इत्युपामन्त्रितो राज्ञा गुणानुकथने हरेः ।
हृषीकेशमनुस्मृत्य प्रतिवक्तुं प्रचक्रमे ॥ ११ ॥
sūta uvāca
ity upāmantrito rājñā guṇānukathane hareḥ
hṛṣīkeśam anusmṛtya prativaktuṁ pracakrame
Word-for-word
sūtaḥ uvāca — Sūta said; iti — thus; upāmantritaḥ — requested, invited to speak; rājñā — by the King; guṇa-anukathane — to recount the qualities; hareḥ — of Hari; hṛṣīkeśam — Hṛṣīkeśa, the Lord of the senses; anusmṛtya — remembering; prativaktum — to answer; pracakrame — began, undertook.
Translation
Sūta said: Thus requested by the King to recount Hari’s qualities, Śuka remembered Hṛṣīkeśa and began to answer.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
इतीति । राज्ञा उपामन्त्रितः प्रार्थितः । प्रचक्रमे देवतागुरुनमस्कारादिरूपमुपक्रमं कृतवानित्यर्थः ॥ ११ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. “Thus” means that he had been requested and entreated by the King. “He began” means that he made the customary beginning consisting of salutations to the deity and guru and the like.

ŚB 2.4.12

श्रीशुक उवाच ।
नमः परस्मै पुरुषाय भूयसे
सदुद्भवस्थाननिरोधलीलया ।
गृहीतशक्तित्रितयाय देहिना-
मन्तर्भवायानुपलक्ष्यवर्त्मने ॥ १२ ॥
śrī-śuka uvāca
namaḥ parasmai puruṣāya bhūyase
sad-udbhava-sthāna-nirodha-līlayā
gṛhīta-śakti-tritayāya dehinām
antar-bhavāyānupalakṣya-vartmane
Word-for-word
namaḥ — obeisance; parasmai puruṣāya — to the Supreme Puruṣa; bhūyase — to the immeasurably great, all-pervading One; sat — of the manifested universe; udbhava — creation; sthāna — maintenance; nirodha — dissolution; līlayā — by the play; gṛhīta — assumed; śakti-tritayāya — three powers; dehinām — of embodied beings; antar-bhavāya — to the indwelling Self; anupalakṣya-vartmane — whose way or nature is not perceptible.
Translation
Obeisance to the Supreme Puruṣa, the immeasurably great One, who through the play of creating, maintaining and dissolving the manifested universe assumes the three powers, who dwells within embodied beings, and whose way is beyond perception.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
तदेवाह त्रयोदशभिः—नम इति । परस्मै सर्वोत्तमाय । तत्र हेतुर्भूयसे अपरिमितमहिम्ने । तद्दर्शयति—सतः प्रपञ्चस्य उद्भवादिषु निमित्तभूता या लीला तया गृहीतं ब्रह्मादिरूपेण रजआदिशक्तित्रितयं येन तस्मै । अन्तर्भवाय अन्तर्यामिणे । अत एव सर्वान्तर्गतत्वादनुपलक्ष्यं वर्त्म यस्य तस्मै ॥ १२ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. With thirteen verses beginning here he offers salutations. “Supreme” means highest of all, and “bhūyas” indicates immeasurable greatness. By the play that is the instrumental cause of the universe’s creation, maintenance and dissolution, He assumes the three powers beginning with rajas in the forms of Brahmā and the others. He is the indwelling controller; because He is within all beings, His way is not perceptible.

ŚB 2.4.13

भूयो नमः सद्वृजिनच्छिदेऽसता-
मसम्भवायाखिलसत्त्वमूर्तये ।
पुंसां पुनः पारमहंस्य आश्रमे
व्यवस्थितानामनुमृग्यदाशुषे ॥ १३ ॥
bhūyo namaḥ sad-vṛjina-cchide ’satām
asambhavāyākhila-sattva-mūrtaye
puṁsāṁ punaḥ pāramahaṁsya āśrame
vyavasthitānām anumṛgya-dāśuṣe
Word-for-word
bhūyaḥ — again; namaḥ — obeisance; sat-vṛjina-cchide — to the destroyer of the suffering or sin of the good; asatām — of the unrighteous; asambhavāya — to the cause of non-arising, destruction; akhila-sattva-mūrtaye — to Him whose forms comprise all divine powers, or whose form is complete sattva; puṁsām — of persons; punaḥ — again; pāramahaṁsye āśrame — established in the state belonging to paramahaṁsas; vyavasthitānām — of those firmly situated; anumṛgya — the reality repeatedly sought through negation of the non-Self; dāśuṣe — to the giver.
Translation
Again I bow to Him who destroys the suffering of the good and prevents the rise of the unrighteous, whose form is complete sattva, and who grants the reality repeatedly sought by those firmly established in the state of the paramahaṁsas.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
विचित्रफलदातृत्वमनुस्मरन् प्रणमति—भूय इति । भूयः पुनश्च नमः । सतां धर्मवर्तिनां वृजिनच्छिदे दुःखहन्त्रे । असतामधर्मशीलानामसम्भवायानुद्भवहेतवे । अखिलसत्त्वमूर्तये तत्तद्देवतादिरूपेण तत्तत्फलदात्रे इत्यर्थः; समग्रसत्त्वमूर्तये इति वा । पुनरिति पूर्वोक्तोभयवैलक्षण्यमाह । पारमहंस्ये प्रत्यनिष्ठारूपे आश्रमे व्यवस्थितानां पुंसामनुमृग्यमतन्निरसनेन पुनः पुनर्मृग्यं यदात्मतत्त्वं तस्य दाशुषे दात्रे ॥ १३ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. Remembering His power to bestow varied results, he bows again. He destroys the suffering of the good who walk in dharma, and is the cause of the non-arising of the unrighteous. “Whose form is all sattva” may mean that, taking the forms of the respective deities, He gives their respective fruits, or that His form is complete sattva. For persons established in the paramahaṁsa state, He gives the truth of the Self that is sought again and again by rejecting what is not That.

ŚB 2.4.14

नमो नमस्तेऽस्त्वृषभाय सात्वतां
विदूरकाष्ठाय मुहुः कुयोगिनाम् ।
निरस्तसाम्यातिशयेन राधसा
स्वधामनि ब्रह्मणि रंस्यते नमः ॥ १४ ॥
namo namas te ’stv ṛṣabhāya sātvatāṁ
vidūra-kāṣṭhāya muhuḥ ku-yoginām
nirasta-sāmyātiśayena rādhasā
sva-dhāmani brahmaṇi raṁsyate namaḥ
Word-for-word
namaḥ namaḥ — repeated obeisance; te — to You; astu — may there be; ṛṣabhāya — to the protector, chief; sātvatām — of devotees; vidūra-kāṣṭhāya — whose limit or direction is exceedingly remote; muhuḥ — repeatedly; ku-yoginām — for false yogins, those without devotion; nirasta — excluded; sāmya — equality; atiśayena — superiority; rādhasā — by majesty, power; sva-dhāmani — in His own nature; brahmaṇi — Brahman; raṁsyate — delighting.
Translation
Repeated obeisance to You, the protector of the Sātvatas, whose limit is immeasurably distant to false yogins, who by majesty admit neither equal nor superior, and who delight in Your own abode, Brahman.
Śrīdhara Sanskrit
नम इति । सात्वतां भक्तानामृषभाय पालकाय । कुयोगिनां भक्तिहीनानां विदूरा काष्ठा दिगपि यस्य दुर्ज्ञेयायेत्यर्थः । तदेवं वैषम्यप्रतीतावपि निर्दोषत्वादचिन्त्यैश्वर्यमाह । निरस्तं साम्यमतिशयश्च यस्य, यदपेक्षयान्यस्य साम्यमतिशयश्च नास्ति तेन राधसा ऐश्वर्येण स्वधामनि स्वस्वरूपे ब्रह्मणि रममाणाय ॥ १४ ॥

Śrīdhara’s Commentary. He is the chief and protector of the Sātvatas, the devotees. For false yogins who lack devotion, even the direction of His farthest limit is unknowable. His inconceivable sovereignty is faultless: by that majesty He has neither equal nor superior, and He delights in His own abode, His own nature, Brahman.

ŚB 2.4.15

यत्कीर्तनं यत्स्मरणं यदीक्षणं
यद्वन्दनं यच्छ्रवणं यदर्हणम् ।
लोकस्य सद्यो विधुनोति कल्मषं
तस्मै सुभद्रश्रवसे नमो नमः ॥ १५ ॥
yat-kīrtanaṁ yat-smaraṇaṁ yad-īkṣaṇaṁ
yad-vandanaṁ yac-chravaṇaṁ yad-arhaṇam
lokasya sadyo vidhunoti kalmaṣaṁ
tasmai subhadra-śravase namo namaḥ
Word-for-word
yat-kīrtanam — whose glorification; yat-smaraṇam — whose remembrance; yat-īkṣaṇam — whose beholding; yat-vandanam — whose salutation; yat-śravaṇam — whose hearing; yat-arhaṇam — whose worship; lokasya — of a person, of the world; sadyaḥ — immediately; vidhunoti — shakes off, destroys; kalmaṣam — sin, impurity; tasmai — to Him; subhadra-śravase — whose fame is supremely auspicious; namaḥ namaḥ — repeated obeisance.
Translation
